===== TICKET =====
KS-1717 — anchoring has no inbound strip for `x-emitter-internal`: a direct compose-network caller can suppress its own provenance row, and the comment over-claims [In Review]

**Ask:** give the anchoring service a guard against a caller setting `x-emitter-internal` itself, and correct the comment above it in the same change.

**What the gate found (note N1 of the #995 verdict):** the marker means "this is K's own originate-to-anchoring hop for an operation that already wrote its own provenance row", and anchoring skips `recordFlatAnchorProvenance` when it is present (`Blockchain/Dev/services/anchoring/src/index.ts`, `const isInternalEmit = typeof req.headers['x-emitter-internal'] === 'string'`, line 531 on develop `a9ce2cff1594`). #995 (merged, KS-741) strips the marker at originate's own inbound. **Anchoring has no inbound strip of its own.** The gate, verbatim: *"Anchoring has no inbound strip; its middleware is helmet, cors, json, rejectNulBytes, requestLogger, then jwtAuthenticate.* `docker-compose.yml` *treats anchoring exactly as it treats originate: both are on the flat network with host ports unpublished (*`:1286` *vs* `:557`*). That is the same exposure the PR uses to justify the originate strip. So a direct compose-network caller holding a connector JWT can set the marker and suppress its own flat-anchor provenance row. This is pre-existing and not introduced by this PR."*

**The comment over-claims.** Lines 519-530 (the KS-566 block) say the header "is not spoofable, by two strips" and that *"the only value that can arrive here is one originate itself set on its outbound hop"*. That holds only for paths through the gateway or originate. #995 left that wording in place (it edited the comment to name the originate strip). The gate: *"Reword the comment, or open a ticket for an anchoring-side guard."* This is that ticket; fix the comment in the same change as the guard.

**Design caution, my reading and not the gate's:** a plain strip at anchoring's inbound would also delete the legitimate marker originate sets on its own hop, so the guard has to tell the two apart (honour the marker only on a request that carries something only originate can present), not just drop the header. Whoever picks this up should settle that first.

**Also from the same gate (note N3, minor):** #995's cell C6 checks the strip's mount order only against `/api/anchors`. A move to between `/api/documents` (`originate/src/index.ts` lines 275-276, which also builds a marked hop) and `/api/anchors` would still pass C6. Today the order is correct. Worth one more cell when the order is next touched.

**Related, read and not a duplicate:** KS-1603 (In Progress, anchoring `POST /api/anchors` never checks who owns `documentId`) records in one bullet that `x-emitter-internal` "is trusted by mere presence" and that "any peer on the compose network can send it". That is the same exposure seen from the ownership ticket; it proposes no fix for the marker and does not touch the over-claiming comment. This ticket owns the marker guard and the comment. (KS-1603's bullet says originate sets the marker at one call site; the gate found two setters, `originate/src/routes/documents.ts` lines 878 and 1375.)

**Impact, as the gate framed it:** a caller on the compose network with a connector JWT can suppress its own flat-anchor provenance row; it needs network position and a valid credential.

Source: QA gate DA5, tier 1, round 1 (verdict `2026-10-23_gate-DA5-pr989-995-tier1/verdict.md`, sha256/16 of the body `be2f26d014ae8997`). Quoted from the verdict; **not re-measured by the seat that filed this.**

+### `EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET` — the signed originate -> anchoring hop (KS-1717)
+
+`x-emitter-internal` tells anchoring that a `POST /api/anchors` is originate's own
+hop for an operation that already wrote its action_provenance row, so anchoring
+skips its flat-anchor `anchor` row (KS-566). originate's two setters forward the
+CALLER'S OWN bearer, so the hop and a direct compose-network caller holding the
+same connector JWT are indistinguishable by credential. anchoring therefore honours
+the marker only when an HMAC-SHA256 originate made under this key verifies — over
+the method, the path, the marker, a timestamp within **±60 s**, the **caller's
+Authorization header** (hashed exactly as sent), and the **raw body bytes**
+(`packages/shared/src/security/emitterSignature.ts`, used by both sides).
+
+**What it does not do:** anyone holding the key can sign, and there is no nonce
+store. A byte-identical replay under the **same bearer** verifies for just under 61 s
+after signing when the two clocks agree, and for up to about 121 s if the signer's
+clock runs up to 60 s fast. A replay with a different body, method, path, marker,
+timestamp or bearer is refused.
+
+**Where it may live:** `.env`, which compose interpolates into the `environment:`
+blocks of **originate and anchoring only** — never `.env.local` and never
+`commonEnvVars` in `services.bicep`, for the reasons given for the vouch above. The
+same value on both services.
+
+**Unset FAILS CLOSED**, the same shape as Kam's 2026-10-13 ruling for the vouch:
+anchoring honours no marker and logs `[emitter-guard] FAIL-CLOSED …` at boot
+(`[emitter-guard] ACTIVE …` when configured), and originate's own hops each write a
+**second** action_provenance row until the secret is set on BOTH services. **Set it
+on both before deploying the images that check it.** A marker anchoring refuses
+while it IS configured is logged with its reason — a forgery, or a half-configured
+deploy.
+
+Generate per environment with `openssl rand -hex 32`.
+
 ---

+# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+# EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET — KS-1717 (the signed originate -> anchoring hop)
+# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
+# HMAC key originate signs its own hops to anchoring with. `x-emitter-internal`
+# tells anchoring to skip the flat-anchor `anchor` provenance row, because the
+# operation already wrote its own. anchoring honours it ONLY when the signature
+# (method, path, marker, a timestamp within 60 s, the caller's Authorization
+# header, and the raw body bytes) verifies under this key. Without that check,
+# a caller reaching anchoring directly on the compose network with a connector
+# JWT could set the header and suppress its own provenance row.
+#
+# WHAT IT DOES NOT DO: anyone holding the key can sign, and there is no nonce
+# store. A byte-identical replay under the SAME bearer verifies for just under
+# 61 s after signing when the two clocks agree, and for up to about 121 s if the
+# signer's clock runs up to 60 s fast. A replay with a different body, method,
+# path, marker, timestamp or bearer is refused.
+#
+# WHERE IT MAY LIVE: here, in `.env`, which compose interpolates into the
+# `environment:` blocks of originate and anchoring ONLY. NOT in `.env.local`
+# (four services load that file). The SAME value on both services, and at least
+# 32 characters — anything shorter is treated as unset.
+#
+# UNSET FAILS CLOSED. anchoring honours no marker and logs a WARNING at boot, so
+# originate's own hops each write a SECOND action_provenance row (an `anchor` row
+# beside the operation's own) until the secret is set on BOTH services. Set it on
+# both before deploying the images that check it.
+# Generate one with:
+#     openssl rand -hex 32
+EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET=
diff --git a/Blockchain/Dev/packages/shared/src/__tests__/ks1717-emitter-hop-signature.test.ts b/Blockchain/Dev/packages/shared/src/__tests__/ks1717-emitter-hop-signature.test.ts
new file mode 100644
index 000000000..798459462
--- /dev/null
+++ b/Blockchain/Dev/packages/shared/src/__tests__/ks1717-emitter-hop-signature.test.ts
@@ -0,0 +1,245 @@
+/**
+ * =============================================================================
+ * KS-1717 — the ONE signing format for originate's own hop to anchoring
+ * =============================================================================
+ * These cells pin the helper both services share (`security/emitterSignature.ts`).
+ * originate's cells prove its two setters SEND what this helper signs; anchoring's
+ * cells prove the real route HONOURS only what it verifies. This file is the contract
+ * between them, so every way a signed hop can be altered after signing is a cell here,
+ * and each one is expected to be REFUSED.
+ *
+ * The canonicalisation question (Wednesday's ANSWER, 2026-10-26: "reordered JSON keys
+ * must be stated as either canonicalised or rejected") is answered as REJECTED: the
+ * signature covers the raw body bytes, so S4 and S5 are refusals by design.
+ */
+import { describe, it, expect } from 'vitest';
+import crypto from 'crypto';
+import {
+  EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER,
+  EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER,
+  EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER,
+  EMITTER_SIGNATURE_WINDOW_SECONDS,
+  EMITTER_SECRET_MIN_LENGTH,
+  isUsableEmitterSecret,
+  emitterSigningString,
+  signEmitterHop,
+  verifyEmitterHop,
+} from '../index';
+
+const SECRET = 'ks1717-test-signing-secret-0123456789abcdef';
+const NOW_MS = 1_800_000_000_000;
+const URL_ = 'http://anchoring:4005/api/anchors';
+const PATH = '/api/anchors';
+const AUTH = 'Bearer connector-a.header.payload.sig';
+const BODY = JSON.stringify({ documentId: 'doc-ks1717', contentHash: 'a'.repeat(64), network: 'preview' });
+
+function signed(over: Partial<{ secret: string; marker: string; method: string; url: string; authorization: string | undefined; body: string; nowMs: number }> = {}) {
+  return signEmitterHop({ secret: SECRET, marker: 'originate-create', method: 'POST', url: URL_, authorization: AUTH, body: BODY, nowMs: NOW_MS, ...over });
+}
+
+/** The receiving side: `headers` is what arrived, so the caller's bearer defaults to AUTH as sent. */
+function verify(headers: Record<string, string | string[] | undefined>, over: Partial<{ secret: string | undefined; method: string; path: string; body: Buffer | string | undefined; nowMs: number }> = {}) {
+  return verifyEmitterHop({ secret: SECRET, method: 'POST', path: PATH, headers: { authorization: AUTH, ...headers }, body: BODY, nowMs: NOW_MS, ...over });
+}
+
+/** A signature over arbitrary fields with an arbitrary key — for the cells that forge one. */
+function macOver(secret: string, fields: { method: string; path: string; timestamp: string; marker: string; authorization: string; body: string }): string {
+  return crypto.createHmac('sha256', secret).update(emitterSigningString(fields), 'utf8').digest('hex');
+}
+
+describe('KS-1717 S1 — a genuine hop verifies', () => {
+  it('what signEmitterHop produces, verifyEmitterHop accepts, and it returns the marker', () => {
+    expect(verify(signed())).toEqual({ ok: true, marker: 'originate-create' });
+  });
+
+  it('the signer emits exactly the three headers, all under x-emitter- (the gateway edge strip covers that prefix)', () => {
+    const h = signed();
+    expect(Object.keys(h).sort()).toEqual([EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER, EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER, EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER].sort());
+    for (const name of Object.keys(h)) expect(name).toMatch(/^x-emitter-/);
+    expect(h[EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER]).toBe(String(NOW_MS / 1000));
+    expect(h[EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER]).toMatch(/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/);
+  });
+
+  it('the body is compared as BYTES: a Buffer of the same UTF-8 verifies, including non-ASCII', () => {
+    const body = JSON.stringify({ documentId: 'doc-ks1717', issuerName: 'Société Générale — 東京' });
+    const h = signed({ body });
+    expect(verify(h, { body: Buffer.from(body, 'utf8') }).ok).toBe(true);
+  });
+});
+
+describe('KS-1717 S2 — the signing string is pinned literally', () => {
+  it('label, method, path, timestamp, marker, sha256(authorization), sha256(body) — newline-joined, in that order', () => {
+    const s = emitterSigningString({ method: 'POST', path: PATH, timestamp: '1800000000', marker: 'originate-create', authorization: AUTH, body: BODY });
+    const authHash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(AUTH).digest('hex');
+    const bodyHash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(BODY).digest('hex');
+    expect(s).toBe(['secuura-emitter-v1', 'POST', '/api/anchors', '1800000000', 'originate-create', authHash, bodyHash].join('\n'));
+  });
+});
+
+describe('KS-1717 S3 — every field altered after signing is refused', () => {
+  const h = signed();
+
+  it('S3a a different body (the replay-with-another-document case)', () => {
+    const other = JSON.stringify({ documentId: 'doc-someone-elses', contentHash: 'b'.repeat(64), network: 'preview' });
+    expect(verify(h, { body: other })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+  });
+
+  it('S3b the SAME object with its keys reordered — REJECTED, not canonicalised', () => {
+    const reordered = JSON.stringify({ network: 'preview', contentHash: 'a'.repeat(64), documentId: 'doc-ks1717' });
+    expect(JSON.parse(reordered)).toEqual(JSON.parse(BODY)); // same value...
+    expect(verify(h, { body: reordered })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' }); // ...different bytes
+  });
+
+  it('S3c the same object with whitespace added — REJECTED', () => {
+    expect(verify(h, { body: JSON.stringify(JSON.parse(BODY), null, 2) })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+  });
+
+  it('S3d another method', () => {
+    expect(verify(h, { method: 'PUT' })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+  });
+
+  it('S3e another path, and the same path with a query string', () => {
+    expect(verify(h, { path: '/api/anchors/batch' })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+    expect(verify(h, { path: '/api/anchors?x=1' })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+  });
+
+  it('S3f another marker value', () => {
+    expect(verify({ ...h, [EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER]: 'originate-lifecycle' })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+  });
+
+  it('S3g the timestamp header moved, even inside the window', () => {
+    expect(verify({ ...h, [EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER]: String(NOW_MS / 1000 + 1) })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+  });
+
+  it('S3h the same timestamp with a leading zero — the header string is signed verbatim', () => {
+    expect(verify({ ...h, [EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER]: `0${h[EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER]}` })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+  });
+
+  it('S3i another secret', () => {
+    expect(verify(h, { secret: `${SECRET}-rotated` })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+  });
+
+  it('S3j raw bytes NOT captured on the receiving side fail, rather than verifying an empty body', () => {
+    expect(verify(h, { body: undefined })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+  });
+});
+
+describe(`KS-1717 S4 — the window is ±${EMITTER_SIGNATURE_WINDOW_SECONDS} s, inclusive`, () => {
+  const h = signed();
+  const W = EMITTER_SIGNATURE_WINDOW_SECONDS * 1000;
+
+  it('accepted at exactly +60 s and -60 s', () => {
+    expect(verify(h, { nowMs: NOW_MS + W }).ok).toBe(true);
+    expect(verify(h, { nowMs: NOW_MS - W }).ok).toBe(true);
+  });
+
+  it('an EXPIRED timestamp (61 s old) is refused', () => {
+    expect(verify(h, { nowMs: NOW_MS + W + 1000 })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'stale-timestamp' });
+  });
+
+  it('a timestamp 61 s in the FUTURE is refused', () => {
+    expect(verify(h, { nowMs: NOW_MS - W - 1000 })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'stale-timestamp' });
+  });
+});
+
+describe('KS-1717 S5 — fail-closed: no usable secret, nothing verifies', () => {
+  it(`a secret under ${EMITTER_SECRET_MIN_LENGTH} chars is "unset", including the template placeholder`, () => {
+    expect(isUsableEmitterSecret('x'.repeat(EMITTER_SECRET_MIN_LENGTH))).toBe(true);
+    expect(isUsableEmitterSecret('x'.repeat(EMITTER_SECRET_MIN_LENGTH - 1))).toBe(false);
+    expect(isUsableEmitterSecret('placeholder-never-referenced')).toBe(false);
+    expect(isUsableEmitterSecret('')).toBe(false);
+    expect(isUsableEmitterSecret(undefined)).toBe(false);
+  });
+
+  it('a hop signed correctly WITH a short secret is still refused by a verifier holding that same secret', () => {
+    const short = 'short-but-shared';
+    const timestamp = String(NOW_MS / 1000);
+    const forged = {
+      [EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER]: 'originate-create',
+      [EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER]: timestamp,
+      [EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER]: macOver(short, { method: 'POST', path: PATH, timestamp, marker: 'originate-create', authorization: AUTH, body: BODY }),
+    };
+    expect(verify(forged, { secret: short })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'no-secret' });
+  });
+
+  it('an empty secret refuses even a signature MAC-ed under the empty key', () => {
+    const timestamp = String(NOW_MS / 1000);
+    const forged = {
+      [EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER]: 'originate-create',
+      [EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER]: timestamp,
+      [EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER]: macOver('', { method: 'POST', path: PATH, timestamp, marker: 'originate-create', authorization: AUTH, body: BODY }),
+    };
+    expect(verify(forged, { secret: '' })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'no-secret' });
+    expect(verify(forged, { secret: undefined })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'no-secret' });
+  });
+
+  it('the signer with no usable secret sends the marker ALONE — never a timestamp or signature', () => {
+    expect(signed({ secret: '' })).toEqual({ [EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER]: 'originate-create' });
+    expect(signEmitterHop({ secret: undefined, marker: 'originate-lifecycle', method: 'POST', url: URL_, authorization: AUTH, body: BODY })).toEqual({ [EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER]: 'originate-lifecycle' });
+  });
+});
+
+describe('KS-1717 S6 — malformed or missing headers are refused, each with its own reason', () => {
+  const h = signed();
+
+  it('no marker, an empty marker, or a repeated (array) marker', () => {
+    const { [EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER]: _m, ...rest } = h;
+    expect(verify(rest)).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'no-marker' });
+    expect(verify({ ...h, [EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER]: '' })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'no-marker' });
+    expect(verify({ ...h, [EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER]: ['originate-create', 'originate-create'] })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'no-marker' });
+  });
+
+  it('the bare marker — the pre-KS-1717 shape — is refused', () => {
+    expect(verify({ [EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER]: 'originate-create' })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'missing-timestamp' });
+  });
+
+  it.each([['1.5'], ['-1'], [' 1800000000'], [''], ['abc'], ['1'.repeat(13)]])('timestamp %j is malformed', (ts) => {
+    expect(verify({ ...h, [EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER]: ts })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'malformed-timestamp' });
+  });
+
+  it('a repeated (array) timestamp is malformed', () => {
+    expect(verify({ ...h, [EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER]: [String(NOW_MS / 1000)] })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'malformed-timestamp' });
+  });
+
+  it('no signature', () => {
+    const { [EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER]: _s, ...rest } = h;
+    expect(verify(rest)).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'missing-signature' });
+  });
+
+  it('uppercase hex, short, long, non-hex or repeated signatures are malformed — none reaches the compare', () => {
+    const sig = h[EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER];
+    for (const bad of [sig.toUpperCase(), sig.slice(1), `${sig}0`, `${sig.slice(1)}g`, [sig]]) {
+      expect(verify({ ...h, [EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER]: bad as string })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'malformed-signature' });
+    }
+  });
+});
+
+describe('KS-1717 S7 — the caller\'s bearer is bound (gate round 1, Minor 2)', () => {
+  // The round-1 gate replayed a captured hop under ANOTHER connector's token inside the window:
+  // the MAC covered the body and the timestamp but not the Authorization header. It now does.
+  const h = signed();
+  const OTHER = 'Bearer connector-b.header.payload.sig';
+
+  it('S7a the same bearer verifies', () => {
+    expect(verify(h)).toEqual({ ok: true, marker: 'originate-create' });
+  });
+
+  it('S7b a captured hop REPLAYED under another caller\'s bearer is refused', () => {
+    expect(verify({ ...h, authorization: OTHER })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+  });
+
+  it('S7c the bearer removed from a captured hop is refused', () => {
+    expect(verify({ ...h, authorization: undefined })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+  });
+
+  it('S7d the bearer is compared EXACTLY: another scheme spelling or extra whitespace is a different bearer', () => {
+    expect(verify({ ...h, authorization: AUTH.replace('Bearer', 'bearer') })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+    expect(verify({ ...h, authorization: `${AUTH} ` })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+  });
+
+  it('S7e a hop sent with NO bearer signs the empty value, and verifies only with no bearer', () => {
+    const none = signed({ authorization: undefined });
+    expect(verify({ ...none, authorization: undefined }).ok).toBe(true);
+    expect(verify({ ...none, authorization: AUTH })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+  });
+});

+// KS-1717: the ONE signing format for originate's own hop to anchoring. originate signs with
+// signEmitterHop, anchoring checks with verifyEmitterHop, so the two sides cannot drift apart.
+export {
+  EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER,
+  EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER,
+  EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER,
+  EMITTER_SIGNATURE_WINDOW_SECONDS,
+  EMITTER_SECRET_MIN_LENGTH,
+  isUsableEmitterSecret,
+  emitterSigningString,
+  signEmitterHop,
+  verifyEmitterHop,

+/**
+ * =============================================================================
+ * KS-1717 — the SIGNED originate -> anchoring hop (Kam's ruling (b), 2026-10-26)
+ * =============================================================================
+ * `x-emitter-internal` tells anchoring "this is K's own originate -> anchoring hop for
+ * an operation that ALREADY wrote its own action_provenance row", and anchoring then
+ * skips its flat-anchor `anchor` row (KS-566). Before this module anchoring honoured the
+ * marker by PRESENCE, and both of originate's setters forward the CALLER'S OWN bearer
+ * (`routes/documents.ts`, `originate-create` / `originate-lifecycle`) — so originate's
+ * hop and a direct compose-network caller holding the same connector JWT were
+ * indistinguishable, and the caller could suppress its own provenance row.
+ *
+ * This is the ONE implementation of the proof, used by BOTH sides: originate signs with
+ * `signEmitterHop`, anchoring checks with `verifyEmitterHop`. Two copies of a signing
+ * string are two chances to disagree, so neither service carries its own.
+ *
+ * WHAT IS SIGNED — the RAW BODY BYTES, never a re-serialised object. originate signs the
+ * exact string it sends; anchoring hashes the exact bytes it received (captured by
+ * `express.json`'s `verify` hook, before parsing). There is therefore no canonicalisation
+ * step to get wrong: a body re-serialised in transit — reordered keys, other whitespace —
+ * is a DIFFERENT body and is REFUSED. Refusal is the safe direction (see FAIL-CLOSED).
+ *
+ * AND THE CALLER'S BEARER (PR #1690 gate round 1, Minor 2). originate forwards the
+ * caller's own Authorization header on the hop, and anchoring attributes the provenance
+ * row to whoever that bearer names. Without it in the MAC, a hop captured from one
+ * connector replayed under ANOTHER connector's token, inside the window, verified. The
+ * header value is hashed EXACTLY as sent — no case-folding, no trimming — so any other
+ * value, including none, is a different bearer and is refused.
+ *
+ *   secuura-emitter-v1 \n <method> \n <path> \n <timestamp> \n <marker>
+ *     \n <sha256-hex(authorization, or "" when absent)> \n <sha256-hex(body)>
+ *
+ * No field can contain a newline (HTTP forbids it in a method, a request-target and a
+ * header value, and the two hashes are hex), so the join is unambiguous. The timestamp
+ * and marker enter as the header STRINGS, never as re-formatted values. The leading
+ * label separates this key's signatures from any other use of HMAC-SHA256 and versions
+ * the format.
+ *
+ * THE WINDOW — ±60 s, inclusive, on whole seconds. originate signs at send time, compose
+ * services share one host clock, and Container Apps hosts are NTP-synced, so a genuine
+ * hop arrives with a skew of milliseconds. 60 s is generous for that and short for a
+ * replay. Because both sides floor to the second, a hop stays acceptable for just under
+ * 61 s after signing when the clocks agree — and for up to about 121 s if the signer's
+ * clock runs up to 60 s fast, since a timestamp up to 60 s in the future is accepted too.
+ *
+ * RESIDUAL, stated so "signed" is not read as "complete": there is no nonce store. A
+ * byte-identical replay under the SAME bearer verifies inside that window. What it buys
+ * a caller who captured the hop is an exact retry, by the same connector, of an anchor
+ * originate already attributed, with no second `anchor` row. A replay with a DIFFERENT
+ * body, method, path, marker, timestamp or bearer is refused. Closing the same-bearer,
+ * same-body case needs a nonce store on the verify path.
+ *
+ * FAIL-CLOSED. A secret shorter than EMITTER_SECRET_MIN_LENGTH is treated as unset, and
+ * unset means nothing verifies — anchoring records the provenance row, so K's own hops
+ * write a second row (the pre-KS-566 duplication) until the secret is set on BOTH
+ * services. A duplicate row is visible and recoverable; a suppressed one is neither.
+ * Same shape as Kam's KS-1032 ruling for the sibling vouch: "Fail closed, deployed only
+ * after the secret is set". The length floor also means a mis-wired template placeholder
+ * (`placeholder-never-referenced`, 28 chars) can never become a working key.
+ *
+ * WHY THE HEADERS ARE NAMED `x-emitter-*`. api-gateway's edge strip removes every
+ * client-supplied `x-emitter-*` header on `/api/*` (`TRUST_HEADER_PATTERN`), so the
+ * timestamp and signature inherit that strip with no gateway change. It is not what
+ * makes them safe — the secret is — but it keeps them off the gateway path entirely.
+ */
+import crypto from 'crypto';
+
+export const EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER = 'x-emitter-internal';
+export const EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER = 'x-emitter-timestamp';
+export const EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER = 'x-emitter-signature';
+
+/** Accepted clock skew either side of the verifier's now, in seconds (inclusive). */
+export const EMITTER_SIGNATURE_WINDOW_SECONDS = 60;
+
+/** Below this length a configured secret is treated as unset (fail-closed). */
+export const EMITTER_SECRET_MIN_LENGTH = 32;
+
+const SIGNING_LABEL = 'secuura-emitter-v1';
+const TIMESTAMP_FORM = /^[0-9]{1,12}$/;
+const SIGNATURE_FORM = /^[0-9a-f]{64}$/;
+
+/** Why a marked request was not honoured. Logged by the verifier's caller; never the values. */
+export type EmitterHopRefusal =
+  | 'no-marker'
+  | 'no-secret'
+  | 'missing-timestamp'
+  | 'malformed-timestamp'
+  | 'stale-timestamp'
+  | 'missing-signature'
+  | 'malformed-signature'
+  | 'bad-signature';
+
+export type EmitterHopVerdict = { ok: true; marker: string } | { ok: false; reason: EmitterHopRefusal };
+
+/** True when `secret` is long enough to sign with. Anything else is "unset". */
+export function isUsableEmitterSecret(secret: unknown): secret is string {
+  return typeof secret === 'string' && secret.length >= EMITTER_SECRET_MIN_LENGTH;
+}
+
+/** The exact string both sides MAC. Exported so a test can pin the format itself. */
+export function emitterSigningString(fields: {
+  method: string;
+  path: string;
+  timestamp: string;
+  marker: string;
+  authorization: string | undefined;
+  body: Buffer | string;
+}): string {
+  const authHash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(fields.authorization ?? '', 'utf8').digest('hex');
+  const bodyHash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(fields.body).digest('hex');
+  return [SIGNING_LABEL, fields.method, fields.path, fields.timestamp, fields.marker, authHash, bodyHash].join('\n');
+}
+
+function mac(secret: string, signingString: string): string {
+  return crypto.createHmac('sha256', secret).update(signingString, 'utf8').digest('hex');
+}
+
+/**
+ * originate's side: the headers that mark ONE outbound hop. Always carries the marker;
+ * adds the timestamp and signature only with a usable secret. An unsigned marker is still
+ * sent so a half-configured deploy (anchoring keyed, originate not) shows up in anchoring's
+ * refusal log instead of silently doubling rows. `body` must be the exact string sent,
+ * and `authorization` the exact Authorization header value sent (undefined when none is):
+ * it is required, so a setter cannot forget to bind the bearer.
+ */
+export function signEmitterHop(params: {
+  secret: string | undefined;
+  marker: string;
+  method: string;
+  url: string;
+  authorization: string | undefined;
+  body: string;
+  nowMs?: number;
+}): Record<string, string> {
+  const headers: Record<string, string> = { [EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER]: params.marker };
+  if (!isUsableEmitterSecret(params.secret)) return headers;
+  const target = new URL(params.url);
+  const timestamp = String(Math.floor((params.nowMs ?? Date.now()) / 1000));
+  headers[EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER] = timestamp;
+  headers[EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER] = mac(
+    params.secret,
+    emitterSigningString({
+      method: params.method,
+      path: target.pathname + target.search,
+      timestamp,
+      marker: params.marker,
+      authorization: params.authorization,
+      body: params.body,
+    }),
+  );
+  return headers;
+}
+
+/**
+ * anchoring's side: does this request carry a marker originate signed? `path` is the
+ * request-target as received (express `req.originalUrl`); `body` is the raw bytes, or
+ * undefined when none were captured — which then hashes as empty and fails unless the
+ * signer also signed an empty body. The caller's bearer is read from `headers.authorization`
+ * (a non-string value counts as absent).
+ */
+export function verifyEmitterHop(params: {
+  secret: string | undefined;
+  method: string;
+  path: string;
+  headers: Record<string, string | string[] | undefined>;
+  body: Buffer | string | undefined;
+  nowMs?: number;
+}): EmitterHopVerdict {
+  const marker = params.headers[EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER];
+  if (typeof marker !== 'string' || marker.length === 0) return { ok: false, reason: 'no-marker' };
+  if (!isUsableEmitterSecret(params.secret)) return { ok: false, reason: 'no-secret' };
+
+  const timestamp = params.headers[EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER];
+  if (timestamp === undefined) return { ok: false, reason: 'missing-timestamp' };
+  if (typeof timestamp !== 'string' || !TIMESTAMP_FORM.test(timestamp)) {
+    return { ok: false, reason: 'malformed-timestamp' };
+  }
+  const nowSeconds = Math.floor((params.nowMs ?? Date.now()) / 1000);
+  if (Math.abs(nowSeconds - Number(timestamp)) > EMITTER_SIGNATURE_WINDOW_SECONDS) {
+    return { ok: false, reason: 'stale-timestamp' };
+  }
+
+  const signature = params.headers[EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER];
+  if (signature === undefined) return { ok: false, reason: 'missing-signature' };
+  if (typeof signature !== 'string' || !SIGNATURE_FORM.test(signature)) {
+    return { ok: false, reason: 'malformed-signature' };
+  }
+
+  const authorization = typeof params.headers.authorization === 'string' ? params.headers.authorization : undefined;
+  const expected = mac(
+    params.secret,
+    emitterSigningString({ method: params.method, path: params.path, timestamp, marker, authorization, body: params.body ?? '' }),
+  );
+  // Both are 64 hex chars by now, so the buffers are equal length and timingSafeEqual cannot throw.
+  if (!crypto.timingSafeEqual(Buffer.from(signature, 'hex'), Buffer.from(expected, 'hex'))) {
+    return { ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' };
+  }
+  return { ok: true, marker };
+}

+/**
+ * =============================================================================
+ * KS-1717 — POST /api/anchors honours `x-emitter-internal` only on a hop originate SIGNED
+ * =============================================================================
+ * THE DEFECT. The marker switches off the flat-anchor `anchor` provenance row (KS-566).
+ * anchoring honoured it by PRESENCE and had no inbound strip of its own, so a caller on
+ * the compose network holding a connector JWT could set it and suppress its own row
+ * (the #995 gate's note N1). originate's two setters forward the caller's OWN bearer, so
+ * no credential separates the genuine hop from that caller — only a signature can.
+ *
+ * WHY THIS FILE BOOTS THE REAL APP. The defect is in what the ROUTE does with a header,
+ * after the whole middleware chain has run. A cell that called the guard directly would
+ * pass with the guard unmounted. Every cell here therefore drives `POST /api/anchors` on
+ * the real `index.ts` app, with a real RS256 bearer, and observes the one effect that
+ * matters: whether `recordFlatAnchorProvenance` was called.
+ *
+ * HOW THE APP IS LOADED: the KS-1603 recipe (`express.application.listen` is spied before
+ * the import; the app is served on an ephemeral port). No database is connected —
+ * `dbPersistAnchor` returns before any SQL when `isDbAvailable()` is false — so this runs
+ * in the unit lane, and the provenance recorder is the only thing mocked.
+ *
+ * ONE TOKEN PER CELL. The signature covers the caller's Authorization header (gate round 1,
+ * Minor 2), and `token()` mints a new string whenever its `iat` second changes. So each cell
+ * mints its bearer ONCE and uses that same string to sign and to send.
+ *
+ * WHICH CELLS ARE RED ON DEVELOP (presence-honouring code): R1, R3, R4, R5, R8, R9, R10.
+ * R9 (a hop replayed under ANOTHER connector's bearer) is also red on the round-1 head,
+ * where the signature did not cover the bearer. R10 (a hop MAC'd under a 31-character
+ * secret) is red if the 32-character floor is removed (gate round 1, Note 4).
+ * WHICH ARE CONTROLS (green before and after): R2 — the genuine signed hop is still
+ * skipped, which a plain strip would break, and which also proves the guard is REACHED
+ * (an unmounted guard records here); R6 and R7 — the unmarked paths are unchanged; R11 —
+ * exactly 32 characters is enough.
+ * =============================================================================
+ */
+import { describe, it, expect, beforeAll, afterAll, beforeEach, vi } from 'vitest';
+import crypto from 'crypto';
+import http from 'http';
+import type { AddressInfo } from 'net';
+import express from 'express';
+import {
+  signEmitterHop,
+  emitterSigningString,
+  EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER,
+  EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER,
+  EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER,
+} from '@secuura/shared';
+
+vi.mock('../provenance', () => ({ recordFlatAnchorProvenance: vi.fn(async () => undefined) }));
+
+const SECRET = 'ks1717-anchoring-test-secret-0123456789abcdef';
+const TENANT = 'a0000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000001';
+const ORG = '1a171700-0000-4000-8000-0000000017a1';
+const CONNECTOR = 'connector:platform-s:ks1717';
+const CONNECTOR_B = 'connector:platform-s:ks1717-other-key';
+
+const { privateKey, publicKey } = crypto.generateKeyPairSync('rsa', { modulusLength: 2048 });
+const PRIVATE_PEM = privateKey.export({ type: 'pkcs8', format: 'pem' }).toString();
+const PUBLIC_PEM = publicKey.export({ type: 'spki', format: 'pem' }).toString();
+const b64url = (b: Buffer): string => b.toString('base64').replace(/\+/g, '-').replace(/\//g, '_').replace(/=+$/, '');
+function token(claims: Record<string, unknown>): string {
+  const header = b64url(Buffer.from(JSON.stringify({ alg: 'RS256', typ: 'JWT' })));
+  const now = Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000);
+  const payload = b64url(Buffer.from(JSON.stringify({ iat: now, exp: now + 600, ...claims })));
+  return `${header}.${payload}.${b64url(crypto.sign('RSA-SHA256', Buffer.from(`${header}.${payload}`), PRIVATE_PEM))}`;
+}
+/** The caller this ticket is about: a connector key, the same JWT originate would forward. */
+const CONNECTOR_TOKEN = () => token({ userId: CONNECTOR, role: 'connector', organizationId: ORG, tenantId: TENANT });
+/** Another connector key in the SAME tenant — the replayer the round-1 gate measured. */
+const CONNECTOR_B_TOKEN = () => token({ userId: CONNECTOR_B, role: 'connector', organizationId: ORG, tenantId: TENANT });
+/** An interactive caller: writes no flat-anchor row whatever the marker says (KS-566). */
+const USER_TOKEN = () => token({ userId: '2a171700-0000-4000-8000-0000000017a2', role: 'user', organizationId: ORG, tenantId: TENANT });
+
+let seq = 0;
+function anchorBody(): string {
+  seq += 1;
+  return JSON.stringify({ documentId: `doc-${Date.now()}${seq}-ks1717s${seq}`, contentHash: 'a'.repeat(64), network: 'preview' });
+}
+
+type Booted = { server: http.Server; base: string; recorder: ReturnType<typeof vi.fn> };
+
+/** Import the real app with EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET as given, and serve it. */
+async function boot(secret: string | undefined): Promise<Booted> {
+  vi.resetModules();
+  if (secret === undefined) delete process.env.EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET;
+  else process.env.EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET = secret;
+  vi.spyOn(express.application, 'listen').mockReturnValue(
+    { keepAliveTimeout: 0, headersTimeout: 0, close: () => undefined } as unknown as http.Server,
+  );
+  const app = (await import('../index')).default;
+  const { recordFlatAnchorProvenance } = await import('../provenance');
+  const server = http.createServer(app);
+  await new Promise<void>((resolve) => { server.listen(0, '127.0.0.1', resolve); });
+  return { server, base: `http://127.0.0.1:${(server.address() as AddressInfo).port}`, recorder: recordFlatAnchorProvenance as unknown as ReturnType<typeof vi.fn> };
+}
+
+async function close(b: Booted | undefined): Promise<void> {
+  if (!b) return;
+  (b.server as any).closeAllConnections?.();
+  await new Promise<void>((resolve) => b.server.close(() => resolve()));
+}
+
+/** POST the EXACT bytes given, with the headers given. */
+async function post(b: Booted, tok: string, body: string, extra: Record<string, string> = {}): Promise<number> {
+  const res = await fetch(`${b.base}/api/anchors`, {
+    method: 'POST',
+    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json', Authorization: `Bearer ${tok}`, ...extra },
+    body,
+  });
+  await res.text();
+  return res.status;
+}
+
+/** What originate's setter sends for this body and this caller's bearer, signed with `secret`. */
+function hop(b: Booted, body: string, secret: string | undefined, tok: string, nowMs?: number): Record<string, string> {
+  return signEmitterHop({
+    secret,
+    marker: 'originate-create',
+    method: 'POST',
+    url: `${b.base}/api/anchors`,
+    authorization: `Bearer ${tok}`,
+    body,
+    nowMs,
+  });
+}
+
+/** A hop MAC'd by hand under ANY key, so a cell can present a key the signer would refuse. */
+function hopMacedWith(key: string, body: string, tok: string): Record<string, string> {
+  const timestamp = String(Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000));
+  const signature = crypto
+    .createHmac('sha256', key)
+    .update(emitterSigningString({ method: 'POST', path: '/api/anchors', timestamp, marker: 'originate-create', authorization: `Bearer ${tok}`, body }), 'utf8')
+    .digest('hex');
+  return { [EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER]: 'originate-create', [EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER]: timestamp, [EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER]: signature };
+}
+

+  process.env.JWT_PUBLIC_KEY = Buffer.from(PUBLIC_PEM, 'utf8').toString('base64');
+  delete process.env.JWT_JWKS_URL;

+  delete process.env.PLATFORM_WALLET_MNEMONIC;

+  process.env.CARDANO_NETWORK = process.env.CARDANO_NETWORK || 'preview';

+afterAll(() => {

+  vi.restoreAllMocks();
+});

+  });

+    const tok = CONNECTOR_TOKEN();
+    const body = anchorBody();

+    expect(b.recorder).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+
+  it('R3 a captured signature REPLAYED onto a different body is refused, and that body gets its row', async () => {
+    const tok = CONNECTOR_TOKEN();
+    const signedFor = anchorBody();
+    const other = anchorBody();
+    expect(await post(b, tok, other, hop(b, signedFor, SECRET, tok))).toBe(202);
+    expect(b.recorder).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+    expect(b.recorder).toHaveBeenCalledWith(expect.objectContaining({ documentId: JSON.parse(other).documentId }));
+  });
+
+  it('R4 a signature whose timestamp has EXPIRED (120 s old) is refused', async () => {
+    const tok = CONNECTOR_TOKEN();
+    const body = anchorBody();
+    expect(await post(b, tok, body, hop(b, body, SECRET, tok, Date.now() - 120_000))).toBe(202);
+    expect(b.recorder).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+  });
+
+  it('R5 a signature made with any OTHER secret is refused', async () => {
+    const tok = CONNECTOR_TOKEN();
+    const body = anchorBody();
+    expect(await post(b, tok, body, hop(b, body, `${SECRET}-not-the-one`, tok))).toBe(202);
+    expect(b.recorder).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+  });

+    expect(await post(b, CONNECTOR_TOKEN(), anchorBody())).toBe(202);
+    expect(b.recorder).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+  });
+

+    const tok = USER_TOKEN();

+    expect(await post(b, tok, body, hop(b, body, SECRET, tok))).toBe(202);
+    expect(await post(b, tok, anchorBody())).toBe(202);
+    expect(b.recorder).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+
+  it('R9 a hop captured from one connector and REPLAYED under ANOTHER connector\'s bearer is refused, and the replayer gets its row', async () => {
+    const tokA = CONNECTOR_TOKEN();
+    const tokB = CONNECTOR_B_TOKEN();
+    expect(tokB).not.toBe(tokA);
+    const body = anchorBody();
+    // Every header originate signed for connector A, byte for byte — only the bearer changes.
+    expect(await post(b, tokB, body, hop(b, body, SECRET, tokA))).toBe(202);
+    expect(b.recorder).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+    expect(b.recorder).toHaveBeenCalledWith(expect.objectContaining({ documentId: JSON.parse(body).documentId, connectorId: CONNECTOR_B }));
+  });
+});
+

+  });
+});

+    }

+      expect(b.recorder).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+    } finally {
+      await close(b);
+    }
+  }, 30000);

+/**
+ * KS-1717 — anchoring's inbound guard for `x-emitter-internal`.
+ *
+ * The marker switches off the flat-anchor `anchor` provenance row on POST /api/anchors
+ * (KS-566). Until this guard it was honoured by PRESENCE, and anchoring had no inbound
+ * strip of its own: a caller on the compose network holding a connector JWT could set it
+ * and suppress its own row. originate's legitimate hop forwards the caller's own bearer,
+ * so no credential tells the two apart — only a signature originate makes and the caller
+ * cannot (`verifyEmitterHop`, @secuura/shared; Kam's ruling (b), 2026-10-26).
+ *
+ * FAIL-CLOSED BY CONSTRUCTION. The verdict is held in a WeakSet that only this guard
+ * writes, and the route reads it through `isVerifiedInternalEmit`. A request the guard
+ * never saw — the guard unmounted, or a router mounted ahead of it — is simply not in
+ * the set, so it records provenance. Nothing a header can say is consulted after the
+ * guard: it deletes the marker and both proof headers on EVERY request, verified or not.
+ *
+ * The raw body is captured by `express.json`'s `verify` hook (`captureRawBody`) because
+ * the signature covers the bytes as sent, not the parsed object. Without a capture the
+ * body hashes as empty and the check fails — refusal, never a false accept.
+ */
+import type { IncomingMessage } from 'http';
+import type { RequestHandler } from 'express';
+import {
+  EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER,
+  EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER,
+  EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER,
+  isUsableEmitterSecret,
+  verifyEmitterHop,
+} from '@secuura/shared';
+
+type Log = (level: 'info' | 'warn' | 'error', message: string, meta?: object) => void;
+
+const rawBodies = new WeakMap<IncomingMessage, Buffer>();
+const verifiedEmits = new WeakSet<IncomingMessage>();
+
+/** `express.json({ verify })` hook: keep the exact bytes the signature covers. */
+export function captureRawBody(req: IncomingMessage, _res: unknown, buf: Buffer): void {
+  rawBodies.set(req, buf);
+}
+
+/** True only for a request whose marker this guard verified. */
+export function isVerifiedInternalEmit(req: IncomingMessage): boolean {
+  return verifiedEmits.has(req);
+}
+
+/**
+ * Mount after `express.json({ verify: captureRawBody })` and ahead of every router.
+ * A refused marker is logged only when a secret IS configured: then it is either a
+ * forgery or a half-configured deploy, and both are worth a line. Unconfigured, every
+ * marker is refused and the boot line (`describeEmitterGuardState`) already says so.
+ * The log names the reason, never the header values.
+ */
+export function createEmitterSignatureGuard(secret: string, log: Log): RequestHandler {
+  const configured = isUsableEmitterSecret(secret);
+  return (req, _res, next) => {
+    if (req.headers[EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER] !== undefined) {
+      const verdict = verifyEmitterHop({
+        secret,
+        method: req.method,
+        path: req.originalUrl,
+        headers: req.headers,
+        body: rawBodies.get(req),
+      });
+      if (verdict.ok) {
+        verifiedEmits.add(req);
+      } else if (configured) {
+        log('warn', 'KS-1717: x-emitter-internal refused; the provenance row will be recorded', {
+          reason: verdict.reason,
+          method: req.method,
+          path: req.path,
+        });
+      }
+    }
+    delete req.headers[EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER];
+    delete req.headers[EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER];
+    delete req.headers[EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER];
+    next();
+  };
+}
+
+/**
+ * The boot line. States the CONSEQUENCE of the configuration, as originate's
+ * `describeState` does for the gateway vouch.
+ */
+export function describeEmitterGuardState(secret: string): { level: 'info' | 'warn'; message: string } {
+  if (isUsableEmitterSecret(secret)) {
+    return {
+      level: 'info',
+      message: '[emitter-guard] ACTIVE: x-emitter-internal is honoured only on a hop originate signed (KS-1717)',
+    };
+  }
+  return {
+    level: 'warn',
+    message:
+      '[emitter-guard] FAIL-CLOSED: EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET is unset or shorter than 32 characters, so x-emitter-internal is never honoured and originate\'s own hops each write a second action_provenance row (an `anchor` row beside the operation\'s own) until the secret is set on this service AND on originate',
+  };
+}

+/**
+ * =============================================================================
+ * KS-1717 — originate's two marker setters SIGN the hop, and the signature verifies
+ * =============================================================================
+ * anchoring now honours `x-emitter-internal` only when an HMAC over the request verifies
+ * (services/anchoring/src/emitterGuard.ts). That is only half a fix unless originate's
+ * real setters produce exactly what the verifier accepts — the KS-1719 lesson: a guard
+ * nothing asserts is SENT is not a guard. So these cells drive the two real setters
+ * through the real routes and hand the BYTES a recorder received to the very function
+ * anchoring calls (`verifyEmitterHop`, @secuura/shared). The signer and the verifier
+ * meeting on real wire bytes is the cross-direction proof; the shared cells pin each
+ * field in isolation.
+ *
+ *   O1  create (`originate-create`)       — signed, and verifies against the received bytes
+ *   O2  lifecycle (`originate-lifecycle`) — signed, and verifies against the received bytes
+ *   O3  no secret                         — the marker travels ALONE (anchoring then records)
+ *   O4  a secret under 32 chars           — treated as unset, same as O3
+ *   O5  the marker name originate's inbound strip deletes IS the one the signer sends
+ *
+ * O1 and O2 also prove the caller's BEARER is bound (PR #1690 gate round 1, Minor 2): the
+ * caller's own Authorization header rides the hop, the hop verifies under it, and the very
+ * same captured bytes are refused under another caller's bearer.
+ *
+ * RED ON DEVELOP: O1 and O2 (develop sends a bare marker). O3-O5 are controls.
+ *
+ * HARNESS: the ks1385-create shape — repositories, auth, rbac, logger and db mocked, the
+ * routers on a bare express app, anchoring replaced by a recorder on 127.0.0.1:0 that
+ * keeps each request's headers AND raw body.
+ */
+

+    req.on('end', () => {
+      recorded.push({ method: req.method || '', url: req.url || '', headers: req.headers, raw: Buffer.concat(chunks) });

+  jest.clearAllMocks();
+  recorded.length = 0;
+  delete process.env.SIMULATE_ANCHORING;
+  delete process.env.EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET;
+});
+
+/** The anchor POST lands after the response, so wait for it — bounded, never a fixed sleep. */
+async function waitForAnchorPost(timeoutMs = 4000): Promise<Recorded | undefined> {
+  const deadline = Date.now() + timeoutMs;
+  while (Date.now() < deadline) {
+    const hit = recorded.find((r) => r.method === 'POST' && r.url === '/api/anchors');
+    if (hit) return hit;
+    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 20));
+  }
+  return undefined;
+}
+
+/** What anchoring's guard decides about this recorded request, with `secret`. */
+function anchoringVerdict(hit: Recorded, secret: string) {
+  return verifyEmitterHop({ secret, method: hit.method, path: hit.url, headers: hit.headers, body: hit.raw });
+}
+
+/** The same captured bytes, presented under another caller's bearer — the round-1 replay. */
+function replayedUnder(hit: Recorded, authorization: string): Recorded {
+  return { ...hit, headers: { ...hit.headers, authorization } };
+}
+
+async function createDocument(): Promise<Response> {
+  return fetch(`${baseUrl}/api/documents`, {
+    method: 'POST',
+    headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json', authorization: CALLER_BEARER },
+    body: JSON.stringify({ title: 'ks1717 signed hop', type: 'general', data: { note: 'x' } }),
+  });
+}
+
+async function lifecycleEvent(): Promise<Response> {
+  const doc = {
+    id: '33333333-4444-4555-8666-777777777777',
+    contentHash: 'c'.repeat(64),
+    title: 'ks1717 lifecycle target',
+    ownerId: '11111111-2222-4333-8444-555555555555',
+    status: 'active',
+    type: 'general',
+  };
+  mockGetDocument.mockResolvedValue(doc);
+  return fetch(`${baseUrl}/api/documents/${doc.id}/lifecycle-events`, {
+    method: 'POST',
+    headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json', authorization: CALLER_BEARER },
+    body: JSON.stringify({ action: 'rename', payload: { to: 'x' } }),
+  });
+}
+
+describe('KS-1717 — with EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET set, both setters sign and anchoring\'s verifier accepts', () => {
+  it('O1 POST /api/documents: the originate-create hop verifies against the bytes anchoring received', async () => {
+    process.env.EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET = SECRET;
+    expect((await createDocument()).status).toBe(201);
+    const hit = await waitForAnchorPost();
+    expect(hit).toBeDefined();
+    expect(hit!.headers[EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER]).toMatch(/^[0-9]+$/);
+    expect(hit!.headers[EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER]).toMatch(/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/);
+    expect(hit!.headers.authorization).toBe(CALLER_BEARER);
+    expect(anchoringVerdict(hit!, SECRET)).toEqual({ ok: true, marker: 'originate-create' });
+    // ...and the same bytes under any other key are refused: the cell is not vacuous.
+    expect(anchoringVerdict(hit!, `${SECRET}-other`)).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+    // ...and under another caller's bearer: the hop is bound to the bearer it carried.
+    expect(anchoringVerdict(replayedUnder(hit!, OTHER_BEARER), SECRET)).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+  });
+
+  it('O2 POST /:id/lifecycle-events: the originate-lifecycle hop verifies against the bytes anchoring received', async () => {
+    process.env.EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET = SECRET;
+    expect((await lifecycleEvent()).status).toBeLessThan(400);
+    const hit = await waitForAnchorPost();
+    expect(hit).toBeDefined();
+    expect(hit!.headers.authorization).toBe(CALLER_BEARER);
+    expect(anchoringVerdict(hit!, SECRET)).toEqual({ ok: true, marker: 'originate-lifecycle' });
+    expect(anchoringVerdict(hit!, `${SECRET}-other`)).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+    expect(anchoringVerdict(replayedUnder(hit!, OTHER_BEARER), SECRET)).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
+  });
+});
+
+describe('KS-1717 — with no usable secret, the marker travels alone (and anchoring records the row)', () => {
+  it('O3 unset: the create hop carries the bare marker, no timestamp, no signature', async () => {
+    expect((await createDocument()).status).toBe(201);
+    const hit = await waitForAnchorPost();
+    expect(hit).toBeDefined();
+    expect(hit!.headers[SHARED_MARKER_HEADER]).toBe('originate-create');
+    expect(hit!.headers[EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER]).toBeUndefined();
+    expect(hit!.headers[EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER]).toBeUndefined();
+    expect(anchoringVerdict(hit!, SECRET)).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'missing-timestamp' });
+  });
+
+  it('O4 a 31-character secret is treated as unset: the lifecycle hop is unsigned', async () => {
+    process.env.EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET = 'x'.repeat(31);
+    expect((await lifecycleEvent()).status).toBeLessThan(400);
+    const hit = await waitForAnchorPost();
+    expect(hit).toBeDefined();
+    expect(hit!.headers[SHARED_MARKER_HEADER]).toBe('originate-lifecycle');
+    expect(hit!.headers[EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER]).toBeUndefined();
+  });
+});
+
+describe('KS-1717 O5 — one name for the marker', () => {
+  it('the header originate\'s inbound strip deletes (KS-741) is the header the shared signer sends', () => {
+    expect(ORIGINATE_STRIPPED_HEADER).toBe(SHARED_MARKER_HEADER);
+  });
+});

311: # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
312: # Shared secret proving to `originate` that a request arrived through
313: # `api-gateway`. originate honours identity/tenant/role headers only on a
314: # request carrying a matching `x-gateway-vouch`; on anything else it DROPS
315: # those headers (it does not refuse — m365-integration and nft-certificate call
316: # originate directly by design and neither sends a trust header).
317: #
318: # The NAME belongs here; the VALUE never does. Leave it blank in the template
319: # and generate a per-environment one:
320: #     openssl rand -hex 32
321: #
322: # WHERE IT MAY LIVE, and this part is load-bearing:
323: #   - `.env`, which compose interpolates into the `environment:` blocks of
324: #     api-gateway and originate ONLY.
325: #   - NOT `.env.local`. Four services declare `env_file: .env.local`
326: #     (api-gateway, auth, m365-integration, kyc), so a value placed there
327: #     reaches all four — measured, not assumed.
328: #
329: # UNSET FAILS CLOSED (Kam's ruling, 2026-10-13: "Fail closed, deployed only after
330: # the secret is set"). This REPLACES the previous "unset is fail-OPEN by design"
331: # wording, which described the behaviour before KS-1032. An unconfigured box now
332: # honours NO gateway-set identity header. It is not silent — originate logs a
333: # WARNING at boot naming the consequence.
334: #
335: # ⚠ SET THIS BEFORE DEPLOYING, and check what you get afterwards. On an unset or
336: # MISMATCHED secret, Platform-S metering does NOT answer 401: it answers 200 and
337: # reads the DEFAULT tenant's usage, because the gateway also attaches a connector
338: # Bearer that authenticate() accepts (measured by the pull request 1546 gate, cells
339: # GA1/GA2/GC5). 401 comes only when the gateway's token exchange failed, and
340: # MULTI_TENANCY_ENABLED=true with NODE_ENV=production gives 400 on every request.
341: # So a status-code check cannot tell a set secret from a missing one — assert that
342: # the metering response's data.tenantId is Platform S's key tenant.
343: GATEWAY_VOUCH_SECRET=
344: 
345: # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
346: # EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET — KS-1717 (the signed originate -> anchoring hop)
347: # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
348: # HMAC key originate signs its own hops to anchoring with; anchoring skips the
349: # flat-anchor `anchor` provenance row for `x-emitter-internal` ONLY when that
350: # signature (method, path, marker, a timestamp within 60 s, the caller's
351: # Authorization header, the raw body bytes) verifies. Without it, a direct
352: # compose-network caller holding a connector JWT could set the header and
353: # suppress its own provenance row.
354: #
355: # WHAT IT DOES NOT DO: anyone holding the key can sign, and there is no nonce
356: # store. A byte-identical replay under the SAME bearer verifies for just under
357: # 61 s after signing when the two clocks agree, and for up to about 121 s if the
358: # signer's clock runs up to 60 s fast. A replay with a different body, method,
359: # path, marker, timestamp or bearer is refused.
360: #
361: # The NAME belongs here; the VALUE never does. Leave it blank in the template
362: # and generate a per-environment one:
363: #     openssl rand -hex 32
364: #
365: # WHERE IT MAY LIVE:
366: #   - `.env`, which compose interpolates into the `environment:` blocks of
367: #     originate and anchoring ONLY. The same value on both; under 32 characters
368: #     counts as unset.
369: #   - NOT `.env.local` (four services load it).
370: #
371: # UNSET FAILS CLOSED: anchoring honours no marker and warns at boot, and
372: # originate's own hops each write a SECOND action_provenance row until the
373: # secret is set on BOTH services. Set it on both before deploying the images
374: # that check it.
375: EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET=

10: // KS-343 (cost) — standard services are sized 0.5 vCPU / 1 GiB (a valid ACA
11: // Consumption combo, memory = 2x vCPU). Was 1.0 / 2 GiB, which dominated the
12: // demo bill (~$440/mo of ~$470 was Container Apps compute, 24 services pinned
13: // at minReplicas:1 24/7; memory ~half of that). Azure Monitor 7d peak working
14: // set is <=309 MB on every service (most ~180 MB, frontends ~5 MB), so 1 GiB
15: // leaves 3x+ headroom. minReplicas stays 1 (no scale-to-zero) so there is no
16: // cold-start and no feature loss. redis (0.25/0.5) + pgbouncer (0.5/1) are
17: // unchanged. If a service's footprint grows past ~700 MB, bump it back up.

340: // KS-180: the RS256 private key is injected on the AUTH service ONLY (the sole
341: // signer). It is deliberately NOT part of commonSecrets — verifier services
342: // must never hold signing material; they receive the public key via the JWKS
343: // endpoint (KS-182). Sentinel until provisioned (Container Apps rejects empty
344: // secret values).
345: // KS-1041 Step 2 — the vouch secret is declared ONLY on the two apps that use
346: // it (api-gateway mints the header, originate verifies it), not in
347: // commonSecrets. commonSecrets reaches 20 container apps — 17 take it directly
348: // and authSecrets / gatewayVouchSecrets extend it — so declaring the vouch there
349: // would put it on 20 apps to serve 2. (`verifierFrontend`, the container the
350: // KS-1041 probe fired from, is not one of the 20: it declares its own inline
351: // `secrets:` holding only `acr-password`.) A secret whose entire value is its
352: // narrow reach must not be declared on apps with no use for it. This mirrors the
353: // authSecrets idiom directly below.
354: var gatewayVouchSecrets = concat(commonSecrets, [
355:   // Container Apps rejects empty secret values, so this entry
356:   // carries a placeholder when the param is unset — but NEITHER service
357:   // references it in that case (see the conditional env wiring below), so the
358:   // placeholder is never read.
359:   //
360:   // ⚠ It must NEVER be wired as a shared fallback value. If both services
361:   // received the same literal, the gateway would SEND it and originate would
362:   // MATCH it — a vouch that validates against a string published in this repo,
363:   // which is precisely the hole this change closes. And two DIFFERENT
364:   // placeholders would fail-closed on genuine gateway traffic, i.e. an outage.
365:   // Unset must mean "wire neither side", nothing else.
366:   {
367:     name: 'gateway-vouch-secret'
368:     value: empty(gatewayVouchSecret) ? 'placeholder-never-referenced' : gatewayVouchSecret
369:   }
370: ])
371: 
372: // KS-1717 — the emitter signing key is declared ONLY on the two apps that use it
373: // (originate signs, anchoring verifies), for the reason gatewayVouchSecrets gives above.
374: // The same placeholder rule holds: neither app is wired to it when the param is unset
375: // (see the conditional env wiring on each). Were it ever wired, it would still fail
376: // closed: the placeholder is 28 characters and the guard treats under 32 as unset.
377: var emitterSigningSecretEntry = {
378:   name: 'emitter-signing-secret'
379:   value: empty(emitterSigningSecret) ? 'placeholder-never-referenced' : emitterSigningSecret
380: }
381: var originateSecrets = concat(gatewayVouchSecrets, [emitterSigningSecretEntry])
382: var anchoringSecrets = concat(commonSecrets, [emitterSigningSecretEntry])
383: 
384: var authSecrets = concat(commonSecrets, [
385:   {
386:     name: 'jwt-private-key'
387:     value: empty(jwtPrivateKey) ? 'jwt-private-key-not-configured' : jwtPrivateKey
388:   }
389: ])
390: 
391: // Common environment variables
392: var commonEnvVars = [
393:   { name: 'NODE_ENV', value: 'staging' }
394:   // KS-201: pin every backend container to UTC so date-fns boundary functions
395:   // and any Date math are deterministic regardless of the region's host TZ.
396:   { name: 'TZ', value: 'UTC' }
397:   { name: 'DATABASE_URL', secretRef: 'database-url' }
398:   { name: 'REDIS_URL', secretRef: 'redis-url' }
399:   // KS-167/184/302: RS256-only. The public key (not secret) is injected on every
400:   // JWT-verifying service so verifiers can validate RS256 tokens; auth alone also
401:   // gets the PRIVATE key (authSecrets). The explicit-env api-gateway block
402:   // set JWT_PUBLIC_KEY directly (they don't use commonEnvVars). HS256/JWT_SECRET removed.
403:   { name: 'JWT_PUBLIC_KEY', value: jwtPublicKey }
404:   // KS-347: verifiers resolve the RS256 public key by the token's `kid` from
405:   // auth's JWKS endpoint (internal), falling back to JWT_PUBLIC_KEY above — so an
406:   // auth signing-key rotation is picked up WITHOUT redeploying the verifiers.
407:   { name: 'JWT_JWKS_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-auth.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}/.well-known/jwks.json' }
408:   { name: 'DEPLOYMENT_ID', value: deploymentId }
409:   { name: 'CORS_ORIGINS', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-issuer.${containerAppsEnvDomain},https://${resourcePrefix}-admin.${containerAppsEnvDomain},https://${resourcePrefix}-verifier.${containerAppsEnvDomain},https://${resourcePrefix}-outlook.${containerAppsEnvDomain},https://${resourcePrefix}-api.${containerAppsEnvDomain},https://outlook.office.com,https://outlook.office365.com' }
410:   // PII keys — wired on every service so any service that imports the
411:   // encryptedField helper has them available at startup. Services that
412:   // don't need them just have unused env vars.
413:   { name: 'PII_ENCRYPTION_KEY', secretRef: 'pii-encryption-key' }
414:   { name: 'PII_ENCRYPTION_KEY_VERSION', value: piiEncryptionKeyVersion }
415:   { name: 'PII_LOOKUP_HMAC_KEY', secretRef: 'pii-lookup-hmac-key' }
416: ]

418: // =============================================================================
419: // PGBOUNCER (KS-207) — single bounded transaction-pooling gateway to Postgres.
420: // Internal TCP ingress on 6432. minReplicas=maxReplicas=1 is REQUIRED: each
421: // replica holds its own pool, so scaling would multiply server connections and
422: // re-exhaust the 50-slot server. Runtime services reach it by short name
423: // (`${resourcePrefix}-pgbouncer`), which (a) resolves in-env over TCP and (b)
424: // has no "azure" substring, so the app pools' pgSslConfig keeps the app→pgbouncer
425: // leg plaintext while pgbouncer→PG uses TLS (PGBOUNCER_SERVER_TLS_SSLMODE=require).
426: // Deployed only when postgresHost is supplied (deploy.sh dev/demo).
427: // =============================================================================
428: resource pgbouncer 'Microsoft.App/containerApps@2023-05-01' = if (!empty(postgresHost)) {
429:   name: '${resourcePrefix}-pgbouncer'
430:   location: location
431:   properties: {
432:     managedEnvironmentId: containerAppsEnvironmentId
433:     configuration: {
434:       ingress: {
435:         external: false
436:         transport: 'tcp'
437:         targetPort: 6432
438:         exposedPort: 6432
439:       }
440:       registries: [
441:         {
442:           server: acrServer
443:           username: containerRegistryName
444:           passwordSecretRef: 'acr-password'
445:         }
446:       ]
447:       secrets: [
448:         { name: 'acr-password', value: acrPassword }
449:         { name: 'app-db-password', value: empty(appDbPassword) ? 'app-db-password-not-configured' : appDbPassword }
450:         { name: 'pgbouncer-admin-password', value: empty(postgresAdminPassword) ? 'pgbouncer-admin-not-configured' : postgresAdminPassword }
451:       ]
452:     }
453:     template: {
454:       containers: [
455:         {
456:           name: 'pgbouncer'
457:           image: '${acrServer}/secuura/pgbouncer:${imageTag}'
458:           resources: {
459:             cpu: json('0.5')
460:             memory: '1Gi'
461:           }
462:           env: [
463:             { name: 'TZ', value: 'UTC' }
464:             { name: 'POSTGRES_HOST', value: postgresHost }
465:             { name: 'POSTGRES_PORT', value: '5432' }
466:             { name: 'POSTGRES_DB', value: 'secuura' }
467:             // userlist auth: secuura_app is the runtime role the services use;
468:             // admin/stats are the pgbouncer console users.
469:             { name: 'APP_DB_USER', value: 'secuura_app' }
470:             { name: 'APP_DB_PASSWORD', secretRef: 'app-db-password' }
471:             { name: 'PGBOUNCER_ADMIN_PASSWORD', secretRef: 'pgbouncer-admin-password' }
472:             { name: 'PGBOUNCER_STATS_PASSWORD', secretRef: 'pgbouncer-admin-password' }
473:             // Bounded pool sized to max_connections=50 (reserve ~5 for superuser/replication).
474:             { name: 'PGBOUNCER_MAX_DB_CONNECTIONS', value: '45' }
475:             { name: 'PGBOUNCER_DEFAULT_POOL_SIZE', value: '40' }
476:             { name: 'PGBOUNCER_RESERVE_POOL_SIZE', value: '5' }
477:             { name: 'PGBOUNCER_RESERVE_POOL_TIMEOUT', value: '3' }
478:             { name: 'PGBOUNCER_MIN_POOL_SIZE', value: '0' }
479:             { name: 'PGBOUNCER_MAX_CLIENT_CONN', value: '2000' }
480:             // Azure flexible-server requires TLS on the upstream (pgbouncer→PG) leg.
481:             { name: 'PGBOUNCER_SERVER_TLS_SSLMODE', value: 'require' }
482:           ]
483:           probes: [
484:             {
485:               type: 'Liveness'
486:               tcpSocket: { port: 6432 }
487:               initialDelaySeconds: 15
488:               periodSeconds: 10
489:             }
490:             {
491:               type: 'Readiness'
492:               tcpSocket: { port: 6432 }
493:               initialDelaySeconds: 5
494:               periodSeconds: 5
495:             }
496:           ]
497:         }
498:       ]
499:       scale: {
500:         minReplicas: 1
501:         maxReplicas: 1
502:       }
503:     }
504:   }
505: }
506: 
507: // =============================================================================
508: // REDIS (KS Azure migration) — in-env Redis Container App replacing the retired
509: // "Azure Cache for Redis (classic)" managed resource. Azure has globally retired
510: // Microsoft.Cache/redis for NEW creates ("create Azure Managed Redis instead"),
511: // so the lean demo can no longer provision it. Old prod (secuura01) is being
512: // decommissioned, not redeployed, so this is an unconditional switch — there is
513: // no classic Redis path left to keep. Internal TCP ingress on 6379, no auth
514: // (env-internal only, same trust boundary as pgbouncer). minReplicas =
515: // maxReplicas = 1 is REQUIRED: Redis holds cache state in-memory, so scaling
516: // (or scale-to-zero) would split/lose it. Runtime services reach it by SHORT
517: // name (`${resourcePrefix}-redis`) over plain redis:// (no TLS, no key) — same
518: // in-env short-name + no-"azure"-substring pattern as pgbouncer.
519: // =============================================================================
520: resource redis 'Microsoft.App/containerApps@2023-05-01' = {
521:   name: '${resourcePrefix}-redis'
522:   location: location
523:   tags: commonTags
524:   properties: {
525:     managedEnvironmentId: containerAppsEnvironmentId
526:     configuration: {
527:       ingress: {
528:         external: false
529:         transport: 'tcp'
530:         targetPort: 6379
531:         exposedPort: 6379
532:       }
533:       // KS Azure migration: pull redis:8-alpine (KS-368) from ACR (imported there), NOT
534:       // Docker Hub — avoids the runtime anonymous pull-rate limit (KS-102).
535:       registries: [
536:         {
537:           server: acrServer
538:           username: containerRegistryName
539:           passwordSecretRef: 'acr-password'
540:         }
541:       ]
542:       secrets: [
543:         { name: 'acr-password', value: acrPassword }
544:       ]
545:     }
546:     template: {
547:       containers: [
548:         {
549:           name: 'redis'
550:           image: '${acrServer}/redis:8-alpine'
551:           resources: {
552:             cpu: json('0.25')
553:             memory: '0.5Gi'
554:           }
555:           env: [
556:             { name: 'TZ', value: 'UTC' }
557:           ]
558:           probes: [
559:             {
560:               type: 'Liveness'
561:               tcpSocket: { port: 6379 }
562:               initialDelaySeconds: 15
563:               periodSeconds: 10
564:             }
565:             {
566:               type: 'Readiness'
567:               tcpSocket: { port: 6379 }
568:               initialDelaySeconds: 5
569:               periodSeconds: 5
570:             }
571:           ]
572:         }
573:       ]
574:       scale: {
575:         minReplicas: 1
576:         maxReplicas: 1
577:       }
578:     }
579:   }
580: }
