582: // =============================================================================
583: // API GATEWAY (KS-34: moved here from main.bicep so `./deploy.sh services`
584: // rolls the gateway alongside every other service. Previously the gateway
585: // resource lived in main.bicep, which only ran during `setup` — meaning
586: // `services` deploys silently left the gateway on the previous image and
587: // the file-based migration loader never ran for new schema work.)
588: //
589: // The resource name '${resourcePrefix}-api' matches what main.bicep used
590: // historically, so Bicep's incremental deploy will UPDATE the existing
591: // resource rather than create a duplicate. The leftover declaration in
592: // main.bicep was removed in the same commit; Bicep incremental mode
593: // doesn't delete unmanaged resources, so there's no outage between the
594: // two template states.
595: // =============================================================================
596: resource apiGateway 'Microsoft.App/containerApps@2023-05-01' = {
597:   name: '${resourcePrefix}-api'
598:   location: location
599:   properties: {
600:     managedEnvironmentId: containerAppsEnvironmentId
601:     configuration: {
602:       ingress: {
603:         external: true
604:         targetPort: 8080
605:         transport: 'http'
606:         corsPolicy: {
607:           allowedOrigins: ['*']
608:           allowedMethods: ['GET', 'POST', 'PUT', 'DELETE', 'OPTIONS', 'PATCH']
609:           allowedHeaders: ['*']
610:           exposeHeaders: ['*']
611:           maxAge: 3600
612:         }
613:       }
614:       registries: [
615:         {
616:           server: acrServer
617:           username: containerRegistryName
618:           passwordSecretRef: 'acr-password'
619:         }
620:       ]
621:       secrets: gatewayVouchSecrets
622:     }
623:     template: {
624:       containers: [
625:         {
626:           name: 'api-gateway'
627:           image: '${acrServer}/secuura/api-gateway:${imageTag}'
628:           resources: {
629:             cpu: json('0.5')
630:             memory: '1Gi'
631:           }
632:           env: concat([
633:             { name: 'NODE_ENV', value: environment }
634:             { name: 'TZ', value: 'UTC' } // KS-201: deterministic UTC date handling
635:             // KS-546: gateway-only — unhandled rejections log + continue instead of
636:             // killing the process (KS-529 outage class). All other services keep 'exit'.
637:             { name: 'UNHANDLED_REJECTION_MODE', value: 'survive' }
638:             { name: 'PORT', value: '8080' }
639:             { name: 'DATABASE_URL', secretRef: 'database-url' }
640:             { name: 'PLATFORM_DATABASE_URL', secretRef: 'platform-database-url' }
641:             // KS-109: the gateway runs migrations + provisions secuura_app, so it
642:             // uses the admin/owner connection for that path (startup-migrations.ts
643:             // prefers MIGRATION_DATABASE_URL). DATABASE_URL above stays the shared
644:             // one, which becomes secuura_app for the runtime at the Stage-2 cutover.
645:             { name: 'MIGRATION_DATABASE_URL', secretRef: 'migration-database-url' }
646:             { name: 'MIGRATION_PLATFORM_DATABASE_URL', secretRef: 'migration-platform-database-url' }
647:             { name: 'APP_DB_PASSWORD', secretRef: 'app-db-password' }
648:             { name: 'REDIS_URL', secretRef: 'redis-url' }
649:             // KS-302: RS256-only. The gateway uses an explicit env (not commonEnvVars),
650:             // so JWT_PUBLIC_KEY must be set here for verifyRs256 (was JWT_SECRET/HS256).
651:             { name: 'JWT_PUBLIC_KEY', value: jwtPublicKey }
652:             // KS-347: resolve the verification key by `kid` from auth's JWKS
653:             // (zero-redeploy key rotation), falling back to JWT_PUBLIC_KEY above.
654:             // Explicit-env block, so it must be set here too (not via commonEnvVars).
655:             { name: 'JWT_JWKS_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-auth.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}/.well-known/jwks.json' }
656:             { name: 'DEPLOYMENT_ID', value: deploymentId }
657:             // Pen-test B-7/D-2: api-gateway refuses to start with wildcard
658:             // CORS in production-like envs (NODE_ENV=demo|staging|production).
659:             // Build the explicit allowlist from the same FQDN pattern used
660:             // for the per-service URLs.
661:             { name: 'CORS_ORIGINS', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-issuer.${containerAppsEnvDomain},https://${resourcePrefix}-admin.${containerAppsEnvDomain},https://${resourcePrefix}-verifier.${containerAppsEnvDomain},https://${resourcePrefix}-outlook.${containerAppsEnvDomain},https://${resourcePrefix}-api.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
662:             { name: 'AUTH_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-auth.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
663:             { name: 'ORIGINATE_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-originate.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
664:             { name: 'WALLET_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-wallet.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
665:             { name: 'PRISM_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-prism.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
666:             { name: 'TIMESTAMPING_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-timestamp.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
667:             { name: 'ANCHORING_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-anchoring.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
668:             { name: 'SECURITY_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-security.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
669:             { name: 'ANALYTICS_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-analytics.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
670:             { name: 'BILLING_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-billing.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
671:             { name: 'TOKENISATION_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-tokenisation.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
672:             { name: 'TENANT_PROVISIONING_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-tenant-prov.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
673:             { name: 'M365_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-m365.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
674:             // KS-40 wave 2 follow-up: the gateway's services map keyed on
675:             // "kyc" fell back to localhost when the env var was missing, so
676:             // those requests 502'd on Azure. Add the explicit internal FQDN.
677:             { name: 'KYC_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-kyc.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
678:             // KS-96 follow-up: the 5 newly-deployed services need explicit
679:             // internal FQDNs on the gateway too, else config/services.ts falls
680:             // back to http://localhost:<port> and the gateway 502s them on Azure
681:             // (ECONNREFUSED). Same fix pattern as kyc above.
682:             { name: 'GOVERNANCE_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-governance.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
683:             { name: 'TRANSFER_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-transfer.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
684:             { name: 'VC_ISSUER_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-vc-issuer.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
685:             { name: 'STAKING_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-staking.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
686:             { name: 'REFERRAL_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-referral.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
687:             // KS-127: with NFT_SERVICE_URL set, the gateway's NOT_DEPLOYED guard
688:             // (proxy.ts:776) becomes a no-op and /api/nft/* proxies to the nft app.
689:             { name: 'NFT_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-nft.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
690:             // Swagger UI + /api/docs/openapi.json are public-attack-surface
691:             // exposure (audit F-04). Default to enabled on `dev` only so
692:             // Schemathesis / Peter / anyone testing can reach the spec
693:             // without manually flipping the env var after every deploy.
694:             // Demo + prod fall through to the safe default (404).
695:             { name: 'ENABLE_PUBLIC_API_DOCS', value: environment == 'dev' ? 'true' : 'false' }
696:             // KS-206: raise the gateway's general API rate-limit ceiling on dev
697:             // only, so the Schemathesis harness can run at useful throughput (it
698:             // sends 80% of this/60 per second). Demo keeps its current effective
699:             // value (100 — NODE_ENV=demo => IS_PROD=false; the 60/min IS_PROD
700:             // branch is never reached). Unset => services.ts default 100/min.
701:             { name: 'RATE_LIMIT_MAX_REQUESTS', value: environment == 'dev' ? '2000' : '100' }
702:           ], empty(gatewayVouchSecret) ? [] : [
703:             // KS-1041 Step 2: minted onto outgoing proxy requests. Wired ONLY
704:             // when the param is set — unset means the header is never sent and
705:             // originate's check is inert, which is the documented fail-open.
706:             { name: 'GATEWAY_VOUCH_SECRET', secretRef: 'gateway-vouch-secret' }
707:           ])
708:           probes: [
709:             {
710:               type: 'Liveness'
711:               httpGet: {
712:                 path: '/health'
713:                 port: 8080
714:               }
715:               initialDelaySeconds: 30
716:               periodSeconds: 10
717:             }
718:             {
719:               type: 'Readiness'
720:               httpGet: {
721:                 path: '/health'
722:                 port: 8080
723:               }
724:               initialDelaySeconds: 5
725:               periodSeconds: 5
726:             }
727:           ]
728:         }
729:       ]
730:       scale: {
731:         minReplicas: 1
732:         maxReplicas: 20
733:         rules: [
734:           {
735:             name: 'http-rule'
736:             http: {
737:               metadata: {
738:                 concurrentRequests: '100'
739:               }
740:             }
741:           }
742:         ]
743:       }
744:     }
745:   }
746:   tags: commonTags
747: }

749: // =============================================================================
750: // ORIGINATE SERVICE (Core document certification)
751: // =============================================================================
752: resource originateService 'Microsoft.App/containerApps@2023-05-01' = {
753:   name: '${resourcePrefix}-originate'
754:   location: location
755:   tags: commonTags
756:   // KS-75 phase 1: enable system-assigned managed identity. The principal
757:   // exists from this deploy onwards but has no role assignments yet —
758:   // those are granted (and BACKUP_USE_MANAGED_IDENTITY=true is flipped on)
759:   // by KS-76 in a gated follow-up. Zero runtime change tonight.
760:   identity: {
761:     type: 'SystemAssigned'
762:   }
763:   properties: {
764:     managedEnvironmentId: containerAppsEnvironmentId
765:     configuration: {
766:       ingress: {
767:         external: false
768:         targetPort: 4000
769:         transport: 'http'
770:       }
771:       registries: [
772:         {
773:           server: acrServer
774:           username: containerRegistryName
775:           passwordSecretRef: 'acr-password'
776:         }
777:       ]
778:       secrets: originateSecrets
779:       // T3-A: Dapr sidecar enables automatic mTLS for service-to-service
780:       // calls between Dapr-enabled apps in the same Container Apps
781:       // Environment. appId is the service-mesh identity used by
782:       // sidecars; ours match the resource short-name convention.
783:       dapr: {
784:         enabled: true
785:         appId: 'originate'
786:         appPort: 4000
787:         appProtocol: 'http'
788:       }
789:     }
790:     template: {
791:       containers: [
792:         {
793:           name: 'originate'
794:           image: '${acrServer}/secuura/originate:${imageTag}'
795:           resources: {
796:             cpu: json('0.5')
797:             memory: '1Gi'
798:           }
799:           env: concat(commonEnvVars, [
800:             { name: 'PORT', value: '4000' }
801:             { name: 'SERVICE_NAME', value: 'originate' }
802:             // F-DEMO-VERIFY-01 fix (2026-05-01): demo (= production) MUST run
803:             // real Cardano anchoring. With SIMULATE_ANCHORING=true the auto-
804:             // anchor on POST /api/documents flips a draft to status:anchored
805:             // immediately with a fake `tx_sim_...` hash, and the hash-first
806:             // verifier then returns verified:true for any draft — destroying
807:             // the platform's primary trust property. Dev keeps simulation on
808:             // for fast feedback (Yaci DevKit substitute) until the dev
809:             // environment has Blockfrost wired.
810:             { name: 'SIMULATE_ANCHORING', value: environment == 'demo' ? 'false' : 'true' }
811:             { name: 'ANCHORING_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-anchoring.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
812:             // KS-289: originate resolves recipient emails + signs by calling
813:             // auth-service DIRECTLY (server-to-server), not via the public
814:             // gateway — the gateway CSRF-blocks service POSTs (certify's
815:             // /api/users/stub) and the localhost fallback is unreachable here.
816:             { name: 'AUTH_SERVICE_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-auth.internal.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
817:             { name: 'PLATFORM_DATABASE_URL', secretRef: 'platform-database-url' }
818:             { name: 'MULTI_TENANCY_ENABLED', value: 'true' }
819:             // H11: backup script reads these. SAS token is a secret;
820:             // account/container are non-sensitive identifiers.
821:             { name: 'AZURE_STORAGE_ACCOUNT',   value: backupStorageAccount }
822:             { name: 'AZURE_STORAGE_CONTAINER', value: backupStorageContainer }
823:             { name: 'AZURE_STORAGE_SAS_TOKEN', secretRef: 'backup-storage-sas-token' }
824:             // KS-75 phase 1: opt-in. Defaults to 'false' so the SAS path
825:             // (above) keeps working. Flip to 'true' as part of KS-76 once
826:             // the role assignment for the system-assigned identity is in
827:             // place on the backups storage account.
828:             { name: 'BACKUP_USE_MANAGED_IDENTITY', value: 'false' }
829:             // ACS Email (provisioned 2026-04-29). Both vars MUST be present
830:             // for the SDK path to engage; otherwise email.ts falls back to
831:             // SMTP / no-op log. acsSenderAddress empty → wires empty string,
832:             // which keeps ACS_ENABLED=false in code.
833:             { name: 'ACS_CONNECTION_STRING', secretRef: 'acs-connection-string' }
834:             { name: 'ACS_SENDER_ADDRESS',    value: acsSenderAddress }
835:             // KS-488 C-5: optional recipient allow-list. Empty = send to any
836:             // recipient (the behaviour before the guard). Set the param to
837:             // bound who can receive platform mail from this environment.
838:             { name: 'EMAIL_RECIPIENT_ALLOWLIST', value: emailRecipientAllowlist }
839:           ], empty(gatewayVouchSecret) ? [] : [
840:             // KS-1041 Step 2: verified against the vouch api-gateway mints.
841:             // Wired ONLY when the param is set. Unset leaves the secret empty,
842:             // the middleware inert, and originate warns at boot — the
843:             // documented fail-open. It is deliberately NOT in commonEnvVars:
844:             // that list reaches every service, and this secret's whole value
845:             // is its narrow reach.
846:             { name: 'GATEWAY_VOUCH_SECRET', secretRef: 'gateway-vouch-secret' }
847:           ], empty(emitterSigningSecret) ? [] : [
848:             // KS-1717: signs originate's own hops to anchoring. Wired ONLY when
849:             // the param is set; unset sends the marker unsigned and anchoring
850:             // records the row (fail-closed). Not in commonEnvVars, for the
851:             // reason given for the vouch above.
852:             { name: 'EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET', secretRef: 'emitter-signing-secret' }
853:           ])
854:         }
855:       ]
856:       scale: {
857:         minReplicas: 1
858:         maxReplicas: 20
859:       }
860:     }
861:   }
862: }

864: // =============================================================================
865: // AUTH SERVICE
866: // =============================================================================
867: resource authService 'Microsoft.App/containerApps@2023-05-01' = {
868:   name: '${resourcePrefix}-auth'
869:   location: location
870:   tags: commonTags
871:   // KS-346: system-assigned managed identity so auth authenticates to Key Vault
872:   // at runtime (DefaultAzureCredential → managed identity). The matching access
873:   // policy (get/list secrets) is `authKeyVaultAccess` below. Set unconditionally
874:   // (mirrors originate's KS-75 identity); harmless in an env with no vault. This
875:   // also stops a `services` redeploy from STRIPPING an identity attached manually
876:   // in KS-326 — the regression KS-346 exists to prevent.
877:   identity: {
878:     type: 'SystemAssigned'
879:   }
880:   properties: {
881:     managedEnvironmentId: containerAppsEnvironmentId
882:     configuration: {
883:       ingress: {
884:         external: false
885:         targetPort: 4003
886:         transport: 'http'
887:       }
888:       registries: [
889:         {
890:           server: acrServer
891:           username: containerRegistryName
892:           passwordSecretRef: 'acr-password'
893:         }
894:       ]
895:       secrets: authSecrets
896:       dapr: {
897:         enabled: true
898:         appId: 'auth'
899:         appPort: 4003
900:         appProtocol: 'http'
901:       }
902:     }
903:     template: {
904:       containers: [
905:         {
906:           name: 'auth'
907:           image: '${acrServer}/secuura/auth:${imageTag}'
908:           resources: {
909:             cpu: json('0.5')
910:             memory: '1Gi'
911:           }
912:           env: concat(commonEnvVars, [
913:             { name: 'PORT', value: '4003' }
914:             { name: 'SERVICE_NAME', value: 'auth' }
915:             // KS-254: argon2 verifies run on the libuv threadpool (default 4);
916:             // 8 lets concurrent logins hash in parallel (8 × 64 MiB fits 1Gi).
917:             { name: 'UV_THREADPOOL_SIZE', value: '8' }
918:             { name: 'JWT_EXPIRES_IN', value: '1d' }
919:             { name: 'ENABLE_DEMO_SEED', value: 'true' }
920:             // Pen-test F-05 (post-fix): seed gate is explicit two-key opt-in.
921:             // Demo deliberately runs with the default `*123` seed passwords
922:             // — these are intentional public demo credentials, documented in
923:             // USER_TESTING/CREDENTIALS-AND-PORTALS.md. Rotate per-tenant by
924:             // setting DEMO_<TENANT>_PASSWORD env vars on this container.
925:             { name: 'ALLOW_DEFAULT_SEED_PASSWORDS', value: 'true' }
926:             { name: 'PLATFORM_DATABASE_URL', secretRef: 'platform-database-url' }
927:             // ACS Email (provisioned 2026-04-29) — auth sends verification
928:             // + password-reset emails. Same wiring as originate.
929:             { name: 'ACS_CONNECTION_STRING', secretRef: 'acs-connection-string' }
930:             { name: 'ACS_SENDER_ADDRESS',    value: acsSenderAddress }
931:             // KS-488 C-5: optional recipient allow-list. Empty = send to any
932:             // recipient (the behaviour before the guard). Set the param to
933:             // bound who can receive platform mail from this environment.
934:             { name: 'EMAIL_RECIPIENT_ALLOWLIST', value: emailRecipientAllowlist }
935:             // KS-180: RS256 signing material — injected on auth ONLY (it is the
936:             // sole signer). Verifiers never receive the private key; they get
937:             // the public key via JWKS (KS-182). Sentinel until provisioned;
938:             // auth starts signing with it in KS-181.
939:             { name: 'JWT_PRIVATE_KEY', secretRef: 'jwt-private-key' }
940:             { name: 'JWT_KEY_ID',      value: jwtKeyId }
941:           ], empty(keyVaultName) ? [] : [
942:             // KS-346: runtime KMS — auth's SecretsManager (@secuura/shared) reads
943:             // AZURE_KEY_VAULT_URL and fetches `jwt-private-key` from Key Vault at
944:             // startup via the managed identity above. The JWT_PRIVATE_KEY secret
945:             // stays an ALWAYS-ON env fallback (jwt.ts initJwtKeys fallbackToEnv:true),
946:             // so an unreachable/empty vault can never take auth down. The KV DNS
947:             // suffix comes from az.environment() — the `az` namespace, because the
948:             // `environment` PARAM here shadows the bare environment() function.
949:             { name: 'AZURE_KEY_VAULT_URL', value: 'https://${keyVaultName}${az.environment().suffixes.keyvaultDns}/' }
950:           ])
951:         }
952:       ]
953:       scale: {
954:         minReplicas: 1
955:         maxReplicas: 20
956:       }
957:     }
958:   }
959: }
960: 
961: // =============================================================================
962: // KS-346: AUTH → KEY VAULT ACCESS POLICY (runtime KMS)
963: // =============================================================================
964: // Grant auth's system-assigned identity get/list on the vault's secrets so its
965: // runtime SecretsManager can read `jwt-private-key`. `name: 'add'` MERGES into
966: // the vault's existing access policies (does NOT replace them). Applied on every
967: // `services` deploy, so the enablement is durable (was a manual
968: // `az keyvault set-policy` in KS-326). The vault uses the access-policy model
969: // (deploy.sh creates it with --enable-rbac-authorization false), so an access
970: // policy — not an RBAC role assignment — is the correct mechanism, and the deploy
971: // SP (Contributor) can write it (no Microsoft.Authorization role grant needed).
972: resource authKeyVaultAccess 'Microsoft.KeyVault/vaults/accessPolicies@2023-07-01' = if (!empty(keyVaultName)) {
973:   parent: runtimeKeyVault
974:   name: 'add'
975:   properties: {
976:     accessPolicies: [
977:       {
978:         tenantId: subscription().tenantId
979:         objectId: authService.identity.principalId
980:         permissions: {
981:           secrets: [ 'get', 'list' ]
982:         }
983:       }
984:     ]
985:   }
986: }
987:

1487:           ])
1488:         }

1494:         // booted, breaking the synchronous request path (admin operations,

1612: // M365 INTEGRATION SERVICE (Entra / SharePoint / Outlook / Teams backend)
1613: // =============================================================================
1614: resource m365IntegrationService 'Microsoft.App/containerApps@2023-05-01' = {
1615:   name: '${resourcePrefix}-m365'
1616:   location: location
1617:   tags: commonTags
1618:   properties: {
1619:     managedEnvironmentId: containerAppsEnvironmentId
1620:     configuration: {
1621:       ingress: {
1622:         external: false
1623:         targetPort: 4013
1624:         transport: 'http'
1625:       }
1626:       registries: [
1627:         {
1628:           server: acrServer
1629:           username: containerRegistryName
1630:           passwordSecretRef: 'acr-password'
1631:         }
1632:       ]
1633:       secrets: commonSecrets
1634:     }
1635:     template: {
1636:       containers: [
1637:         {
1638:           name: 'm365-integration'
1639:           image: '${acrServer}/secuura/m365-integration:${imageTag}'
1640:           resources: {
1641:             cpu: json('0.5')
1642:             memory: '1Gi'
1643:           }
1644:           env: concat(commonEnvVars, [
1645:             { name: 'PORT', value: '4013' }
1646:             { name: 'SERVICE_NAME', value: 'm365-integration' }
1647:             { name: 'PLATFORM_DATABASE_URL', secretRef: 'platform-database-url' }
1648:             { name: 'ENTRA_TENANT_ID', value: entraTenantId }
1649:             { name: 'ENTRA_CLIENT_ID', value: entraClientId }
1650:             { name: 'ENTRA_CLIENT_SECRET', secretRef: 'entra-client-secret' }
1651:             // KS-1677 (gate BG1 B1): Microsoft must send the browser back to the ADMIN host. The admin wizard calls
1652:             // init with a relative fetch, so the sign-in's binding cookie is set host-only on the admin host; a
1653:             // callback on the API host would never carry it and every sign-in would be refused. Admin nginx already
1654:             // proxies /api/ to the API. Same host expression as FRONTEND_URL below; same condition as before.
1655:             // Deploy precondition: this URI must be registered on the Entra app first.
1656:             { name: 'ENTRA_REDIRECT_URI', value: empty(apiPublicUrl) ? '' : 'https://${resourcePrefix}-admin.${containerAppsEnvDomain}/api/m365/callback' }
1657:             // KS-344: the m365 OAuth connect flow finishes by redirecting the
1658:             // user to FRONTEND_URL + /admin/integrations (success or error).
1659:             // Unset here, the service fell back to http://localhost:6882 (see
1660:             // services/m365-integration/src/index.ts) so every redirect on the
1661:             // demo env pointed at the developer's machine. Point it at the admin
1662:             // portal host — the integrations page lives in the admin dashboard.
1663:             { name: 'FRONTEND_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-admin.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
1664:             // KS-349: the Outlook Adaptive-Card 'View in Secuura' button links to
1665:             // /verify/result/<id>, which lives on the VERIFIER portal — not admin.
1666:             // With FRONTEND_URL = admin host that link 404s. Give the verify link
1667:             // its own base pointing at the verifier portal host.
1668:             { name: 'VERIFIER_URL', value: 'https://${resourcePrefix}-verifier.${containerAppsEnvDomain}' }
1669:             { name: 'SHAREPOINT_SITE_URL', value: sharepointSiteUrl }
1670:           ])
1671:         }
1672:       ]
1673:       scale: {
1674:         // KS-81 (2026-05-15): bumped from minReplicas:0 to keep one replica
1675:         // warm. Cold-start exceeded the gateway's proxy timeout window — the
1676:         // first request after a 5-min idle period 504'd while the container
1677:         // booted, breaking the synchronous request path (admin operations,
1678:         // cert issuance, M365 integration). Cost delta: ~one always-on
1679:         // replica per env per service. tokenomics is intentionally left at
1680:         // minReplicas:0 — it's a marketing site where cold-start latency
1681:         // is acceptable.
1682:         minReplicas: 1
1683:         maxReplicas: 20
1684:       }
1685:     }
1686:   }
1687: }

1: # =============================================================================
2: # SECUURA DEVELOPMENT ENVIRONMENT
3: # =============================================================================
4: # This docker-compose file sets up the complete Secuura development environment
5: # including all microservices, databases, and local Cardano devnet.
6: #
7: # IMPORTANT: Default passwords are for LOCAL DEVELOPMENT ONLY.
8: # For production/staging, set ALL passwords via a .env file or secrets manager:
9: #   POSTGRES_PASSWORD, REDIS_PASSWORD, JWT_SECRET
10: #
11: # Usage:
12: #   docker-compose up -d              # Start all services
13: #   docker-compose up -d postgres redis  # Start only infrastructure
14: #   docker-compose logs -f originate  # Follow logs for a service
15: #   docker-compose down               # Stop all services
16: #
17: # =============================================================================
18: 
19: # =============================================================================
20: # STACK OWNERSHIP LABELS (KS-666)
21: # =============================================================================
22: # Merged into EVERY service below, so a running stack carries its owner, branch,
23: # commit and start time on every container rather than on one marker alone.
24: #
25: # A YAML anchor cannot cross an `include:` boundary, so this block is declared in
26: # each compose file that defines services — duplicated deliberately, not by
27: # oversight.
28: #
29: # The `:-` defaults must NOT become `:?required`: required-variable syntax makes
30: # every plain `docker compose config`/`ps` fail for anyone who has not exported
31: # these, including both CI workflows.
32: # KS-667 / s86-s87 trap: an image cannot say which commit it was built from. Tonight a
33: # gateway image had to be identified by build TIME plus a content probe because it
34: # carried no revision label, and the deploy-row base SHA in the KS-742 record had to be
35: # inferred from an rsync manifest. `org.opencontainers.image.revision` makes that a fact
36: # instead of an inference, permanently, for every future rollback image.
37: #
38: # Reuses STACK_COMMIT rather than introducing a second variable, so the image label and
39: # the container label below can never disagree about which commit is running.
40: x-image-labels: &image_labels
41:   org.opencontainers.image.revision: "${STACK_COMMIT:-unknown}"
42:   org.opencontainers.image.source: "https://github.com/Secuura/Distributed_Secuura"
43: 
44: x-stack-labels: &stack_labels
45:   com.secuura.stack.owner: "${STACK_OWNER:-unknown}"
46:   com.secuura.stack.branch: "${STACK_BRANCH:-unknown}"
47:   com.secuura.stack.commit: "${STACK_COMMIT:-unknown}"
48:   # ISO 8601 UTC — KS-201 requires every timestamp be region-independent.
49:   com.secuura.stack.started_at: "${STACK_STARTED_AT:-unknown}"
50: 
51: services:
52:   # ===========================================================================
53:   # STACK OWNERSHIP MARKER (KS-666)
54:   # ===========================================================================
55:   # WHY: the VM hosts one shared, mutable stack. Before KS-666 nothing recorded
56:   # who was using it, so the routine `down -v` + `git checkout develop` recovery
57:   # path destroyed other people's checkouts, volumes and uncommitted work
58:   # silently and reported success. This container is the ownership record that
59:   # `scripts/stack_guard.sh` reads before any destructive operation.
60:   #
61:   # WHY A CONTAINER RATHER THAN A STATE FILE: a label dies with the stack, so it
62:   # cannot outlive a crash or a manual teardown and go stale. A lease file can,
63:   # and a stale lease is worse than none — it trains people to ignore it.
64:   #
65:   # WHY A MARKER *AS WELL AS* LABELS ON EVERY SERVICE: every service carries the
66:   # `x-stack-labels` anchor (see the top of this file), so ownership is readable
67:   # from any container. The marker adds one container that exists for no other
68:   # reason, so ownership survives the moments when the rest of the stack does not
69:   # — mid-build, mid-teardown, or when services are stopped individually. Reading
70:   # ownership off a service that happens to be down would otherwise report "no
71:   # stack running" while the stack is plainly up, which is the false all-clear
72:   # this whole mechanism exists to avoid.
73:   #
74:   # The anchor is declared in BOTH compose files because a YAML anchor cannot
75:   # cross an `include:` boundary — duplicated deliberately, not by oversight.
76:   stack-marker:
77:     image: busybox:1.37
78:     container_name: ${STACK_PREFIX:-secuura}-stack-marker
79:     restart: unless-stopped
80:     user: "65534:65534"
81:     # BusyBox's `sleep` takes integer seconds only — `sleep infinity` is a GNU
82:     # coreutils extension and exits non-zero here, which under `restart:
83:     # unless-stopped` would crash-loop the marker and intermittently blind the
84:     # guard. Loop instead.
85:     command: ["sh", "-c", "while true; do sleep 3600; done"]
86:     labels:
87:       <<: *stack_labels
88:       com.secuura.stack.owner: "${STACK_OWNER:-unknown}"
89:       com.secuura.stack.branch: "${STACK_BRANCH:-unknown}"
90:       com.secuura.stack.commit: "${STACK_COMMIT:-unknown}"
91:       # ISO 8601 UTC — KS-201 requires every timestamp be region-independent.
92:       com.secuura.stack.started_at: "${STACK_STARTED_AT:-unknown}"
93:     environment:
94:       TZ: UTC
95:     healthcheck:
96:       test: ["CMD", "true"]
97:       interval: 60s
98:       timeout: 3s
99:       retries: 2
100:     networks:
101:       - secuura-network

124:       # deployment/azure/deploy.sh). So these defaults cannot reach a deployed
125:       # environment, and the literals are throwaway local values, NOT real
126:       # secrets (verified: the APP_DB_PASSWORD default below does not match the
127:       # live dev secret). Production is additionally guarded by

131:       # team's existing local stacks, and a fresh volume bakes the DB password
132:       # at first init).
133:       POSTGRES_USER: ${POSTGRES_USER:-secuura}

135:       POSTGRES_DB: ${POSTGRES_DB:-secuura}
136:       # Security settings
137:       POSTGRES_INITDB_ARGS: "--auth-host=scram-sha-256 --auth-local=scram-sha-256"
138:       # KS-72: forward APP_DB_* into the postgres container so the
139:       # docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/01-create-app-role.sh script can provision
140:       # the secuura_app role with the same password every service's
141:       # DATABASE_URL is built from. Without this, a fresh volume hits the
142:       # KS-8 guardrail ("FATAL: APP_DB_PASSWORD is not set"), the role is
143:       # never created, subsequent init scripts are skipped, and every service
144:       # auth-fails through pgbouncer.

146:       APP_DB_PASSWORD: ${APP_DB_PASSWORD:-YuBzECVdrAU9nUHhQDWMg2yMMSlgrxJP}
147:     # KS-614: bound to LOOPBACK, not 0.0.0.0. The old form published to every
148:     # interface, and on the demo VM `ss -lntp` showed docker-proxy on
149:     # 0.0.0.0:6432 with ufw inactive and an empty DOCKER-USER chain — i.e. no
150:     # host firewall at all, leaving one Azure NSG rule as the sole control.
151:     # Docker's published ports bypass ufw, so "we'll add a host firewall later"
152:     # does not fix this; binding is what fixes it. Every in-repo consumer uses

154:     # (The previous "remove in production" comment was never a control.)

429:       - PORT=8080
430:       # Swagger UI gate — default FALSE (BACKLOG 2026-07-17 / #555 decision):
431:       # this same compose file runs the internet-facing demo VM, where the old
432:       # `:-true` default published the full Swagger UI + raw spec and defeated
433:       # the F-04 opt-in guard. Local dev opts in via ENABLE_PUBLIC_API_DOCS=true

435:       - ENABLE_PUBLIC_API_DOCS=${ENABLE_PUBLIC_API_DOCS:-false}
436:       # KS-546 (Kam, 2026-07-31): the gateway SURVIVES unhandled promise
437:       # rejections (log + continue) instead of Node's fail-fast exit — KS-529
438:       # proved one unguarded async path here is a platform-wide outage. The
439:       # gateway is the ONLY service with this; everything else keeps 'exit',
440:       # and uncaughtException still exits everywhere.
441:       - UNHANDLED_REJECTION_MODE=survive
442:       # KS-1041 Step 2: proves to originate that a request came through this
443:       # gateway before originate honours any identity/tenant/role header.
444:       # Declared HERE and on `originate` only — deliberately NOT in
445:       # `.env.local`, which api-gateway, auth, m365-integration and kyc all
446:       # load, and which would therefore hand the secret to two services with
447:       # no business holding it (measured: a .env.local value reads identically
448:       # in all four containers). Compose passes through nothing a service does
449:       # not declare, so this keeps the blast radius at 2 containers of 34.
450:       # Empty default => header not sent => originate treats calls as
451:       # unvouched and drops trust headers. Unconfigured loses privilege.
452:       - GATEWAY_VOUCH_SECRET=${GATEWAY_VOUCH_SECRET:-}

475:       # KS-215: CORE_MIGRATIONS need DDL (CREATE TABLE/ROLE), but the runtime
476:       # DATABASE_URL above is the least-priv secuura_app role (KS-92/KS-109), so
477:       # startup-migrations fails "permission denied for schema public" and the
478:       # CORE tables it owns (charge_events, platform_document_registry, ...) are
479:       # never created on local — which 500s cert-issue. Give the gateway a
480:       # superuser migration connection (the POSTGRES_USER owner, direct to
481:       # postgres, NOT pgbouncer) so migrations run as owner. startup-migrations

784:       # Pen-test F-05 gate (introduced post-base-seed) had been leaving the 11

786:       # to true here ONLY because docker-compose.yml is the local-dev compose;
787:       # production is on Container Apps where this env var is unset and the
788:       # gate stays closed. See SEEDED_USERS_AND_ROLES.md §11.
789:       - ALLOW_DEFAULT_SEED_PASSWORDS=${ALLOW_DEFAULT_SEED_PASSWORDS:-}
790:       # KS-971: forward the platform SYSTEM_ADMIN's seed password.
791:       # (Carried on KS-682 too, byte-identical, so whichever PR lands first fixes fresh local
792:       # stacks — measured 2026-09-10 on slot 1: without it the seeder logs "no password
793:       # supplied" for admin@secuura.com, the bootstrap admin cannot grant roles, and the
794:       # Playwright suite's fallback account 401s. The other lands as a no-op.)
795:       #
796:       # KS-964 removed the published fallback for admin@secuura.com — the seeder's
797:       # `requiredSeedPw('ADMIN_USER_PASSWORD')` returns null when this is unset, and the account is
798:       # then written with an unusable password hash and `status = 'suspended'`. That is correct, and
799:       # it is why `admin@secuura.com / admin123` now returns 401 (KS-972).
800:       #
801:       # But this variable was forwarded to the auth service NOWHERE, so setting it in
802:       # `Blockchain/Dev/.env` — the obvious place, and where `.env.example:133` templates it — could
803:       # never reach the container: compose reads `.env` only to substitute `${...}` into variables
804:       # listed here, and this one was not listed. The only route in was `.env.local`, the service's
805:       # `env_file`. So the documented knob silently did nothing wherever it was most likely to be
806:       # set, and the account stayed unusable however carefully it was configured.
807:       #
808:       # The `:-` default means an unset host variable arrives in the container as an EMPTY STRING,
809:       # not as absent. That is safe here, and only because the seeder reads it as
810:       # `process.env.ADMIN_USER_PASSWORD || null`: '' is falsy, so it takes the null branch, skips
811:       # the account and leaves it suspended. Fail-closed either way — but if that read is ever
812:       # changed to `?? null`, an empty string would become a seeded blank password on the
813:       # cross-tenant SYSTEM_ADMIN. Verified with `docker compose config`: unset -> "", set -> value.
814:       #
815:       # NOTE: the sibling DEMO_*_PASSWORD / *_EMAIL overrides the seeder also reads are still not
816:       # forwarded. They are unaffected by this defect because they carry published fallbacks and are
817:       # gated on ALLOW_DEFAULT_SEED_PASSWORDS, so they work without an override — but a value set in
818:       # `.env` expecting it to apply will not. Forwarding those is a separate change.
819:       - ADMIN_USER_PASSWORD=${ADMIN_USER_PASSWORD:-}
820:       # Email / SMTP — KS-488 C-5. This block previously defaulted SMTP_ENABLED
821:       # to TRUE with a real provider as the host, so every deployment of this
822:       # file ran the auth service with the transport ON, pointed at a third
823:       # party, without anyone setting anything. Defaults off and empty now; see
824:       # the note on the originate service above.
825:       - SMTP_HOST=${SMTP_HOST:-}
826:       - SMTP_PORT=${SMTP_PORT:-465}
827:       - SMTP_USER=${SMTP_USER:-}
828:       - SMTP_PASS=${SMTP_PASS:-}
829:       - SMTP_FROM=${SMTP_FROM:-}
830:       - SMTP_ENABLED=${SMTP_ENABLED:-false}
831:       # KS-488 C-5 — see the note on the originate service above.
832:       - EMAIL_RECIPIENT_ALLOWLIST=${EMAIL_RECIPIENT_ALLOWLIST:-}
833:       # Social OAuth, Entra ID, Auth0 credentials loaded via env_file (.env.local)

835:       pgbouncer:
836:         condition: service_healthy
837:       redis:
838:         condition: service_healthy
839:       migrations:
840:         condition: service_completed_successfully

842:       test: ["CMD", "wget", "-q", "--spider", "http://localhost:4003/health/ready"]
843:       interval: 15s
844:       timeout: 5s
845:       retries: 3
846:       start_period: 10s

848:       resources:
849:         limits:

851:           # UV_THREADPOOL_SIZE=8 argon2 verifies (8 × 64 MiB) real headroom.

1326:       # default FAILS CLOSED: no marker is honoured, and the boot log says so.
1327:       - EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET=${EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET:-}

2241:   # ===========================================================================
2242:   # DEMO OVERLAY — BUILD SURFACE ONLY (KS-654)
2243:   # ===========================================================================
2244:   # This service exists so `docker compose build` compiles the overlay. It is
2245:   # not wired to anything at runtime and is not a dependency of any service.
2246:   #
2247:   # Why it is needed: frontend/demo-overlay had no lockfile and no Dockerfile,
2248:   # so nothing built it and no failure could surface. Its build was broken for
2249:   # 33 days (2026-07-15 -> 2026-08-17, KS-461's lucide icon name on an MUI
2250:   # import path) and was found only by accident. It is a root workspace member,
2251:   # so a broken build also breaks `npm run build --workspaces`. The image build
2252:   # is the red surface that was missing.
2253:   #
2254:   # Why it publishes nowhere: NOTHING SERVES THE OVERLAY. Verified 2026-08-17 —
2255:   # the gateway's `location /demo-overlay/` and sub_filter injection were
2256:   # removed in nginx-demo.conf:363-379 ("no functional value"), the URL 404s,
2257:   # and no .conf references the mount path. The bind-mount that used to be on
2258:   # nginx-gateway below was the misleading artefact: it made this look live.
2259:   # Revive-vs-retire is an open product decision on KS-654 — until it is taken,
2260:   # this builds and reports, and touches nothing.
2261:   demo-overlay:
2262:     labels:
2263:       <<: *stack_labels
2264:     build:
2265:       context: .
2266:       dockerfile: frontend/demo-overlay/Dockerfile
2267:       labels:
2268:         <<: *image_labels
2269:     container_name: ${STACK_PREFIX:-secuura}-demo-overlay
2270:     restart: "no"
2271:     networks:
2272:       - secuura-network
2273:     deploy:
2274:       resources:
2275:         limits:
2276:           cpus: '0.25'
2277:           memory: 64M
2278: 
2279:   # ===========================================================================
2280:   # NGINX GATEWAY - Unified local entry point (port 6882)
2281:   # ===========================================================================
2282: 
2283:   nginx-gateway:
2284:     labels:
2285:       <<: *stack_labels
2286:     build:
2287:       context: ./docker/nginx-gateway
2288:       dockerfile: Dockerfile
2289:       labels:
2290:         <<: *image_labels
2291:     container_name: ${STACK_PREFIX:-secuura}-nginx-gateway
2292:     restart: unless-stopped
2293:     ports:
2294:       - "${GATEWAY_PORT:-6882}:80"
2295:     volumes:
2296:       - ./docker/nginx-gateway/nginx-demo.conf:/etc/nginx/conf.d/default.conf:ro
2297:       # KS-654: the `./frontend/demo-overlay/dist:/usr/share/nginx/demo-overlay`
2298:       # bind-mount was REMOVED here on 2026-08-17. Nothing read it — the
2299:       # `location /demo-overlay/` and sub_filter blocks were deleted from
2300:       # nginx-demo.conf (see :363-379), /demo-overlay/demo-overlay.js returns
2301:       # 404, and no directive references the mount path. The mount was the
2302:       # misleading artefact: it made an unserved, untracked, 33-day-broken
2303:       # bundle look live. Do not re-add it without also restoring the location
2304:       # block — that is the open revive-vs-retire decision on KS-654.
2305:     depends_on:

### FILE AT HEAD: Blockchain/Dev/docs/ENVIRONMENT-VARIABLES.md
1: # Secuura Environment Variables
2: 
3: Comprehensive reference for all environment variables used across the Secuura platform.
4: 
5: **Last Updated:** 12 May 2026
6: 
7: ---
8: 
9: ## Configuration Files
10: 
11: | File | Purpose | Git Status |
12: |------|---------|------------|
13: | `env.example` | Core infrastructure template | Tracked |
14: | `.env.local.example` | Identity provider credentials template | Tracked |
15: | `.env` | Active core configuration (copy of env.example) | Gitignored |
16: | `.env.local` | Active identity credentials | Gitignored |
17: 
18: Services load `.env` via Docker Compose variable substitution and `.env.local` via `env_file` directives (auth, api-gateway, kyc).

55: 
56: ## Auth / JWT
57: 
58: JWT is **RS256-only** (KS-167/184/302 — HS256 fully dropped). There is no shared
59: `JWT_SECRET`. The auth service signs tokens with the private key; every other
60: service verifies with the public key and **fails closed** if `JWT_PUBLIC_KEY` is
61: missing. Local dev generates the keypair via `./scripts/bootstrap-env.sh`.
62: 
63: | Variable | Service(s) | Required | Default | Description |
64: |----------|-----------|----------|---------|-------------|
65: | `JWT_PRIVATE_KEY` | auth | **Yes** (auth) | — | RS256 PEM private key — auth signs tokens. Auth will not boot without it. |
66: | `JWT_PUBLIC_KEY` | All backend services | **Yes** | — | RS256 PEM public key — all services verify with it; verifiers fail closed if unset. |
67: | `JWT_KEY_ID` | auth, all services | **Yes** | — | `kid` for the active RS256 keypair (set in the JWT header / matched on verify). |
68: | `JWT_EXPIRES_IN` | auth | No | `1d` | JWT access token expiry |
69: | `JWT_REFRESH_EXPIRES_IN` | auth | No | `7d` | Refresh token expiry |
70: | `CSRF_SECRET` | api-gateway | Prod only | — | CSRF protection secret (required in production) |
71: | `CSRF_COOKIE_SECURE` | api-gateway | No | `false` | Set `true` for HTTPS-only CSRF cookies |
72: | `DEMO_MODE` | auth, anchoring, demo-service | No | `true` | Enable demo login and mock fallbacks |
73: | `ENABLE_TEST_TOKENS` | api-gateway, auth | No | `false` | Enable test token endpoints (dev only) |
74: | `GATEWAY_VOUCH_SECRET` | **api-gateway, originate ONLY** | Not required to boot, but **unset fails CLOSED** — see below | — | KS-1041 Step 2. Proves to originate that a request came through api-gateway, so identity/tenant/role headers are honoured only on vouched requests. |
75: | `EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET` | **originate, anchoring ONLY** | Not required to boot, but **unset fails CLOSED** — see below | — | KS-1717. HMAC key originate signs its own hops to anchoring with; anchoring honours `x-emitter-internal` (skip the flat-anchor provenance row) only when the signature verifies. Same value on both; under 32 characters counts as unset. |
76: 
77: ---
78: 
79: ### `GATEWAY_VOUCH_SECRET` — scope is part of the control (KS-1041 Step 2)
80: 
81: `originate` honours `x-user-role`, `x-tenant-id` and the rest of the trust family
82: as authoritative — `routes/metering.ts` promotes `x-user-role: connector`
83: straight into `req.user` without consulting a JWT. Through `api-gateway` that is
84: safe: the gateway strips client-supplied trust headers and re-sets them from a
85: verified JWT. On the direct path nothing established who set them, and
86: `originate:4000` is reachable by every peer on the compose network.
87: 
88: The gateway sets `x-gateway-vouch` on the requests it proxies **to originate, and
89: to no other service** (`VOUCH_RECIPIENTS` in `api-gateway/src/routes/proxy.ts`).
90: originate **drops** the trust family from any request without a matching value.
91: The vouch is a bearer credential: a service that received it could replay it to
92: `originate:4000` directly, so who receives it is as much the control as where it
93: is stored.
94: 
95: **Where it may live — this is load-bearing, not a preference:**
96: 
97: - Set it in **`.env`**. Compose interpolates it into the `environment:` blocks of
98:   **api-gateway and originate only**. Compose passes through nothing a service
99:   does not declare, so the blast radius is 2 containers.
100: - **Never `.env.local`.** Four services declare `env_file: .env.local`
101:   (api-gateway, auth, m365-integration, kyc). A value placed there reaches all
102:   four — measured, not assumed — handing the secret to two services with no
103:   business holding it. A lock is worth what its key's storage is worth.
104: - **Never `commonEnvVars`** in `deployment/azure/services.bicep`, for the same
105:   reason: that list goes to every service.
106: 
107: **Unset FAILS CLOSED** — Kam's ruling of 2026-10-13, verbatim: *"Fail closed,
108: deployed only after the secret is set"*. This REPLACES the previous "unset is valid
109: and fail-OPEN by design" wording, which described the behaviour before KS-1032: an
110: unconfigured box now honours NO gateway-set identity header.
111: 
112: ⚠ **Verify a deploy by TENANT, not by status code.** On an unset *or mismatched*
113: secret, Platform-S metering answers **200 reading the DEFAULT tenant's usage**, not
114: 401 — the gateway also attaches a connector Bearer that `authenticate()` accepts, and
115: the handler then scopes by `req.tenantId`, which the KS-458 block set to the default
116: because the header was stripped. Measured by the pull request 1546 gate (cells GA1,
117: GA2, GC5; verdict `34fb8ee6b4650ac6`). **401** happens only when the gateway's token
118: exchange failed, and `MULTI_TENANCY_ENABLED=true` with `NODE_ENV=production` gives
119: **400** on every request (`extractTenantContext`, "Missing tenant context"). A
120: mismatched secret is therefore indistinguishable from a missing one by status code, so
121: DEPLOY-BLOCKERS step (3) asserts `data.tenantId` equals Platform S's key tenant.
122: 
123: It is not silent: originate logs a WARNING at boot naming the
124: consequence (`[gateway-provenance] DISABLED — …honoured from ANY caller…`), and
125: an ACTIVE line when configured. Check that line to know which state a box is in.
126: 
127: Generate per environment with `openssl rand -hex 32`. The name belongs in the
128: templates; the value never does.
129: 
130: ### `EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET` — the signed originate -> anchoring hop (KS-1717)
131: 
132: `x-emitter-internal` tells anchoring that a `POST /api/anchors` is originate's own
133: hop for an operation that already wrote its action_provenance row, so anchoring
134: skips its flat-anchor `anchor` row (KS-566). originate's two setters forward the
135: CALLER'S OWN bearer, so the hop and a direct compose-network caller holding the
136: same connector JWT are indistinguishable by credential. anchoring therefore honours
137: the marker only when an HMAC-SHA256 originate made under this key verifies — over
138: the method, the path, the marker, a timestamp within **±60 s**, the **caller's
139: Authorization header** (hashed exactly as sent), and the **raw body bytes**
140: (`packages/shared/src/security/emitterSignature.ts`, used by both sides).
141: 
142: **What it does not do:** anyone holding the key can sign, and there is no nonce
143: store. A byte-identical replay under the **same bearer** verifies for just under 61 s
144: after signing when the two clocks agree, and for up to about 121 s if the signer's
145: clock runs up to 60 s fast. A replay with a different body, method, path, marker,
146: timestamp or bearer is refused.
147: 
148: **Where it may live:** `.env`, which compose interpolates into the `environment:`
149: blocks of **originate and anchoring only** — never `.env.local` and never
150: `commonEnvVars` in `services.bicep`, for the reasons given for the vouch above. The
151: same value on both services.
152: 
153: **Unset FAILS CLOSED**, the same shape as Kam's 2026-10-13 ruling for the vouch:
154: anchoring honours no marker and logs `[emitter-guard] FAIL-CLOSED …` at boot
155: (`[emitter-guard] ACTIVE …` when configured), and originate's own hops each write a
156: **second** action_provenance row until the secret is set on BOTH services. **Set it
157: on both before deploying the images that check it.** A marker anchoring refuses
158: while it IS configured is logged with its reason — a forgery, or a half-configured
159: deploy.
160: 
161: Generate per environment with `openssl rand -hex 32`.
162:

204: ## Services (General)
205: 
206: | Variable | Service(s) | Required | Default | Description |
207: |----------|-----------|----------|---------|-------------|
208: | `NODE_ENV` | All | No | `development` | Environment: `development`, `staging`, `production` |
209: | `LOG_LEVEL` | All backend services | No | `info` | Log level: `debug`, `info`, `warn`, `error` |
210: | `PORT` | Each service | Yes | Service-specific | Internal container port |
211: | `CORS_ORIGINS` | api-gateway, demo-service | No | `http://localhost:6100,...,http://localhost:6882` | Comma-separated allowed CORS origins |
212: | `RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_MS` | api-gateway | No | `60000` | Rate limit window in ms |
213: | `RATE_LIMIT_MAX_REQUESTS` | api-gateway | No | `2000` (dev, KS-206) / `60` (prod) | Max requests per window |
214: | `PLATFORM_S_CONNECTOR_RATE_LIMIT` | api-gateway | No | `3000` | Per-hour rate limit that `register-connector` mints on a new Platform S connector key (window fixed at 3600 s). KS-1195 (2026-10-09): *"Raise the default for S connector keys to 3,000 per hour, tunable by env; new keys only"* — existing keys keep 1,000/h until rotated. |
215: | `FRONTEND_URL` | auth | No | `http://localhost:6882` | Frontend URL for email links |
216: | `SERVICE_ENABLED` | guardian, queue | No | `false` | Enable/disable placeholder services |
217: | `DEFAULT_TENANT_ID` | api-gateway | No | `default` | Default multi-tenant ID |
218: | `ENABLE_PUBLIC_API_DOCS` | api-gateway | No | `false` | Expose Swagger UI at `/api/docs` + raw spec at `/api/docs/openapi.json` (audit F-04 — every endpoint, parameter, and auth mechanism becomes a public attack-surface map). Defaulted to `true` on `dev` via `services.bicep` so Schemathesis / spec-driven testing has a target; demo + prod stay `false`. |
219: | `ENABLE_MOCK_ENDPOINTS` | api-gateway | No | `false` | Wires the **mock wallet challenge/authenticate** routes (return forgeable `mock_jwt_*` tokens — never enable in production). As of PR #21 the admin + verification routers are *no longer gated* by this flag (they carry real CRUD: orgs, doc-types listing, workflows, notification settings); only the wallet mocks remain inside the gate. |
220: | `PLATFORM_UPSTREAM_TIMEOUT_MS` | api-gateway | No | `8000` | KS-5: hard deadline (ms) on `services/api-gateway/src/routes/platform.ts`'s `fetch()` calls to `tenant-provisioning` (and the best-effort originate `refresh-tenants` ping). On timeout the gateway returns **504 `GATEWAY_TIMEOUT`** (vs 502 `BAD_GATEWAY` for connection-refused / DNS) instead of hanging on the OS TCP timeout. The live demo env (`secuura02`) uses the code default. (A `5000` manual override existed on the OLD `secuura-dev-api` Container App, now decommissioning — see `5_Project_History/AZURE-MIGRATION-2026-06-17.md`.) |
221: | `PLATFORM_AUDIT_MAX_OFFSET` | api-gateway | No | `5000` | KS-5: clamp on the `offset` query param of `GET /api/platform/audit-log`. The handler overfetches `limit + offset` rows from both sources then sorts + per-row `JSON.parse`s in memory, so an unbounded `offset` made that the latency floor. Deep audit paging past this point clamps rather than crawling. |
222: 
223: ### Gateway → downstream service URLs (Azure / Container Apps)
224: 
225: The gateway's services map (`services/api-gateway/src/config/services.ts`) reads
226: each downstream service URL from a `<KEY>_SERVICE_URL` env var, falling back to
227: `http://localhost:<port>` for local dev. On Azure these MUST be set explicitly
228: to the internal FQDN or the proxy will 502.
229: 
230: | Variable | Service | Default | Internal FQDN pattern |
231: |----------|---------|---------|------------------------|
232: | `AUTH_SERVICE_URL` | auth | `http://localhost:6003` | `https://<prefix>-auth.internal.<envDomain>` |
233: | `ORIGINATE_SERVICE_URL` | originate | `http://localhost:6000` | `https://<prefix>-originate.internal.<envDomain>` |
234: | `WALLET_SERVICE_URL` | wallet-connector | `http://localhost:6002` | `https://<prefix>-wallet.internal.<envDomain>` |
235: | `PRISM_SERVICE_URL` | prism | `http://localhost:6001` | `https://<prefix>-prism.internal.<envDomain>` |
236: | `TIMESTAMPING_SERVICE_URL` | timestamping | `http://localhost:6004` | `https://<prefix>-timestamp.internal.<envDomain>` |
237: | `ANCHORING_SERVICE_URL` | anchoring | `http://localhost:6005` | `https://<prefix>-anchoring.internal.<envDomain>` |
238: | `SECURITY_SERVICE_URL` | security | `http://localhost:6008` | `https://<prefix>-security.internal.<envDomain>` |
239: | `ANALYTICS_SERVICE_URL` | analytics | `http://localhost:6010` | `https://<prefix>-analytics.internal.<envDomain>` |
240: | `BILLING_SERVICE_URL` | billing | `http://localhost:6009` | `https://<prefix>-billing.internal.<envDomain>` |
241: | `M365_SERVICE_URL` | m365-integration | `http://localhost:6013` | `https://<prefix>-m365.internal.<envDomain>` |
242: | `KYC_SERVICE_URL` | kyc | `http://localhost:6011` | `https://<prefix>-kyc.internal.<envDomain>` |
243: | `TOKENISATION_URL` | tokenisation | `http://localhost:6024` | `https://<prefix>-tokenisation.internal.<envDomain>` |
244: | `TENANT_PROVISIONING_URL` | tenant-provisioning | `http://localhost:6028` | `https://<prefix>-tenant-prov.internal.<envDomain>` |
245: 
246: `KYC_SERVICE_URL` was missing from `services.bicep` before PR #21 follow-up and added in 2026-05-11. Without it, `/api/kyc/*` all 502.

299: ## Email / SMTP
300:

435: 
436: ## Production Security Notes
437: 
438: 1. **JWT keypair (RS256)** — generate with `./scripts/bootstrap-env.sh` (or sync the keypair into Key Vault for Azure). `JWT_PUBLIC_KEY` must be present on every service (verifiers fail closed without it); `JWT_PRIVATE_KEY` lives only on the auth service. There is no `JWT_SECRET` (HS256 dropped, KS-302).
439: 2. **CSRF_SECRET** must be set in production: `openssl rand -hex 32`
440: 3. **POSTGRES_PASSWORD** and **REDIS_PASSWORD** must be strong and unique
441: 4. **BLOCKFROST_API_KEY** and **PLATFORM_WALLET_MNEMONIC** must never be committed
442: 5. All secrets should be managed via a secrets manager (AWS Secrets Manager, Azure Key Vault)
443: 6. **DEMO_MODE** and **ENABLE_TEST_TOKENS** must be `false` in production
444: 7. **CORS_ORIGINS** must be restricted to known production domains

### FILE AT HEAD: Blockchain/Dev/env.example
1: # =============================================================================
2: # SECUURA LOCAL DEVELOPMENT ENVIRONMENT CONFIGURATION
3: # =============================================================================
4: # Copy this file to .env.local:  cp env.example .env.local
5: # This template has working defaults for local development
6: # =============================================================================
7: 
8: # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
9: # DATABASE
10: # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
11: POSTGRES_USER=secuura
12: # KS-731: intentionally BLANK. scripts/bootstrap-env.sh generates a per-clone
13: # value here, and it only generates into an EMPTY value — shipping a literal
14: # would make the generator a no-op and leave every clone, worktree and slot
15: # sharing one password again, which is the whole defect. If you copy this file
16: # by hand instead of running bootstrap, compose falls back to its own literal
17: # (KS-762) and the stack still starts.
18: POSTGRES_PASSWORD=
19: POSTGRES_DB=secuura
20: DATABASE_URL=postgresql://secuura:@localhost:6432/secuura   # bootstrap-env.sh rebuilds this with the generated password (KS-731)
21: 
22: # The password for the least-privilege application role (`secuura_app`) that
23: # every service's DATABASE_URL is built from, and that the init script
24: # docker/init/01-create-app-role.sh provisions on a fresh volume.
25: #
26: # KS-762 — SET THIS. It was never documented here, which is how it came to be
27: # relied on as a committed literal instead: `docker-compose.yml` carries a
28: # `${APP_DB_PASSWORD:-<literal>}` fallback at 51 sites (plus one in
29: # scripts/rls/verify-fail-closed.sh — 52 in total), so a clone with no value set
30: # silently runs on a password that is in git history. Measured 2026-09-03: that
31: # literal is a WORKING credential on a local stack, not a placeholder — `psql`
32: # as secuura_app with it returns AUTH OK, and a deliberately wrong password
33: # returns FATAL, so the probe discriminates.
34: #
35: # The fallbacks have NOT been removed. Turning them into `${APP_DB_PASSWORD:?}`
36: # is the obvious fix and is deliberately HELD: docker-compose.yml:121-132
37: # carries a KS-165 instruction not to, because a required variable stops every
38: # existing local stack and a fresh clone until its .env gains this line. Both
39: # positions are right and the trade is a decision with a team cost, so it sits
40: # with Kam rather than being taken quietly. Until it is ruled, setting this
41: # value is what takes YOUR stack off the committed one.
42: #
43: # ⚠ ON AN EXISTING STACK, SETTING A NEW VALUE HERE BREAKS AUTH. The secuura_app
44: # role password is baked into the Postgres volume by 01-create-app-role.sh at
45: # FIRST INIT and is not re-read afterwards, so changing this line alone leaves
46: # the services presenting a password the database does not have. To move off the
47: # committed value on a stack you already have: either recreate the volume, or
48: # ALTER the role password to match. On a fresh clone, just set it.
49: #
50: # ⚠ Removing the default would not un-commit the literal — it is in history from
51: # 1305bbda9. ROTATION is the half that actually retires it, scheduled
52: # 2026-09-05 09:00 (KS-762). After that rotation this value changes and a fresh
53: # volume is required for it to take effect on an existing stack.
54: #
55: # Verify where your stack stands at any time:
56: #     Blockchain/Dev/scripts/check-app-db-password-default.sh
57: APP_DB_USER=secuura_app
58: APP_DB_PASSWORD=change-me-before-first-run
59: 
60: # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
61: # REDIS (REQUIRED - password required for production)
62: # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
63: REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379
64: # KS-731: intentionally BLANK. scripts/bootstrap-env.sh generates a per-clone
65: # value here, and it only generates into an EMPTY value — shipping a literal
66: # would make the generator a no-op and leave every clone, worktree and slot
67: # sharing one password again, which is the whole defect. If you copy this file
68: # by hand instead of running bootstrap, compose falls back to its own literal
69: # (KS-762) and the stack still starts.
70: REDIS_PASSWORD=

332: 
333: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
334: # Cardano platform wallet (KS-535, 2026-07-30) — READ THIS BEFORE SETTING IT
335: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
336: # LEAVE THIS EMPTY on a local/dev stack unless you have your OWN funded preview
337: # wallet. Never copy the demo VM's mnemonic into a local .env.
338: #
339: # Why: a Cardano transaction consumes specific UTxOs. Two environments sharing
340: # one wallet select the same UTxOs and race — the loser's transaction references
341: # an already-spent input and fails with
342: #   ConwayMempoolFailure "All inputs are spent. Transaction has probably
343: #   already been included"
344: # That is exactly what happened: local and demo ran the same mnemonic and
345: # accumulated 295 (local) and 2 (demo) failures of that kind. The anchoring
346: # service's withWalletLock only serialises submissions WITHIN one process; it
347: # cannot coordinate across separate deployments, and nothing else does.
348: #
349: # Empty => isCardanoReady() is false => anchoring uses dev mock mode. That is
350: # safe and honest since KS-520/KS-522: a simulated or mock anchor is never

419: # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
420: # DEMO SERVICE — inbound auth + non-prod gate (KS-641)
421: # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
422: # demo-service mints REAL platform tokens without asking the caller for a
423: # credential: POST /demo-api/persona/switch authenticates server-side as one of
424: # four seeded personas — including admin@secuura.io, role Platform Admin — and
425: # proxies the httpOnly refresh cookie back. POST /demo-api/reset runs a
426: # pg_restore plus a Redis FLUSHALL.
427: #
428: # It therefore refuses to start unless BOTH are set, and refuses to start in
429: # production regardless of what they say:
430: #   DEMO_SERVICE_ENABLED  must be exactly "true" — a deliberate opt-in.
431: #   DEMO_SERVICE_KEY      shared key, >= 16 chars. Every /demo-api route except
432: #                         /health requires it as the X-Demo-Key header.
433: #
434: # The key is meant to be held by the GATEWAY and injected server-side, not by a
435: # browser — the only caller is the demo overlay, which has no credential of its
436: # own, and a secret baked into a public demo bundle is not a secret. Nothing
437: # routes /demo-api/ today (the location block was removed under F-09), so if you
438: # restore that block you must also add:
439: #     proxy_set_header X-Demo-Key <the value below>;
440: # Change this value on any deployed environment.
441: DEMO_SERVICE_ENABLED=true
442: DEMO_SERVICE_KEY=local-dev-demo-key-change-me
443: 
444: # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
445: # GATEWAY_VOUCH_SECRET — KS-1041 Step 2 (gateway provenance)
446: # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
447: # Shared secret proving to `originate` that a request arrived through
448: # `api-gateway`. originate honours identity/tenant/role headers only on a
449: # request carrying a matching `x-gateway-vouch`; on anything else it DROPS
450: # those headers. It does not refuse the request — two services call originate
451: # directly by design (m365-integration's Outlook verify-hash, nft-certificate's
452: # minting fetch) and neither sends a trust header, so stripping closes the hole
453: # without breaking them.
454: #
455: # WHERE IT MAY LIVE, and this part is load-bearing:
456: #   - set it HERE, in `.env`, which docker compose interpolates into the
457: #     `environment:` blocks of api-gateway and originate ONLY.
458: #   - do NOT put it in `.env.local`. Four services declare
459: #     `env_file: .env.local` (api-gateway, auth, m365-integration, kyc), so a
460: #     value placed there reaches all four — measured, not assumed. That hands
461: #     the secret to two services with no business holding it, and a lock is
462: #     worth what its key's storage is worth.
463: #
464: # UNSET FAILS CLOSED (Kam's ruling, 2026-10-13: "Fail closed, deployed only after
465: # the secret is set"). This REPLACES the previous "fail-OPEN by design" wording,
466: # which described the behaviour before KS-1032. An unconfigured box honours NO
467: # gateway-set identity header. It is not silent — originate logs a WARNING at boot
468: # naming the consequence, and the gateway simply omits the header.
469: #
470: # ⚠ SET THIS BEFORE DEPLOYING, and verify by TENANT, not by status code. On an unset
471: # or MISMATCHED secret, Platform-S metering answers 200 reading the DEFAULT tenant's
472: # usage — not 401 — because the gateway also attaches a connector Bearer that
473: # authenticate() accepts (pull request 1546 gate, cells GA1/GA2/GC5). 401 occurs only
474: # when the token exchange failed; MULTI_TENANCY_ENABLED=true with NODE_ENV=production
475: # gives 400 on every request. Assert data.tenantId == Platform S's key tenant.
476: # Generate one with:
477: #     openssl rand -hex 32
478: GATEWAY_VOUCH_SECRET=
479: 
480: # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
481: # EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET — KS-1717 (the signed originate -> anchoring hop)
482: # -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
483: # HMAC key originate signs its own hops to anchoring with. `x-emitter-internal`
484: # tells anchoring to skip the flat-anchor `anchor` provenance row, because the
485: # operation already wrote its own. anchoring honours it ONLY when the signature
486: # (method, path, marker, a timestamp within 60 s, the caller's Authorization
487: # header, and the raw body bytes) verifies under this key. Without that check,
488: # a caller reaching anchoring directly on the compose network with a connector
489: # JWT could set the header and suppress its own provenance row.
490: #
491: # WHAT IT DOES NOT DO: anyone holding the key can sign, and there is no nonce
492: # store. A byte-identical replay under the SAME bearer verifies for just under
493: # 61 s after signing when the two clocks agree, and for up to about 121 s if the
494: # signer's clock runs up to 60 s fast. A replay with a different body, method,
495: # path, marker, timestamp or bearer is refused.
496: #
497: # WHERE IT MAY LIVE: here, in `.env`, which compose interpolates into the
498: # `environment:` blocks of originate and anchoring ONLY. NOT in `.env.local`
499: # (four services load that file). The SAME value on both services, and at least
500: # 32 characters — anything shorter is treated as unset.
501: #
502: # UNSET FAILS CLOSED. anchoring honours no marker and logs a WARNING at boot, so
503: # originate's own hops each write a SECOND action_provenance row (an `anchor` row
504: # beside the operation's own) until the secret is set on BOTH services. Set it on
505: # both before deploying the images that check it.
506: # Generate one with:
507: #     openssl rand -hex 32
508: EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET=

### FILE AT HEAD: Blockchain/Dev/packages/shared/src/__tests__/ks1717-emitter-hop-signature.test.ts
1: /**
2:  * =============================================================================
3:  * KS-1717 — the ONE signing format for originate's own hop to anchoring
4:  * =============================================================================
5:  * These cells pin the helper both services share (`security/emitterSignature.ts`).
6:  * originate's cells prove its two setters SEND what this helper signs; anchoring's
7:  * cells prove the real route HONOURS only what it verifies. This file is the contract
8:  * between them, so every way a signed hop can be altered after signing is a cell here,
9:  * and each one is expected to be REFUSED.
10:  *
11:  * The canonicalisation question (Wednesday's ANSWER, 2026-10-26: "reordered JSON keys
12:  * must be stated as either canonicalised or rejected") is answered as REJECTED: the
13:  * signature covers the raw body bytes, so S4 and S5 are refusals by design.
14:  */
15: import { describe, it, expect } from 'vitest';
16: import crypto from 'crypto';
17: import {
18:   EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER,
19:   EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER,
20:   EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER,
21:   EMITTER_SIGNATURE_WINDOW_SECONDS,
22:   EMITTER_SECRET_MIN_LENGTH,
23:   isUsableEmitterSecret,
24:   emitterSigningString,
25:   signEmitterHop,
26:   verifyEmitterHop,
27: } from '../index';
28: 
29: const SECRET = 'ks1717-test-signing-secret-0123456789abcdef';
30: const NOW_MS = 1_800_000_000_000;
31: const URL_ = 'http://anchoring:4005/api/anchors';
32: const PATH = '/api/anchors';
33: const AUTH = 'Bearer connector-a.header.payload.sig';
34: const BODY = JSON.stringify({ documentId: 'doc-ks1717', contentHash: 'a'.repeat(64), network: 'preview' });
35: 
36: function signed(over: Partial<{ secret: string; marker: string; method: string; url: string; authorization: string | undefined; body: string; nowMs: number }> = {}) {
37:   return signEmitterHop({ secret: SECRET, marker: 'originate-create', method: 'POST', url: URL_, authorization: AUTH, body: BODY, nowMs: NOW_MS, ...over });
38: }
39: 
40: /** The receiving side: `headers` is what arrived, so the caller's bearer defaults to AUTH as sent. */
41: function verify(headers: Record<string, string | string[] | undefined>, over: Partial<{ secret: string | undefined; method: string; path: string; body: Buffer | string | undefined; nowMs: number }> = {}) {
42:   return verifyEmitterHop({ secret: SECRET, method: 'POST', path: PATH, headers: { authorization: AUTH, ...headers }, body: BODY, nowMs: NOW_MS, ...over });
43: }
44: 
45: /** A signature over arbitrary fields with an arbitrary key — for the cells that forge one. */
46: function macOver(secret: string, fields: { method: string; path: string; timestamp: string; marker: string; authorization: string; body: string }): string {
47:   return crypto.createHmac('sha256', secret).update(emitterSigningString(fields), 'utf8').digest('hex');
48: }
49: 
50: describe('KS-1717 S1 — a genuine hop verifies', () => {
51:   it('what signEmitterHop produces, verifyEmitterHop accepts, and it returns the marker', () => {
52:     expect(verify(signed())).toEqual({ ok: true, marker: 'originate-create' });
53:   });
54: 
55:   it('the signer emits exactly the three headers, all under x-emitter- (the gateway edge strip covers that prefix)', () => {
56:     const h = signed();
57:     expect(Object.keys(h).sort()).toEqual([EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER, EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER, EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER].sort());
58:     for (const name of Object.keys(h)) expect(name).toMatch(/^x-emitter-/);
59:     expect(h[EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER]).toBe(String(NOW_MS / 1000));
60:     expect(h[EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER]).toMatch(/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/);
61:   });
62: 
63:   it('the body is compared as BYTES: a Buffer of the same UTF-8 verifies, including non-ASCII', () => {
64:     const body = JSON.stringify({ documentId: 'doc-ks1717', issuerName: 'Société Générale — 東京' });
65:     const h = signed({ body });
66:     expect(verify(h, { body: Buffer.from(body, 'utf8') }).ok).toBe(true);
67:   });
68: });
69: 
70: describe('KS-1717 S2 — the signing string is pinned literally', () => {
71:   it('label, method, path, timestamp, marker, sha256(authorization), sha256(body) — newline-joined, in that order', () => {
72:     const s = emitterSigningString({ method: 'POST', path: PATH, timestamp: '1800000000', marker: 'originate-create', authorization: AUTH, body: BODY });
73:     const authHash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(AUTH).digest('hex');
74:     const bodyHash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(BODY).digest('hex');
75:     expect(s).toBe(['secuura-emitter-v1', 'POST', '/api/anchors', '1800000000', 'originate-create', authHash, bodyHash].join('\n'));
76:   });
77: });
78: 
79: describe('KS-1717 S3 — every field altered after signing is refused', () => {
80:   const h = signed();
81: 
82:   it('S3a a different body (the replay-with-another-document case)', () => {
83:     const other = JSON.stringify({ documentId: 'doc-someone-elses', contentHash: 'b'.repeat(64), network: 'preview' });
84:     expect(verify(h, { body: other })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
85:   });
86: 
87:   it('S3b the SAME object with its keys reordered — REJECTED, not canonicalised', () => {
88:     const reordered = JSON.stringify({ network: 'preview', contentHash: 'a'.repeat(64), documentId: 'doc-ks1717' });
89:     expect(JSON.parse(reordered)).toEqual(JSON.parse(BODY)); // same value...
90:     expect(verify(h, { body: reordered })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' }); // ...different bytes
91:   });
92: 
93:   it('S3c the same object with whitespace added — REJECTED', () => {
94:     expect(verify(h, { body: JSON.stringify(JSON.parse(BODY), null, 2) })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
95:   });
96: 
97:   it('S3d another method', () => {
98:     expect(verify(h, { method: 'PUT' })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
99:   });
100: 
101:   it('S3e another path, and the same path with a query string', () => {
102:     expect(verify(h, { path: '/api/anchors/batch' })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
103:     expect(verify(h, { path: '/api/anchors?x=1' })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
104:   });
105: 
106:   it('S3f another marker value', () => {
107:     expect(verify({ ...h, [EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER]: 'originate-lifecycle' })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
108:   });
109: 
110:   it('S3g the timestamp header moved, even inside the window', () => {
111:     expect(verify({ ...h, [EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER]: String(NOW_MS / 1000 + 1) })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
112:   });
113: 
114:   it('S3h the same timestamp with a leading zero — the header string is signed verbatim', () => {
115:     expect(verify({ ...h, [EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER]: `0${h[EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER]}` })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
116:   });
117: 
118:   it('S3i another secret', () => {
119:     expect(verify(h, { secret: `${SECRET}-rotated` })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
120:   });
121: 
122:   it('S3j raw bytes NOT captured on the receiving side fail, rather than verifying an empty body', () => {
123:     expect(verify(h, { body: undefined })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' });
124:   });
125: });

127: describe(`KS-1717 S4 — the window is ±${EMITTER_SIGNATURE_WINDOW_SECONDS} s, inclusive`, () => {
128:   const h = signed();
129:   const W = EMITTER_SIGNATURE_WINDOW_SECONDS * 1000;
130: 
131:   it('accepted at exactly +60 s and -60 s', () => {
132:     expect(verify(h, { nowMs: NOW_MS + W }).ok).toBe(true);
133:     expect(verify(h, { nowMs: NOW_MS - W }).ok).toBe(true);
134:   });
135: 
136:   it('an EXPIRED timestamp (61 s old) is refused', () => {
137:     expect(verify(h, { nowMs: NOW_MS + W + 1000 })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'stale-timestamp' });
138:   });
139: 
140:   it('a timestamp 61 s in the FUTURE is refused', () => {
141:     expect(verify(h, { nowMs: NOW_MS - W - 1000 })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'stale-timestamp' });
142:   });
143: });
