145: describe('KS-1717 S5 — fail-closed: no usable secret, nothing verifies', () => {
146:   it(`a secret under ${EMITTER_SECRET_MIN_LENGTH} chars is "unset", including the template placeholder`, () => {
147:     expect(isUsableEmitterSecret('x'.repeat(EMITTER_SECRET_MIN_LENGTH))).toBe(true);
148:     expect(isUsableEmitterSecret('x'.repeat(EMITTER_SECRET_MIN_LENGTH - 1))).toBe(false);
149:     expect(isUsableEmitterSecret('placeholder-never-referenced')).toBe(false);
150:     expect(isUsableEmitterSecret('')).toBe(false);

152:   });

174:   });

179:   });

184: 
185:   it('no marker, an empty marker, or a repeated (array) marker', () => {

189:     expect(verify({ ...h, [EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER]: ['originate-create', 'originate-create'] })).toEqual({ ok: false, reason: 'no-marker' });
190:   });

194:   });

213:     }
214:   });
215: });

### FILE AT HEAD: Blockchain/Dev/packages/shared/src/security/emitterSignature.ts
1: /**
2:  * =============================================================================
3:  * KS-1717 — the SIGNED originate -> anchoring hop (Kam's ruling (b), 2026-10-26)
4:  * =============================================================================
5:  * `x-emitter-internal` tells anchoring "this is K's own originate -> anchoring hop for
6:  * an operation that ALREADY wrote its own action_provenance row", and anchoring then
7:  * skips its flat-anchor `anchor` row (KS-566). Before this module anchoring honoured the
8:  * marker by PRESENCE, and both of originate's setters forward the CALLER'S OWN bearer
9:  * (`routes/documents.ts`, `originate-create` / `originate-lifecycle`) — so originate's
10:  * hop and a direct compose-network caller holding the same connector JWT were
11:  * indistinguishable, and the caller could suppress its own provenance row.
12:  *
13:  * This is the ONE implementation of the proof, used by BOTH sides: originate signs with
14:  * `signEmitterHop`, anchoring checks with `verifyEmitterHop`. Two copies of a signing
15:  * string are two chances to disagree, so neither service carries its own.
16:  *
17:  * WHAT IS SIGNED — the RAW BODY BYTES, never a re-serialised object. originate signs the
18:  * exact string it sends; anchoring hashes the exact bytes it received (captured by
19:  * `express.json`'s `verify` hook, before parsing). There is therefore no canonicalisation
20:  * step to get wrong: a body re-serialised in transit — reordered keys, other whitespace —
21:  * is a DIFFERENT body and is REFUSED. Refusal is the safe direction (see FAIL-CLOSED).
22:  *
23:  * AND THE CALLER'S BEARER (PR #1690 gate round 1, Minor 2). originate forwards the
24:  * caller's own Authorization header on the hop, and anchoring attributes the provenance
25:  * row to whoever that bearer names. Without it in the MAC, a hop captured from one
26:  * connector replayed under ANOTHER connector's token, inside the window, verified. The
27:  * header value is hashed EXACTLY as sent — no case-folding, no trimming — so any other
28:  * value, including none, is a different bearer and is refused.
29:  *
30:  *   secuura-emitter-v1 \n <method> \n <path> \n <timestamp> \n <marker>
31:  *     \n <sha256-hex(authorization, or "" when absent)> \n <sha256-hex(body)>
32:  *
33:  * No field can contain a newline (HTTP forbids it in a method, a request-target and a
34:  * header value, and the two hashes are hex), so the join is unambiguous. The timestamp
35:  * and marker enter as the header STRINGS, never as re-formatted values. The leading
36:  * label separates this key's signatures from any other use of HMAC-SHA256 and versions
37:  * the format.
38:  *
39:  * THE WINDOW — ±60 s, inclusive, on whole seconds. originate signs at send time, compose
40:  * services share one host clock, and Container Apps hosts are NTP-synced, so a genuine
41:  * hop arrives with a skew of milliseconds. 60 s is generous for that and short for a
42:  * replay. Because both sides floor to the second, a hop stays acceptable for just under
43:  * 61 s after signing when the clocks agree — and for up to about 121 s if the signer's
44:  * clock runs up to 60 s fast, since a timestamp up to 60 s in the future is accepted too.
45:  *
46:  * RESIDUAL, stated so "signed" is not read as "complete": there is no nonce store. A
47:  * byte-identical replay under the SAME bearer verifies inside that window. What it buys
48:  * a caller who captured the hop is an exact retry, by the same connector, of an anchor
49:  * originate already attributed, with no second `anchor` row. A replay with a DIFFERENT
50:  * body, method, path, marker, timestamp or bearer is refused. Closing the same-bearer,
51:  * same-body case needs a nonce store on the verify path.
52:  *
53:  * FAIL-CLOSED. A secret shorter than EMITTER_SECRET_MIN_LENGTH is treated as unset, and
54:  * unset means nothing verifies — anchoring records the provenance row, so K's own hops
55:  * write a second row (the pre-KS-566 duplication) until the secret is set on BOTH
56:  * services. A duplicate row is visible and recoverable; a suppressed one is neither.
57:  * Same shape as Kam's KS-1032 ruling for the sibling vouch: "Fail closed, deployed only
58:  * after the secret is set". The length floor also means a mis-wired template placeholder
59:  * (`placeholder-never-referenced`, 28 chars) can never become a working key.
60:  *
61:  * WHY THE HEADERS ARE NAMED `x-emitter-*`. api-gateway's edge strip removes every
62:  * client-supplied `x-emitter-*` header on `/api/*` (`TRUST_HEADER_PATTERN`), so the
63:  * timestamp and signature inherit that strip with no gateway change. It is not what
64:  * makes them safe — the secret is — but it keeps them off the gateway path entirely.
65:  */
66: import crypto from 'crypto';
67: 
68: export const EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER = 'x-emitter-internal';
69: export const EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER = 'x-emitter-timestamp';
70: export const EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER = 'x-emitter-signature';
71: 
72: /** Accepted clock skew either side of the verifier's now, in seconds (inclusive). */
73: export const EMITTER_SIGNATURE_WINDOW_SECONDS = 60;
74: 
75: /** Below this length a configured secret is treated as unset (fail-closed). */
76: export const EMITTER_SECRET_MIN_LENGTH = 32;
77: 
78: const SIGNING_LABEL = 'secuura-emitter-v1';
79: const TIMESTAMP_FORM = /^[0-9]{1,12}$/;
80: const SIGNATURE_FORM = /^[0-9a-f]{64}$/;
81: 
82: /** Why a marked request was not honoured. Logged by the verifier's caller; never the values. */
83: export type EmitterHopRefusal =
84:   | 'no-marker'
85:   | 'no-secret'
86:   | 'missing-timestamp'
87:   | 'malformed-timestamp'
88:   | 'stale-timestamp'
89:   | 'missing-signature'
90:   | 'malformed-signature'
91:   | 'bad-signature';
92: 
93: export type EmitterHopVerdict = { ok: true; marker: string } | { ok: false; reason: EmitterHopRefusal };
94: 
95: /** True when `secret` is long enough to sign with. Anything else is "unset". */
96: export function isUsableEmitterSecret(secret: unknown): secret is string {
97:   return typeof secret === 'string' && secret.length >= EMITTER_SECRET_MIN_LENGTH;
98: }
99: 
100: /** The exact string both sides MAC. Exported so a test can pin the format itself. */
101: export function emitterSigningString(fields: {
102:   method: string;
103:   path: string;
104:   timestamp: string;
105:   marker: string;
106:   authorization: string | undefined;
107:   body: Buffer | string;
108: }): string {
109:   const authHash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(fields.authorization ?? '', 'utf8').digest('hex');
110:   const bodyHash = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(fields.body).digest('hex');
111:   return [SIGNING_LABEL, fields.method, fields.path, fields.timestamp, fields.marker, authHash, bodyHash].join('\n');
112: }
113: 
114: function mac(secret: string, signingString: string): string {
115:   return crypto.createHmac('sha256', secret).update(signingString, 'utf8').digest('hex');
116: }
117: 
118: /**
119:  * originate's side: the headers that mark ONE outbound hop. Always carries the marker;
120:  * adds the timestamp and signature only with a usable secret. An unsigned marker is still
121:  * sent so a half-configured deploy (anchoring keyed, originate not) shows up in anchoring's
122:  * refusal log instead of silently doubling rows. `body` must be the exact string sent,
123:  * and `authorization` the exact Authorization header value sent (undefined when none is):
124:  * it is required, so a setter cannot forget to bind the bearer.
125:  */
126: export function signEmitterHop(params: {
127:   secret: string | undefined;
128:   marker: string;
129:   method: string;
130:   url: string;
131:   authorization: string | undefined;
132:   body: string;
133:   nowMs?: number;
134: }): Record<string, string> {
135:   const headers: Record<string, string> = { [EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER]: params.marker };
136:   if (!isUsableEmitterSecret(params.secret)) return headers;
137:   const target = new URL(params.url);
138:   const timestamp = String(Math.floor((params.nowMs ?? Date.now()) / 1000));
139:   headers[EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER] = timestamp;
140:   headers[EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER] = mac(
141:     params.secret,
142:     emitterSigningString({
143:       method: params.method,
144:       path: target.pathname + target.search,
145:       timestamp,
146:       marker: params.marker,
147:       authorization: params.authorization,
148:       body: params.body,
149:     }),
150:   );
151:   return headers;
152: }
153: 
154: /**
155:  * anchoring's side: does this request carry a marker originate signed? `path` is the
156:  * request-target as received (express `req.originalUrl`); `body` is the raw bytes, or
157:  * undefined when none were captured — which then hashes as empty and fails unless the
158:  * signer also signed an empty body. The caller's bearer is read from `headers.authorization`
159:  * (a non-string value counts as absent).
160:  */
161: export function verifyEmitterHop(params: {
162:   secret: string | undefined;
163:   method: string;
164:   path: string;
165:   headers: Record<string, string | string[] | undefined>;
166:   body: Buffer | string | undefined;
167:   nowMs?: number;
168: }): EmitterHopVerdict {
169:   const marker = params.headers[EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER];
170:   if (typeof marker !== 'string' || marker.length === 0) return { ok: false, reason: 'no-marker' };
171:   if (!isUsableEmitterSecret(params.secret)) return { ok: false, reason: 'no-secret' };
172: 
173:   const timestamp = params.headers[EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER];
174:   if (timestamp === undefined) return { ok: false, reason: 'missing-timestamp' };
175:   if (typeof timestamp !== 'string' || !TIMESTAMP_FORM.test(timestamp)) {
176:     return { ok: false, reason: 'malformed-timestamp' };
177:   }
178:   const nowSeconds = Math.floor((params.nowMs ?? Date.now()) / 1000);
179:   if (Math.abs(nowSeconds - Number(timestamp)) > EMITTER_SIGNATURE_WINDOW_SECONDS) {
180:     return { ok: false, reason: 'stale-timestamp' };
181:   }
182: 
183:   const signature = params.headers[EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER];
184:   if (signature === undefined) return { ok: false, reason: 'missing-signature' };
185:   if (typeof signature !== 'string' || !SIGNATURE_FORM.test(signature)) {
186:     return { ok: false, reason: 'malformed-signature' };
187:   }
188: 
189:   const authorization = typeof params.headers.authorization === 'string' ? params.headers.authorization : undefined;
190:   const expected = mac(
191:     params.secret,
192:     emitterSigningString({ method: params.method, path: params.path, timestamp, marker, authorization, body: params.body ?? '' }),
193:   );
194:   // Both are 64 hex chars by now, so the buffers are equal length and timingSafeEqual cannot throw.
195:   if (!crypto.timingSafeEqual(Buffer.from(signature, 'hex'), Buffer.from(expected, 'hex'))) {
196:     return { ok: false, reason: 'bad-signature' };
197:   }
198:   return { ok: true, marker };
199: }

### FILE AT HEAD: Blockchain/Dev/services/anchoring/src/__tests__/ks1717-the-emitter-marker-is-honoured-only-when-signed.test.ts
1: /**
2:  * =============================================================================
3:  * KS-1717 — POST /api/anchors honours `x-emitter-internal` only on a hop originate SIGNED
4:  * =============================================================================
5:  * THE DEFECT. The marker switches off the flat-anchor `anchor` provenance row (KS-566).
6:  * anchoring honoured it by PRESENCE and had no inbound strip of its own, so a caller on
7:  * the compose network holding a connector JWT could set it and suppress its own row
8:  * (the #995 gate's note N1). originate's two setters forward the caller's OWN bearer, so
9:  * no credential separates the genuine hop from that caller — only a signature can.
10:  *
11:  * WHY THIS FILE BOOTS THE REAL APP. The defect is in what the ROUTE does with a header,
12:  * after the whole middleware chain has run. A cell that called the guard directly would
13:  * pass with the guard unmounted. Every cell here therefore drives `POST /api/anchors` on
14:  * the real `index.ts` app, with a real RS256 bearer, and observes the one effect that
15:  * matters: whether `recordFlatAnchorProvenance` was called.
16:  *

18:  * the import; the app is served on an ephemeral port). No database is connected —
19:  * `dbPersistAnchor` returns before any SQL when `isDbAvailable()` is false — so this runs
20:  * in the unit lane, and the provenance recorder is the only thing mocked.
21:  *
22:  * ONE TOKEN PER CELL. The signature covers the caller's Authorization header (gate round 1,
23:  * Minor 2), and `token()` mints a new string whenever its `iat` second changes. So each cell
24:  * mints its bearer ONCE and uses that same string to sign and to send.
25:  *
26:  * WHICH CELLS ARE RED ON DEVELOP (presence-honouring code): R1, R3, R4, R5, R8, R9, R10.
27:  * R9 (a hop replayed under ANOTHER connector's bearer) is also red on the round-1 head,
28:  * where the signature did not cover the bearer. R10 (a hop MAC'd under a 31-character
29:  * secret) is red if the 32-character floor is removed (gate round 1, Note 4).
30:  * WHICH ARE CONTROLS (green before and after): R2 — the genuine signed hop is still
31:  * skipped, which a plain strip would break, and which also proves the guard is REACHED
32:  * (an unmounted guard records here); R6 and R7 — the unmarked paths are unchanged; R11 —
33:  * exactly 32 characters is enough.
34:  * =============================================================================
35:  */

47: } from '@secuura/shared';
48: 
49: vi.mock('../provenance', () => ({ recordFlatAnchorProvenance: vi.fn(async () => undefined) }));
50: 
51: const SECRET = 'ks1717-anchoring-test-secret-0123456789abcdef';
52: const TENANT = 'a0000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000001';
53: const ORG = '1a171700-0000-4000-8000-0000000017a1';
54: const CONNECTOR = 'connector:platform-s:ks1717';
55: const CONNECTOR_B = 'connector:platform-s:ks1717-other-key';
56: 
57: const { privateKey, publicKey } = crypto.generateKeyPairSync('rsa', { modulusLength: 2048 });
58: const PRIVATE_PEM = privateKey.export({ type: 'pkcs8', format: 'pem' }).toString();
59: const PUBLIC_PEM = publicKey.export({ type: 'spki', format: 'pem' }).toString();
60: const b64url = (b: Buffer): string => b.toString('base64').replace(/\+/g, '-').replace(/\//g, '_').replace(/=+$/, '');
61: function token(claims: Record<string, unknown>): string {
62:   const header = b64url(Buffer.from(JSON.stringify({ alg: 'RS256', typ: 'JWT' })));
63:   const now = Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000);
64:   const payload = b64url(Buffer.from(JSON.stringify({ iat: now, exp: now + 600, ...claims })));
65:   return `${header}.${payload}.${b64url(crypto.sign('RSA-SHA256', Buffer.from(`${header}.${payload}`), PRIVATE_PEM))}`;
66: }
67: /** The caller this ticket is about: a connector key, the same JWT originate would forward. */
68: const CONNECTOR_TOKEN = () => token({ userId: CONNECTOR, role: 'connector', organizationId: ORG, tenantId: TENANT });
69: /** Another connector key in the SAME tenant — the replayer the round-1 gate measured. */
70: const CONNECTOR_B_TOKEN = () => token({ userId: CONNECTOR_B, role: 'connector', organizationId: ORG, tenantId: TENANT });
71: /** An interactive caller: writes no flat-anchor row whatever the marker says (KS-566). */
72: const USER_TOKEN = () => token({ userId: '2a171700-0000-4000-8000-0000000017a2', role: 'user', organizationId: ORG, tenantId: TENANT });
73: 
74: let seq = 0;
75: function anchorBody(): string {
76:   seq += 1;
77:   return JSON.stringify({ documentId: `doc-${Date.now()}${seq}-ks1717s${seq}`, contentHash: 'a'.repeat(64), network: 'preview' });
78: }

80: type Booted = { server: http.Server; base: string; recorder: ReturnType<typeof vi.fn> };
81: 
82: /** Import the real app with EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET as given, and serve it. */
83: async function boot(secret: string | undefined): Promise<Booted> {
84:   vi.resetModules();
85:   if (secret === undefined) delete process.env.EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET;
86:   else process.env.EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET = secret;
87:   vi.spyOn(express.application, 'listen').mockReturnValue(
88:     { keepAliveTimeout: 0, headersTimeout: 0, close: () => undefined } as unknown as http.Server,
89:   );
90:   const app = (await import('../index')).default;
91:   const { recordFlatAnchorProvenance } = await import('../provenance');
92:   const server = http.createServer(app);
93:   await new Promise<void>((resolve) => { server.listen(0, '127.0.0.1', resolve); });
94:   return { server, base: `http://127.0.0.1:${(server.address() as AddressInfo).port}`, recorder: recordFlatAnchorProvenance as unknown as ReturnType<typeof vi.fn> };
95: }
96: 
97: async function close(b: Booted | undefined): Promise<void> {
98:   if (!b) return;
99:   (b.server as any).closeAllConnections?.();
100:   await new Promise<void>((resolve) => b.server.close(() => resolve()));
101: }
102: 
103: /** POST the EXACT bytes given, with the headers given. */
104: async function post(b: Booted, tok: string, body: string, extra: Record<string, string> = {}): Promise<number> {
105:   const res = await fetch(`${b.base}/api/anchors`, {
106:     method: 'POST',
107:     headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json', Authorization: `Bearer ${tok}`, ...extra },
108:     body,
109:   });
110:   await res.text();
111:   return res.status;
112: }
113: 
114: /** What originate's setter sends for this body and this caller's bearer, signed with `secret`. */
115: function hop(b: Booted, body: string, secret: string | undefined, tok: string, nowMs?: number): Record<string, string> {
116:   return signEmitterHop({
117:     secret,
118:     marker: 'originate-create',
119:     method: 'POST',
120:     url: `${b.base}/api/anchors`,
121:     authorization: `Bearer ${tok}`,
122:     body,
123:     nowMs,
124:   });
125: }
126: 
127: /** A hop MAC'd by hand under ANY key, so a cell can present a key the signer would refuse. */
128: function hopMacedWith(key: string, body: string, tok: string): Record<string, string> {
129:   const timestamp = String(Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000));
130:   const signature = crypto
131:     .createHmac('sha256', key)
132:     .update(emitterSigningString({ method: 'POST', path: '/api/anchors', timestamp, marker: 'originate-create', authorization: `Bearer ${tok}`, body }), 'utf8')
133:     .digest('hex');
134:   return { [EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER]: 'originate-create', [EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER]: timestamp, [EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER]: signature };
135: }

### FILE AT HEAD: Blockchain/Dev/services/anchoring/src/emitterGuard.ts
1: /**
2:  * KS-1717 — anchoring's inbound guard for `x-emitter-internal`.
3:  *
4:  * The marker switches off the flat-anchor `anchor` provenance row on POST /api/anchors
5:  * (KS-566). Until this guard it was honoured by PRESENCE, and anchoring had no inbound
6:  * strip of its own: a caller on the compose network holding a connector JWT could set it
7:  * and suppress its own row. originate's legitimate hop forwards the caller's own bearer,
8:  * so no credential tells the two apart — only a signature originate makes and the caller
9:  * cannot (`verifyEmitterHop`, @secuura/shared; Kam's ruling (b), 2026-10-26).
10:  *
11:  * FAIL-CLOSED BY CONSTRUCTION. The verdict is held in a WeakSet that only this guard
12:  * writes, and the route reads it through `isVerifiedInternalEmit`. A request the guard
13:  * never saw — the guard unmounted, or a router mounted ahead of it — is simply not in
14:  * the set, so it records provenance. Nothing a header can say is consulted after the
15:  * guard: it deletes the marker and both proof headers on EVERY request, verified or not.
16:  *
17:  * The raw body is captured by `express.json`'s `verify` hook (`captureRawBody`) because
18:  * the signature covers the bytes as sent, not the parsed object. Without a capture the
19:  * body hashes as empty and the check fails — refusal, never a false accept.
20:  */
21: import type { IncomingMessage } from 'http';
22: import type { RequestHandler } from 'express';
23: import {
24:   EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER,
25:   EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER,
26:   EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER,
27:   isUsableEmitterSecret,
28:   verifyEmitterHop,
29: } from '@secuura/shared';
30: 
31: type Log = (level: 'info' | 'warn' | 'error', message: string, meta?: object) => void;
32: 
33: const rawBodies = new WeakMap<IncomingMessage, Buffer>();
34: const verifiedEmits = new WeakSet<IncomingMessage>();
35: 
36: /** `express.json({ verify })` hook: keep the exact bytes the signature covers. */
37: export function captureRawBody(req: IncomingMessage, _res: unknown, buf: Buffer): void {
38:   rawBodies.set(req, buf);
39: }
40: 
41: /** True only for a request whose marker this guard verified. */
42: export function isVerifiedInternalEmit(req: IncomingMessage): boolean {
43:   return verifiedEmits.has(req);
44: }
45: 
46: /**
47:  * Mount after `express.json({ verify: captureRawBody })` and ahead of every router.
48:  * A refused marker is logged only when a secret IS configured: then it is either a
49:  * forgery or a half-configured deploy, and both are worth a line. Unconfigured, every
50:  * marker is refused and the boot line (`describeEmitterGuardState`) already says so.
51:  * The log names the reason, never the header values.
52:  */
53: export function createEmitterSignatureGuard(secret: string, log: Log): RequestHandler {
54:   const configured = isUsableEmitterSecret(secret);
55:   return (req, _res, next) => {
56:     if (req.headers[EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER] !== undefined) {
57:       const verdict = verifyEmitterHop({
58:         secret,
59:         method: req.method,
60:         path: req.originalUrl,
61:         headers: req.headers,
62:         body: rawBodies.get(req),
63:       });
64:       if (verdict.ok) {
65:         verifiedEmits.add(req);
66:       } else if (configured) {
67:         log('warn', 'KS-1717: x-emitter-internal refused; the provenance row will be recorded', {
68:           reason: verdict.reason,
69:           method: req.method,
70:           path: req.path,
71:         });
72:       }
73:     }
74:     delete req.headers[EMITTER_MARKER_HEADER];
75:     delete req.headers[EMITTER_TIMESTAMP_HEADER];
76:     delete req.headers[EMITTER_SIGNATURE_HEADER];
77:     next();
78:   };
79: }
80: 
81: /**
82:  * The boot line. States the CONSEQUENCE of the configuration, as originate's
83:  * `describeState` does for the gateway vouch.
84:  */
85: export function describeEmitterGuardState(secret: string): { level: 'info' | 'warn'; message: string } {
86:   if (isUsableEmitterSecret(secret)) {
87:     return {
88:       level: 'info',
89:       message: '[emitter-guard] ACTIVE: x-emitter-internal is honoured only on a hop originate signed (KS-1717)',
90:     };
91:   }
92:   return {
93:     level: 'warn',
94:     message:
95:       '[emitter-guard] FAIL-CLOSED: EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET is unset or shorter than 32 characters, so x-emitter-internal is never honoured and originate\'s own hops each write a second action_provenance row (an `anchor` row beside the operation\'s own) until the secret is set on this service AND on originate',
96:   };
97: }

107: interface Anchor {
108:   id: string;

110:   anchorType: 'single' | 'batch';

113:   merkleProof?: MerkleProofStep[];

123:    * hash is persisted, but the node has not yet acknowledged it. Distinct from
124:    * 'submitted' (node accepted) and from 'pending' (nothing signed yet), because
125:    * only this state means "a transaction bearing our name may already be on
126:    * chain". 'failed' remains TERMINAL by construction (KS-535) — originate's
127:    * poller depends on that and this state does not disturb it.
128:    */
129:   status: 'pending' | 'submitting' | 'submitted' | 'confirmed' | 'failed';
130:   fee?: number;
131:   confirmations: number;
132:   confirmedAt?: Date;
133:   errorMessage?: string;
134:   retryCount: number;
135:   // KS-587: true when this anchor was produced by the mock/simulated provider
136:   // and corresponds to no Cardano transaction.
137:   simulated?: boolean;
138:   createdAt: Date;
139:   updatedAt: Date;
140: }

162: // =============================================================================
163: // DATABASE TABLES (ensured on first write)
164: // =============================================================================
165: 
166: let tablesEnsured = false;
167: 
168: async function ensureTables(): Promise<void> {
169:   if (tablesEnsured || !isDbAvailable()) return;
170:   try {
171:     // KS-92: anchor_store + anchor_batches are owned by the superuser migration
172:     // path (api-gateway CORE_MIGRATIONS). The runtime role (secuura_app) lacks
173:     // CREATE on schema public, so the CREATE TABLE below throws 42501 even with
174:     // IF NOT EXISTS. Verify the migration-created tables are present and skip
175:     // the redundant DDL when they are.
176:     const existing: any = await query(
177:       `SELECT to_regclass('public.anchor_store') AS s, to_regclass('public.anchor_batches') AS b`
178:     );
179:     const row = existing?.rows?.[0] ?? existing?.[0];
180:     if (row && row.s && row.b) {
181:       tablesEnsured = true;
182:       return;
183:     }
184:     await query(`
185:       CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS anchor_store (
186:         id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
187:         document_id TEXT NOT NULL,
188:         anchor_type TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'single',
189:         content_hash TEXT NOT NULL,
190:         merkle_root TEXT,
191:         merkle_proof JSONB,
192:         network TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'cardano',
193:         metadata_label INTEGER DEFAULT 674,
194:         metadata_payload JSONB,
195:         status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',
196:         transaction_hash TEXT,
197:         block_hash TEXT,
198:         block_number BIGINT,
199:         slot BIGINT,
200:         fee BIGINT,
201:         confirmations INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
202:         confirmed_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
203:         error_message TEXT,
204:         retry_count INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
205:         simulated BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE,
206:         created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
207:         updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
208:       )
209:     `);
210:     await query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_anchor_store_document ON anchor_store(document_id)`);
211:     await query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_anchor_store_tx ON anchor_store(transaction_hash)`);
212:     await query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_anchor_store_status ON anchor_store(status)`);
213:     // KS-281: composite unique so dbPersistAnchor's ON CONFLICT (document_id,
214:     // network) resolves on a fresh DB where ensureTables created the table
215:     // before migration 031 ran. On migrated DBs ensureTables verify-skips and
216:     // migration 031 owns this index.
217:     await query(`CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS anchor_store_document_network_uniq ON anchor_store(document_id, network)`);
218: 
219:     await query(`
220:       CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS anchor_batches (
221:         id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
222:         document_ids TEXT[],
223:         merkle_root TEXT NOT NULL,
224:         document_count INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
225:         anchor_id TEXT,
226:         status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',
227:         transaction_hash TEXT,
228:         created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
229:       )
230:     `);
231:     tablesEnsured = true;
232:   } catch (err: any) {
233:     log('warn', 'Failed to ensure anchor tables', { error: err?.message });
234:   }
235: }
236:

239: // =============================================================================

241: function log(level: 'info' | 'warn' | 'error', message: string, meta?: object) {
242:   const timestamp = new Date().toISOString();
243:   console.log(JSON.stringify({ timestamp, level, service: 'anchoring', message, ...meta }));
244: }

253:   credentials: true,
254: }));
255: // KS-1717: `verify` keeps the raw bytes, because the emitter signature covers the body as sent.
256: app.use(express.json({ verify: captureRawBody }));
257: app.use(rejectNulBytes()); // KS-471: no U+0000 may pass the boundary (raw-500 / persist class)

259: // KS-1717: verify, then delete, `x-emitter-internal` and its proof headers on EVERY request,
260: // ahead of every router. Unset secret => nothing is honoured (fail-closed; see emitterGuard.ts).
261: const EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET = process.env.EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET || '';
262: const emitterGuardState = describeEmitterGuardState(EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET);
263: log(emitterGuardState.level, emitterGuardState.message);
264: app.use(createEmitterSignatureGuard(EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET, log));

266: // Request logging
267: const requestLogger: RequestHandler = (req, res, next) => {
268:   const start = Date.now();
269:   res.on('finish', () => {
270:     log('info', 'Request completed', {
271:       method: req.method,
272:       path: req.path,
273:       statusCode: res.statusCode,
274:       duration: `${Date.now() - start}ms`,
275:     });
276:   });
277:   next();
278: };
279: app.use(requestLogger);

307: // Health check
308: app.get('/health', async (_req: Request, res: Response) => {
309:   // KS-671: report what the chain ACTUALLY did on the calls this service
310:   // already made — never from isCardanoReady(), which only tests that the key
311:   // is SET. A present-but-over-quota key satisfied the old check, which is how
312:   // demo reported healthy through six days of 402s (KS-670).
313:   //
314:   // The derivation itself lives in ./chainHealthStatus as a pure function so
315:   // it can be tested without booting this service (#728 review: defect #2 was
316:   // the hardcoded `status`, and that half shipped untested).
317:   const chain = getChainHealth();
318:   const verdict = deriveHealth({
319:     chain,
320:     cardanoConfigured: isCardanoConfigured(),
321:     cardanoReady: isCardanoReady(),
322:     dbAvailable: isDbAvailable(),
323:     degradedAfterFailures: CHAIN_DEGRADED_AFTER_FAILURES,
324:   });
325: 
326:   let cardanoStatus = verdict.cardanoStatus;
327: 
328:   // Dev/CI only. Probing a local devnet is free and never touches the
329:   // Blockfrost quota — but it runs ONLY when no credentials were supplied at
330:   // all. In the `mock-fallback` state we already know precisely what is wrong,
331:   // and overwriting that with 'mock mode' is what buried KS-670 for six days.
332:   if (verdict.mode === 'mock') {
333:     try {
334:       // KS-91: 500ms (not 3000) — api-gateway's /health/ready races each
335:       // downstream /health with a 3000ms CHECK_TIMEOUT. A 3000ms fetch
336:       // timeout here means /health always completes >3000ms when the
337:       // devnet is unreachable (CI workflow boot set excludes cardano-node),
338:       // so the gateway times out and reports 503. Production hits the
339:       // isCardanoReady() branch above (Blockfrost), so this fetch only
340:       // runs in dev/CI against a localhost devnet — 500ms is plenty.
341:       const response = await fetch(`${CARDANO_NODE_URL}/health`, {
342:         signal: AbortSignal.timeout(500)
343:       });
344:       cardanoStatus = response.ok ? 'connected (devnet)' : 'error';
345:     } catch {
346:       cardanoStatus = verdict.cardanoStatus;
347:     }
348:   }
349: 
350:   let pendingCount = 0;
351:   try {
352:     if (isDbAvailable()) {
353:       await ensureTables();
354:       const result = await query("SELECT COUNT(*) AS cnt FROM anchor_store WHERE status = 'pending'");
355:       pendingCount = Number(result.rows[0]?.cnt ?? 0);
356:     }
357:   } catch {
358:     // ignore — report 0
359:   }
360: 
361:   // KS-671: `status` was hardcoded 'healthy', so nothing the body reported
362:   // could ever change it. It is now derived — see ./chainHealthStatus.
363:   //
364:   // DELIBERATE: this endpoint still answers HTTP 200 when the chain is
365:   // degraded, because the compose healthcheck (`wget --spider`) treats a
366:   // non-200 as container death and `restart: unless-stopped` would then
367:   // restart-loop the service. Restarting does not refill a quota, and killing
368:   // anchoring on a provider outage is worse than running degraded. Liveness
369:   // and chain reachability are different questions; this reports both and
370:   // conflates neither. Wiring an alert to `status: 'degraded'` is the
371:   // monitoring change, tracked on KS-671.
372:   res.json({
373:     status: verdict.status,
374:     degradedReasons: verdict.degradedReasons,
375:     service: 'anchoring',
376:     version: '0.2.0',
377:     timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
378:     database: isDbAvailable() ? 'connected' : 'unavailable',
379:     cardano: {
380:       network: CARDANO_NETWORK,
381:       mode: verdict.mode,
382:       walletAddress: getWalletAddress(),
383:       status: cardanoStatus,
384:       reachability: chain.reachability,
385:       stale: chain.stale,
386:       lastSuccessAt: chain.lastSuccessAt,
387:       lastFailureAt: chain.lastFailureAt,
388:       lastFailureStatus: chain.lastFailureStatus,
389:       consecutiveFailures: chain.consecutiveFailures,
390:     },
391:     queue: {
392:       pending: pendingCount,
393:     },
394:   });
395: });
396: 
397: // Readiness check — verifies PostgreSQL is reachable
398: app.get('/health/ready', async (_req: Request, res: Response) => {
399:   const CHECK_TIMEOUT = 3000;
400:   const checks: Record<string, { status: string; latencyMs: number; error?: string }> = {};
401: 
402:   const start = Date.now();
403:   try {
404:     await Promise.race([
405:       (async () => {
406:         if (!isDbAvailable()) throw new Error('Database not available');
407:         await query('SELECT 1');
408:       })(),
409:       new Promise<never>((_, reject) =>
410:         setTimeout(() => reject(new Error('timeout')), CHECK_TIMEOUT),
411:       ),
412:     ]);
413:     checks.postgres = { status: 'up', latencyMs: Date.now() - start };
414:   } catch (err: any) {
415:     checks.postgres = {
416:       status: 'down',
417:       latencyMs: Date.now() - start,
418:       error: err?.message || 'Unknown error',
419:     };
420:   }
421: 
422:   const allUp = Object.values(checks).every(c => c.status === 'up');
423: 
424:   res.status(allUp ? 200 : 503).json({
425:     ready: allUp,
426:     checks,
427:   });
428: });
429: 
430: // API info
431: app.get('/api', (_req: Request, res: Response) => {
432:   res.json({
433:     service: 'Secuura Anchoring Service',
434:     version: '0.1.0',
435:     network: CARDANO_NETWORK,
436:     endpoints: {
437:       'POST /api/anchors': 'Anchor a single document',
438:       'POST /api/anchors/batch': 'Anchor multiple documents (Merkle tree)',
439:       'GET /api/anchors/:id': 'Get anchor by ID',
440:       'GET /api/anchors/document/:documentId': 'Get anchors for document',
441:       'GET /api/anchors/tx/:txHash': 'Get anchor by transaction hash',
442:       'POST /api/anchors/verify': 'Verify an anchor',
443:       'GET /api/anchors/queue': 'Get queue status',
444:     },
445:     metadataLabels: {
446:       674: 'Secuura Document Anchor (single)',
447:       675: 'Secuura Merkle Root Anchor (batch)',
448:     },
449:   });
450: });
451: 
452: // KS-584 (P3, QA-High): the chain-fact read is registered BEFORE the auth
453: // mount so anchor truth is caller-independent. Originate's verify heal used
454: // to FORWARD the caller's bearer here — anonymous/invalid callers therefore
455: // could not confirm a stale 'pending' anchor and were served a false-negative
456: // isAnchored while authenticated callers got the truth (QA finding, 2026-08-11;
457: // Kam ruled anonymous verify is a supported public path). The response is the
458: // same chain-public fact set K's public verify already emits (tx, block,
459: // anchor metadata) — no credential material, no tenant-owned naming — so the
460: // H2 rationale below (forgeable trust headers) does not apply to it. The
461: // gateway's own spec-auth gate for EXTERNAL calls is unchanged.
462: app.get('/api/anchors/verify/:hash', (req: Request, res: Response) => handleAnchorVerifyByHash(req, res));
463: 
464: // Pen-test H2 — every /api/* route below this point JWT-verifies the
465: // bearer token via @secuura/shared's `authenticate()`. Without this,
466: // downstream services trusted the gateway-injected x-user-* headers,
467: // which an attacker reaching a service directly (intra-cluster, leaked
468: // internal endpoint, etc.) could forge.
469: app.use('/api', jwtAuthenticate());
470: 
471: /**
472:  * POST /api/anchors
473:  * Anchor a single document to the blockchain
474:  */
475: app.post('/api/anchors', async (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
476:   try {
477:     const data = anchorDocumentSchema.parse(req.body);
478: 
479:     // KS-480 §6 / KS-566: attribution on the flat fallback path. Kam's G-1
480:     // ruling puts this surface on the CONNECTOR pattern, so there is no
481:     // caller-supplied principal to validate any more — `onBehalfOf` is
482:     // stripped by the schema (anchorSchema.ts) rather than 400'd, because
483:     // Platform S is live sending it (PS-616, UAT since 2026-08-21T10:07Z).
484:     // Stored OFF-CHAIN in action_provenance, never in metadataPayload
485:     // (whitelist below).
486:     const caller = (req as any).user as { role?: string; userId?: string; organizationId?: string; tenantId?: string } | undefined;
487: 
488:     // Create anchor record
489:     const anchorId = `anchor_${require('crypto').randomUUID()}`;
490: 
491:     // KS-240/KS-480: THE single whitelist construction path — extracted so
492:     // the "provenance never reaches the chain" guarantee is unit-tested
493:     // structurally, not remembered per-field (Peter §6 condition 1).
494:     const metadataPayload = buildFlatAnchorMetadataPayload(data, CARDANO_NETWORK);
495:     
496:     const anchor: Anchor = {
497:       id: anchorId,
498:       documentId: data.documentId,
499:       anchorType: 'single',
500:       contentHash: data.contentHash,
501:       network: CARDANO_NETWORK,
502:       metadataLabel: 674,
503:       metadataPayload,
504:       status: 'pending',
505:       confirmations: 0,
506:       retryCount: 0,
507:       createdAt: new Date(),
508:       updatedAt: new Date(),
509:     };
510:     
511:     // Persist to database. KS-281: dbPersistAnchor returns the ACTUAL stored id
512:     // (the existing row's id on a re-anchor of the same document+network) and
513:     // throws on failure — so the surfaced id always resolves on GET, and a
514:     // failed write becomes a 5xx via the catch below instead of a false 202.
515:     // KS-1385 MAJ-1: the caller decides whether an EXISTING attestation may be
516:     // replaced. `caller` is the same verified `req.user` the provenance row uses.
517:     const persistedId = await dbPersistAnchor(anchor, caller);
518: 
519:     // KS-480 §6 / KS-566: append the provenance row fire-and-forget (the anchor
520:     // itself never blocks on attribution). CONNECTOR-ONLY — one row per
521:     // connector flat anchor, attributed to the key, no principal.
522:     //
523:     // The condition moved from `data.onBehalfOf` to the caller's role, and
524:     // that IS the fix: before KS-566 a bare connector anchor wrote no
525:     // provenance row at all, so dropping the field would have left this route
526:     // with no actor of any kind. Interactive (JWT) callers still write none —
527:     // their identity is the JWT, and inventing a connector row for a human
528:     // would be a false attribution.
529:     // KS-566: `x-emitter-internal` means this is K's own originate->anchoring
530:     // hop for an operation that ALREADY wrote its own action_provenance row
531:     // (create / share / transfer-custody / revoke / lifecycle). Recording again
532:     // here would make one user action produce two rows and roughly double the
533:     // table — measured live on 2026-08-26, where a single lifecycle call
534:     // produced both a `lifecycle:rename` and an `anchor` row. Platform-S's
535:     // genuine flat fallback carries no such header and still records its
536:     // `anchor` row.
537:     //
538:     // KS-1717: WHAT IS GUARDED, and what is not. The marker is honoured only
539:     // when the emitter guard (emitterGuard.ts, mounted ahead of every router)
540:     // verified an HMAC originate made over this request's method, path, marker,
541:     // a timestamp within 60 s, the caller's Authorization header and the raw body
542:     // bytes, under EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET.
543:     // The header itself is never read here: the guard deletes it, so a value a
544:     // caller sends cannot reach this line. The two strips (the gateway's edge
545:     // strip on `/api/*`, originate's inbound strip, KS-741) cover requests that
546:     // pass through those services ONLY — they do nothing for a caller that
547:     // reaches anchoring directly on the compose network, which is why the
548:     // signature exists. It does NOT make the marker unforgeable: anyone holding
549:     // the secret can sign, and a byte-identical replay under the SAME bearer
550:     // verifies for just under 61 s (up to ~121 s with a fast signer clock; no
551:     // nonce store). With the secret unset, nothing verifies and
552:     // originate's own hops write a second row (fail-closed).
553:     const isInternalEmit = isVerifiedInternalEmit(req);
554:     if (caller?.role === 'connector' && !isInternalEmit) {
555:       recordFlatAnchorProvenance({
556:         documentId: data.documentId,
557:         connectorId: caller.userId,
558:         organizationId: caller.organizationId,
559:         tenantId: caller.tenantId,
560:       }).catch((provErr: Error) => log('warn', 'flat-anchor provenance record failed', { documentId: data.documentId, error: provErr.message }));
561:     }
562: 
563:     // Process anchor (in development mode, mock the transaction)
564:     processAnchor(persistedId);
565: 
566:     log('info', 'Anchor created', { anchorId: persistedId, documentId: data.documentId });
567: 
568:     res.status(202).json({
569:       success: true,
570:       data: {
571:         id: persistedId,
572:         documentId: anchor.documentId,
573:         contentHash: anchor.contentHash,
574:         status: anchor.status,
575:         network: anchor.network,
576:         metadataLabel: anchor.metadataLabel,
577:         createdAt: anchor.createdAt,
578:         message: 'Anchor request submitted. Transaction will be processed shortly.',
579:       },
580:     });
581:   } catch (error) {
582:     if (error instanceof z.ZodError) {
583:       return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'Validation failed', details: error.errors } });
584:     }
585:     // KS-1603: a deliberate refusal, not a fault — without this arm `next(error)`
586:     // would answer 500. The body names neither the owning tenant nor whether the
587:     // document exists at all: the caller is not entitled to either.
588:     if (isAnchorOwnershipError(error)) {
589:       return res.status(403).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'FORBIDDEN', message: 'Caller does not own this document' } });
590:     }
591:     next(error);
592:   }
593: });
594: 
595: /**
596:  * GET /api/anchors/verify/:hash
597:  * Chain-first verification: given a content hash, return the on-chain anchor
598:  * record (if any). Works across environments because the source of truth is
599:  * the blockchain, not per-environment databases.
600:  *
601:  * Lookup order:
602:  *   1. Local anchor_store DB (fast path — same-environment hit)
603:  *   2. Blockfrost scan of platform wallet's recent txs at metadata label 674
604:  *      (cross-environment hit — chain is shared)
605:  */
606: 
607: // KS-252: the step-2 chain scan was unbounded — 1 + up-to-100 sequential
608: // indexer calls (~20-30 s) for EVERY hash not anchored yet, which is the
609: // common case when a verify races a fresh anchor. Under the KS-194 load
610: // baseline that stalled 39% of /api/verification/verify calls to client
611: // timeout. Bound it three ways: batch the metadata fetches concurrently,
612: // stop at a wall-clock budget, and cache scan outcomes (a positive hit is
613: // an immutable on-chain fact; a miss gets a short TTL since the hash may
614: // confirm at any moment).
615: const VERIFY_SCAN_BUDGET_MS = parseInt(process.env.VERIFY_CHAIN_SCAN_BUDGET_MS || '4000', 10);
616: const VERIFY_SCAN_BATCH = 10;
617: const VERIFY_SCAN_NEG_TTL_MS = 30_000;
618: const VERIFY_SCAN_POS_TTL_MS = 600_000;
619: const VERIFY_SCAN_CACHE_MAX = 5_000;
620: const verifyScanCache = new Map<string, { expires: number; hit: Record<string, any> | null }>();
621: 
622: // KS-584 (P3): extracted to a named handler — registered pre-auth above (see
623: // the comment at the registration site); the route path stays identical.
624: const handleAnchorVerifyByHash = async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
625:   const raw = (req.params.hash || '').trim();
626:   const hash = raw.replace(/^sha256:/i, '').toLowerCase();
627:   if (!/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/.test(hash)) {
628:     return res.status(400).json({ verified: false, error: 'Invalid content hash — expect 64 hex chars' });
629:   }
630: 
631:   // KS-1175: the body builder lives in anchorReadback.ts (pure, unit-tested without a boot) — it was a closure here,
632:   // which is why KS-721's read-back could only be checked through a booted service. Same shape, plus metadata.identity.
633:   const buildResponse = (source: 'db' | 'chain', onChain: any, row?: any) =>
634:     buildVerifyResponse(source, onChain, row, { hash, network: CARDANO_NETWORK });
635: 
636:   try {
637:     // Step 1 — local DB lookup (fast, same-env)
638:     try {
639:       const result = await query(
640:         `SELECT id, document_id, content_hash, transaction_hash, block_number,
641:                 block_hash, confirmed_at, created_at, metadata_payload, status
642:            FROM anchor_store
643:           -- KS-726: 'submitting' included. The row carries a REAL, locally
644:           -- derived transaction hash at this point, so omitting it made a
645:           -- written-ahead anchor invisible to verify-by-hash — and a row RESTING
646:           -- in 'submitting' (the state the failure path now leaves behind)
647:           -- invisible indefinitely. Invisible is the worse failure.
648:           --
649:           -- #805 review F1: this comment used to claim the status was "selected
650:           -- and returned". It was selected and then DROPPED — buildResponse had
651:           -- no status key, verified:true is unconditional, and KS-522's honesty
652:           -- branch keys off the tx-hash PREFIX, which a real written-ahead
653:           -- 64-hex passes straight through. So the caller was handed
654:           -- verified:true plus a live explorer link for a submission the node
655:           -- had never acknowledged, with no way to tell it from a confirmed
656:           -- one. The mitigation is now actually implemented: see the status and
657:           -- acknowledged fields in buildResponse.
658:           --
659:           -- NOTE: no backticks in this block. It sits inside a TEMPLATE
660:           -- LITERAL, so a backtick here terminates the string and the SQL
661:           -- comment marker gives no protection — it fails as a JS syntax error
662:           -- pointing at the WHERE clause, ten lines below the real cause.
663:           WHERE LOWER(content_hash) = $1 AND status IN ('submitting','submitted','confirmed')
664:           ORDER BY confirmed_at DESC NULLS LAST, created_at DESC
665:           LIMIT 1`,
666:         [hash],
667:       );
668:       if (result.rows.length > 0) {
669:         const row = result.rows[0] as any;
670:         const payload = typeof row.metadata_payload === 'string'
671:           ? JSON.parse(row.metadata_payload)
672:           : (row.metadata_payload || {});
673:         return res.json(buildResponse('db', payload, row));
674:       }
675:     } catch (err: any) {
676:       log('warn', 'verify-by-hash: local DB lookup failed', { error: err?.message });
677:     }
678: 
679:     // Step 2 — chain scan (cross-env). Only possible if Blockfrost is ready.
680:     if (!isCardanoReady()) {
681:       return res.status(404).json({ verified: false, hash, reason: 'Not found in local DB and Blockfrost not configured.' });
682:     }
683: 
684:     // KS-252: serve a recent scan outcome from cache before hitting the chain.
685:     const cached = verifyScanCache.get(hash);
686:     if (cached && cached.expires > Date.now()) {
687:       if (cached.hit) {
688:         return res.json(cached.hit);
689:       }
690:       return res.status(404).json({ verified: false, hash, reason: 'No on-chain anchor found for this hash (cached scan).' });
691:     }
692: 
693:     const { getProvider } = await import('./cardano/provider');
694:     const bf: any = getProvider();
695:     const walletAddress = getWalletAddress();
696:     if (!bf || !walletAddress) {
697:       return res.status(404).json({ verified: false, hash, reason: 'Blockfrost or wallet not initialised.' });
698:     }
699: 
700:     // Fetch recent txs for the platform wallet. 100 is plenty for a testnet;
701:     // paginate further if needed. KS-252: metadata lookups run in concurrent
702:     // batches under a wall-clock budget instead of one-by-one (the old
703:     // sequential walk took ~20-30 s on a miss).
704:     const txs: any[] = (await bf.addressesTransactions(walletAddress, { order: 'desc', count: 100 })) || [];
705:     const scanDeadline = Date.now() + VERIFY_SCAN_BUDGET_MS;
706:     let scanComplete = true;
707: 
708:     if (verifyScanCache.size >= VERIFY_SCAN_CACHE_MAX) {
709:       verifyScanCache.clear(); // simple bound; entries are cheap to recompute
710:     }
711: 
712:     for (let i = 0; i < txs.length; i += VERIFY_SCAN_BATCH) {
713:       if (Date.now() > scanDeadline) {
714:         scanComplete = false;
715:         log('warn', 'verify-by-hash: chain-scan budget exhausted', {
716:           hash: hash.substring(0, 16), scanned: i, total: txs.length, budgetMs: VERIFY_SCAN_BUDGET_MS,
717:         });
718:         break;
719:       }
720:       const batch = txs.slice(i, i + VERIFY_SCAN_BATCH);
721:       const metas = await Promise.all(batch.map(async (tx: any) => {
722:         try {
723:           return { tx, md: (await bf.txsMetadata(tx.tx_hash)) as any[] };
724:         } catch (err: any) {
725:           log('warn', 'verify-by-hash: metadata fetch failed for tx', { txHash: tx.tx_hash, error: err?.message });
726:           return { tx, md: null as any[] | null };
727:         }
728:       }));
729: 
730:       for (const { tx, md } of metas) {
731:         const secEntry = (md || []).find((m: any) => String(m.label) === '674');
732:         // KS-1284: undo the tx-build codec BEFORE anything reads a value — including the hash compare below. The hash
733:         // is exactly 64 bytes and never chunked, but the comparison must not depend on that coincidence.
734:         const sec: any = fromCardanoMetadatum(secEntry?.json_metadata?.secuura);
735:         if (!sec) continue;
736:         const onChainHash = String(sec.hash || '').replace(/^sha256:/i, '').toLowerCase();
737:         if (onChainHash !== hash) continue;
738: 
739:         // Hash matches — resolve block info
740:         const txInfo: any = await bf.txs(tx.tx_hash).catch(() => null);
741:         const onChain = {
742:           secuura: sec,
743:           txHash: tx.tx_hash,
744:           blockNumber: txInfo?.block_height || null,
745:           confirmedAt: txInfo?.block_time ? new Date(txInfo.block_time * 1000).toISOString() : null,
746:         };
747:         const body = buildResponse('chain', onChain);
748:         // An on-chain anchor is immutable — cache the hit for re-verifies.
749:         verifyScanCache.set(hash, { expires: Date.now() + VERIFY_SCAN_POS_TTL_MS, hit: body });
750:         return res.json(body);
751:       }
752:     }
753: 
754:     // Cache the miss briefly only when the scan covered every candidate tx —
755:     // a budget-truncated scan proves nothing.
756:     if (scanComplete) {
757:       verifyScanCache.set(hash, { expires: Date.now() + VERIFY_SCAN_NEG_TTL_MS, hit: null });
758:     }
759:     return res.status(404).json({ verified: false, hash, reason: 'No on-chain anchor found for this hash.' });
760:   } catch (error: any) {
761:     log('error', 'verify-by-hash failed', { error: error?.message });
762:     return res.status(500).json({ verified: false, error: 'Chain verification failed', details: error?.message });
763:   }
764: };
765: 
766: /**
767:  * POST /api/anchors/batch
768:  * Anchor multiple documents using Merkle tree
769:  */
770: app.post('/api/anchors/batch', async (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
771:   try {
772:     const data = batchAnchorSchema.parse(req.body);
773: 
774:     // KS-1385: the same verified principal the flat route reads at :474.
775:     const batchCaller = (req as any).user as AnchorCaller | undefined;
776: 
777:     // KS-1603: every id checked in ONE read BEFORE anything is written. The gate
778:     // inside dbPersistAnchor is the guarantee and would refuse the offending item
779:     // on its own — but it would refuse it mid-loop, after `dbPersistBatch` and
780:     // after the items before it had already landed. That partial write is this
781:     // route's existing shape for ANY per-item failure (there is no per-item error
782:     // collection: the loop sits in the handler's single try, so the first failure
783:     // aborts the batch and nothing is returned), and the KS-1603 refusal would
784:     // have made it reachable far more often. Checking up front refuses the whole
785:     // batch, which IS this route's behaviour today, while writing nothing.
786:     const batchWrite = await mayWriteAnchorRows(data.documents.map((d) => d.documentId), batchCaller, log);
787:     if (!batchWrite.allow) {
788:       log('warn', 'KS-1603: refused a BATCH anchor write — one of its documents is in another tenant; nothing was written', {
789:         documentId: batchWrite.documentId,
790:         documentCount: data.documents.length,
791:         ownerTenantId: batchWrite.ownerTenantId,
792:         callerTenantId: batchCaller?.tenantId,
793:         callerOrganizationId: batchCaller?.organizationId,
794:       });
795:       throw new AnchorOwnershipError(batchWrite);
796:     }
797: 
798:     // Build Merkle tree
799:     const hashes = data.documents.map(d => d.contentHash);
800:     const { root, proofs } = buildMerkleTree(hashes);
801:     
802:     // Create batch record
803:     const batchId = `batch_${require('crypto').randomUUID()}`;
804:     const batch: MerkleBatch = {
805:       id: batchId,
806:       documentIds: data.documents.map(d => d.documentId),
807:       merkleRoot: root,
808:       documentCount: data.documents.length,
809:       status: 'pending',
810:       createdAt: new Date(),
811:     };
812:     // Persist batch to database
813:     await dbPersistBatch(batch);
814:     
815:     // Create anchors for each document
816:     const anchorIds: string[] = [];
817:     
818:     for (let i = 0; i < data.documents.length; i++) {
819:       const doc = data.documents[i];
820:       const anchorId = `anchor_${require('crypto').randomUUID()}_${i}`;
821:       
822:       const anchor: Anchor = {
823:         id: anchorId,
824:         documentId: doc.documentId,
825:         anchorType: 'batch',
826:         contentHash: doc.contentHash,
827:         merkleRoot: root,
828:         merkleProof: proofs[i],
829:         network: CARDANO_NETWORK,
830:         metadataLabel: 675,
831:         metadataPayload: {
832:           secuura: {
833:             version: '1.0',
834:             type: 'batch',
835:             merkleRoot: root,
836:             documentCount: data.documents.length,
837:             batchId,
838:             timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
839:           },
840:         },
841:         status: 'pending',
842:         confirmations: 0,
843:         retryCount: 0,
844:         createdAt: new Date(),
845:         updatedAt: new Date(),
846:       };
847:       
848:       anchorIds.push(anchorId);
849: 
850:       // Persist each anchor to database.
851:       // KS-1385 MIN-3: a batch item for an already-anchored document used to
852:       // wipe that row's attestation and leave a hybrid row. The anchor_type
853:       // equality in the upsert's CASE is what stops it, so this path is
854:       // guarded whatever the caller is -- the caller is forwarded for the same
855:       // reason the flat route forwards it, not as the fix for MIN-3.
856:       await dbPersistAnchor(anchor, batchCaller);
857:     }
858:     
859:     // Process batch anchor (single transaction for Merkle root)
860:     processBatchAnchor(batchId, anchorIds);
861:     
862:     log('info', 'Batch anchor created', { batchId, documentCount: data.documents.length });
863:     
864:     res.status(202).json({
865:       success: true,
866:       data: {
867:         batchId,
868:         merkleRoot: root,
869:         documentCount: data.documents.length,
870:         status: 'pending',
871:         anchors: anchorIds.map((id, i) => ({
872:           id,
873:           documentId: data.documents[i].documentId,
874:           merkleProof: proofs[i],
875:         })),
876:         message: 'Batch anchor request submitted.',
877:       },
878:     });
879:   } catch (error) {
880:     if (error instanceof z.ZodError) {
881:       return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'Validation failed', details: error.errors } });
882:     }
883:     // KS-1603, as on the flat route: a refusal, answered 403, naming nothing.
884:     if (isAnchorOwnershipError(error)) {
885:       return res.status(403).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'FORBIDDEN', message: 'Caller does not own this document' } });
886:     }
887:     next(error);
888:   }
889: });
890: 
891: /**
892:  * GET /api/anchors/:id
893:  * Get anchor by ID or document ID (for backwards compatibility)
894:  */
895: app.get('/api/anchors/:id', async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
896:   const { id } = req.params;
897:   
898:   // First, try to find by anchor ID in DB
899:   let anchor = await dbGetAnchorById(id);
900: 
901:   // If not found and not an anchor ID format, try to find by document ID
902:   if (!anchor && !id.startsWith('anchor_')) {
903:     // Handle non-existent patterns for testing
904:     if (id.includes('non-existent') || id.includes('missing')) {
905:       return res.status(404).json({
906:         success: false,
907:         error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'No anchor found for this document', documentId: id },
908:       });
909:     }
910: 
911:     // Look up by document ID in DB
912:     const docAnchors = await dbGetAnchorsByDocument(id);
913:     if (docAnchors.length > 0) {
914:       anchor = docAnchors[0];
915:     }
916: 
917:     // If still not found, return 404 in every environment. A previous
918:     // dev-mode fallback returned a synthesized `tx_...` here, which
919:     // poisoned downstream caller state (originate cached the fake into
920:     // the doc DB). Never fabricate chain-state signals — if a caller
921:     // needs a mock, it should opt in explicitly.
922:     if (!anchor) {
923:       return res.status(404).json({
924:         success: false,
925:         error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'No anchor found for this document', documentId: id },
926:       });
927:     }
928:   }
929:   
930:   if (!anchor) {
931:     return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Anchor not found' } });
932:   }
933:   
934:   // Return authoritative state only. Previous versions of this endpoint
935:   // synthesized random `tx_...` / block-number values in non-production
936:   // envs for test convenience, but that corrupted originate's poll —
937:   // downstream callers need null for "not yet submitted", not a fake hash.
938:   //
939:   // KS-587: a fabricated (mock/simulated) anchor is never presented as
940:   // on-chain proof — same KS-522 rules the chain-verify path applies. The
941:   // placeholder moves to `simulatedTxRef`; `verified` requires a real tx.
942:   const txView = honestTxView(anchor.transactionHash, anchor.simulated);
943:   res.json({
944:     documentId: anchor.documentId,
945:     transactionId: txView.txHash,
946:     transactionHash: txView.txHash,
947:     ...(txView.simulated ? { simulated: true, simulatedTxRef: txView.simulatedTxRef } : {}),
948:     status: anchor.status,
949:     blockNumber: anchor.blockNumber || null,
950:     network: anchor.network,
951:     anchoredAt: anchor.confirmedAt?.toISOString() || null,
952:     createdAt: anchor.createdAt?.toISOString() || null,
953:     contentHash: anchor.contentHash,
954:     verified: !txView.simulated && anchor.status === 'confirmed',
955:     // KS-1175: what S records into DocumentTransaction.AnchorIdentityJson on confirmation (PS-871) — the anchored
956:     // identityCommitment (KS-721) and the seven non-PII identity fields, from the row's metadata_payload (logical
957:     // form). null / null for an anchor that predates the fields. This route returned neither before KS-1175.
958:     ...anchorIdentityView((anchor.metadataPayload as any)?.secuura),
959:     // KS-535: surfaced so originate's poller/reconciler can record WHY a
960:     // terminal failure happened on the document (KS-520 `error` field), and
961:     // for operator diagnostics. `failed` is terminal (see processAnchor —
962:     // retries reset to 'pending' before their backoff starts).
963:     errorMessage: anchor.errorMessage || null,
964:     retryCount: anchor.retryCount ?? 0,
965:   });
966: });

968: /**
969:  * GET /api/anchors/document/:documentId
970:  * Get all anchors for a document.
971:  *
972:  * BACKLOG H2 (was F-API-01 part 2): previously returned 200 with an empty
973:  * `anchors:[]` array AND echoed the requested documentId for any unknown id.
974:  * That's id-reflection — enabling enumeration ("did this id exist or not?")
975:  * and giving an attacker confirmation that their guess landed on a syntactic
976:  * shape the API will accept. Now: 404 with no echoed id when no anchors
977:  * match. Gateway callers already handle non-200 by resolving null

979:  * so the verifier-portal flow is unaffected.

981: app.get('/api/anchors/document/:documentId', async (req: Request, res: Response) => {

985:   }
986:   const documentAnchors = dbResults.map(formatAnchorResponse);
987:   res.json({
988:     success: true,
989:     data: {
990:       documentId: req.params.documentId,
991:       anchors: documentAnchors,
992:       count: documentAnchors.length,

994:   });

997: /**
998:  * POST /api/anchors/thread-mint
999:  * F6 + AUDIT S-3/S-17 Sprint 2 Phase 2 part B — mints the per-document
1000:  * state-thread NFT and locks the initial Draft DocumentDatum at the
1001:  * document_contract script address. Called by services/originate during
1002:  * document creation; originate persists the returned tuple into
1003:  * state_thread_registry via stateThreadRepo.
1004:  *
1005:  * Internal-only (behind authenticate()). Body shape mirrors
1006:  * InitialDocumentParams in cardano/threadTokenMint.ts.
1007:  */
1008: app.post('/api/anchors/thread-mint', async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
1009:   try {
1010:     // KS-445: validate the whole body against the published contract
1011:     // (threadMintRequestSchema mirrors ThreadMintRequestSchema in
1012:     // anchoring.openapi.ts) instead of only truthy-string-checking the five
1013:     // required fields. Previously a spec-illegal fuzz body — non-hex hashes,
1014:     // float/string createdAtMs (BigInt() throws), non-boolean dryRun —
1015:     // sailed into the Cardano encoding layer and came back a raw 500.
1016:     const parsed = threadMintRequestSchema.safeParse(req.body ?? {});
1017:     if (!parsed.success) {
1018:       const firstIssue = parsed.error.issues[0];
1019:       const fieldName = firstIssue?.path.join('.') || 'body';
1020:       return res.status(400).json({
1021:         success: false,
1022:         error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: `Missing or invalid field: ${fieldName}` },
1023:       });
1024:     }
1025:     const body = parsed.data;
1026: 
1027:     // The spec keeps deploymentIdHex a plain string, so the schema doesn't
1028:     // tighten it — but a non-hex value can never encode (lucid Data.to
1029:     // throws → was a 500). Guard-and-map to the honest 400 instead.
1030:     if (body.deploymentIdHex !== undefined && !isEncodableDeploymentIdHex(body.deploymentIdHex)) {
1031:       return res.status(400).json({
1032:         success: false,
1033:         error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'Missing or invalid field: deploymentIdHex' },
1034:       });
1035:     }
1036: 
1037:     // KS-445: on stacks without Cardano credentials (the demo VM runs
1038:     // SIMULATE_ANCHORING=true — originate simulates its anchor and never
1039:     // calls thread-mint; this service has no simulated mint path) a direct
1040:     // call is an unconfigured-dependency 503, answered before paying the
1041:     // lucid WASM import cost. mintAndLock's own guards + the classifier
1042:     // below back this up should the env change mid-flight.
1043:     if (!process.env.BLOCKFROST_API_KEY || !process.env.PLATFORM_WALLET_MNEMONIC) {
1044:       return res.status(503).json({
1045:         success: false,
1046:         error: { code: 'SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE', message: 'Cardano anchoring dependency not configured' },
1047:       });
1048:     }
1049: 
1050:     // Lazy import keeps the lucid WASM cost off the boot path — only
1051:     // pulled in when an actual mint is requested.
1052:     const { mintAndLock } = await import('./cardano/threadTokenMint');
1053: 
1054:     const result = await mintAndLock({
1055:       document: {
1056:         documentId: body.documentId,
1057:         documentHashHex: body.documentHashHex,
1058:         originatorPkhHex: body.originatorPkhHex,
1059:         documentType: body.documentType,
1060:         metadataHashHex: body.metadataHashHex,
1061:         deploymentIdHex: body.deploymentIdHex || '',
1062:         createdAtMs: body.createdAtMs,
1063:       },
1064:       dryRun: body.dryRun === true,
1065:     });
1066: 
1067:     return res.json({
1068:       success: true,
1069:       data: result,
1070:     });
1071:   } catch (err: any) {
1072:     // KS-445: classify dependency/config/encoding failures into their honest
1073:     // status (503/400) before falling back to the 500 for the truly unknown.
1074:     const mapped = classifyThreadMintError(err);
1075:     if (mapped) {
1076:       log('warn', 'thread-mint dependency/input failure', { status: mapped.status, error: err?.message });
1077:       return res.status(mapped.status).json({
1078:         success: false,
1079:         error: { code: mapped.code, message: mapped.message },
1080:       });
1081:     }
1082:     log('error', 'thread-mint failed', { error: err?.message, stack: err?.stack });
1083:     return res.status(500).json({
1084:       success: false,
1085:       error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'thread-mint failed', details: err?.message },
1086:     });
1087:   }
1088: });

1090: /**
1091:  * GET /api/anchors/thread-state/:documentId
1092:  * F6 + AUDIT S-3/S-17 Sprint 2 Phase 1 — visibility endpoint for the
1093:  * state-thread registry. Returns the (policy_id, asset_name, script_address)
1094:  * tuple if the document has been minted, plus a status field indicating
1095:  * whether Phase 2 (mint+lock tx construction) has actually been wired up.
1096:  *
1097:  * Always returns 200 — a missing registry entry is not a 404 here, it's a
1098:  * "not yet on the NFT path" status. Use /api/anchors/document/:id for the
1099:  * older metadata-label history.
1100:  */
1101: app.get('/api/anchors/thread-state/:documentId', async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
1102:   try {
1103:     const result = await query(
1104:       `SELECT document_id, policy_id, asset_name, script_address,
1105:               seed_tx_out_ref, mint_tx_hash, network, originator_pkh,
1106:               created_at, status, burned_at, burn_tx_hash
1107:          FROM state_thread_registry
1108:         WHERE document_id = $1
1109:         LIMIT 1`,
1110:       [req.params.documentId],
1111:     );
1112:     if (result.rows.length === 0) {
1113:       return res.json({
1114:         documentId: req.params.documentId,
1115:         threadToken: null,
1116:         phase: 'pre-nft',
1117:         note: 'Document predates the state-thread NFT path or Phase 2 mint not yet shipped.',
1118:       });
1119:     }
1120:     const row = result.rows[0] as any;
1121:     return res.json({
1122:       documentId: row.document_id,
1123:       threadToken: {
1124:         policyId: row.policy_id,
1125:         assetName: row.asset_name,
1126:         scriptAddress: row.script_address,
1127:         seedTxOutRef: row.seed_tx_out_ref,
1128:         mintTxHash: row.mint_tx_hash,
1129:         network: row.network,
1130:         originatorPkh: row.originator_pkh,
1131:         status: row.status,
1132:         createdAt: row.created_at,
1133:         burnedAt: row.burned_at,
1134:         burnTxHash: row.burn_tx_hash,
1135:       },
1136:       phase: row.mint_tx_hash ? 'live' : 'registered-not-minted',
1137:     });
1138:   } catch (err: any) {
1139:     // Migration 010 may not have run yet in older environments — degrade gracefully.
1140:     if (/relation .*state_thread_registry.* does not exist/i.test(String(err?.message))) {
1141:       return res.json({
1142:         documentId: req.params.documentId,
1143:         threadToken: null,
1144:         phase: 'pre-migration',
1145:         note: 'Migration 010 (state_thread_registry) has not run in this environment.',
1146:       });
1147:     }
1148:     log('error', 'thread-state lookup failed', { error: err?.message });
1149:     return res.status(500).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'thread-state lookup failed', details: err?.message } });
1150:   }
1151: });

1171: /**
1172:  * POST /api/anchors/verify
1173:  * Verify a document anchor
1174:  */
1175: app.post('/api/anchors/verify', async (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
1176:   try {
1177:     const data = verifyAnchorSchema.parse(req.body);
1178:     
1179:     // Find anchor by content hash in DB
1180:     let foundAnchor: Anchor | undefined;
1181:     try {
1182:       if (isDbAvailable()) {
1183:         await ensureTables();
1184:         const result = await query(
1185:           "SELECT * FROM anchor_store WHERE content_hash = $1 AND status = 'confirmed' LIMIT 1",
1186:           [data.contentHash]
1187:         );
1188:         if (result.rows.length > 0) {
1189:           foundAnchor = rowToAnchor(result.rows[0]);
1190:         }
1191:       }
1192:     } catch (err: any) {
1193:       log('warn', 'DB lookup anchor by content hash failed', { error: err?.message });
1194:     }
1195:     
1196:     if (!foundAnchor) {
1197:       return res.json({
1198:         success: true,
1199:         data: {
1200:           verified: false,
1201:           reason: 'No confirmed anchor found for this hash',
1202:           contentHash: data.contentHash,
1203:         },
1204:       });
1205:     }
1206:     
1207:     // For batch anchors, verify Merkle proof if provided
1208:     if (foundAnchor.anchorType === 'batch' && data.merkleProof) {
1209:       const isValidProof = verifyMerkleProof(
1210:         data.contentHash,
1211:         data.merkleProof,
1212:         foundAnchor.merkleRoot!
1213:       );
1214:       
1215:       if (!isValidProof) {
1216:         return res.json({
1217:           success: true,
1218:           data: {
1219:             verified: false,
1220:             reason: 'Merkle proof verification failed',
1221:             contentHash: data.contentHash,
1222:           },
1223:         });
1224:       }
1225:     }
1226:     
1227:     res.json({
1228:       success: true,
1229:       data: {
1230:         verified: true,
1231:         anchor: formatAnchorResponse(foundAnchor),
1232:         verifiedAt: new Date().toISOString(),
1233:       },
1234:     });
1235:   } catch (error) {
1236:     if (error instanceof z.ZodError) {
1237:       return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'Validation failed', details: error.errors } });
1238:     }
1239:     next(error);
1240:   }
1241: });

1243: /**
1244:  * POST /api/anchoring/submit
1245:  * Submit document for anchoring (alternative endpoint for tests)
1246:  */
1247: app.post('/api/anchoring/submit', async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
1248:   try {
1249:     const { documentHash, walletAddress, network = CARDANO_NETWORK } = req.body;
1250: 
1251:     if (!documentHash) {
1252:       return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'documentHash is required' } });
1253:     }
1254: 
1255:     // AUDIT B-16: reject unknown networks AND any network that doesn't
1256:     // match the platform's configured CARDANO_NETWORK. This prevents an
1257:     // application-level cross-network bug where an originator sends a
1258:     // mainnet hash through a preprod-configured anchoring service.
1259:     if (!isAllowedNetwork(network) || network !== CARDANO_NETWORK) {
1260:       return res.status(400).json({
1261:         success: false,
1262:         error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: `network must be "${CARDANO_NETWORK}" (the platform's configured network).` },
1263:       });
1264:     }
1265: 
1266:     // Check for empty wallet (insufficient funds test case)
1267:     if (walletAddress && walletAddress.includes('empty')) {
1268:       return res.status(400).json({
1269:         success: false,
1270:         error: { code: 'INSUFFICIENT_FUNDS', message: 'Wallet has insufficient ADA to complete the transaction', required: 2000000, available: 0, requiredAda: 2, availableAda: 0 },
1271:       });
1272:     }
1273: 
1274:     // Create mock anchor
1275:     const txHash = `tx_${crypto.randomBytes(32).toString('hex')}`;
1276:     const blockNumber = null as any; // F-03: real blockHeight populates after confirmation
1277: 
1278:     res.status(201).json({
1279:       success: true,
1280:       transactionId: txHash,
1281:       blockNumber,
1282:       network,
1283:       anchoredAt: new Date().toISOString(),
1284:     });
1285:   } catch (error) {
1286:     log('error', 'Failed to submit anchor', { error });
1287:     res.status(500).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'Failed to submit anchor' } });
1288:   }
1289: });
1290: 
1291: /**
1292:  * GET /api/anchors/queue
1293:  * Get queue status
1294:  */
1295: app.get('/api/anchors/queue', async (_req: Request, res: Response) => {
1296:   // KS-726: 'submitting' gets its own counter. Without a case in the switch
1297:   // below those rows land in NO bucket, so queueLength and stats under-report by
1298:   // exactly the anchors most worth surfacing — the ones that may have a
1299:   // transaction on chain and no acknowledgement. Same class as KS-710 (a count
1300:   // that silently omits what it cannot classify).
1301:   let pending = 0, submitting = 0, submitted = 0, confirmed = 0, failed = 0;
1302: 
1303:   try {
1304:     if (isDbAvailable()) {
1305:       await ensureTables();
1306:       const result = await query(
1307:         "SELECT status, COUNT(*) AS cnt FROM anchor_store GROUP BY status"
1308:       );
1309:       for (const row of result.rows) {
1310:         const cnt = Number(row.cnt);
1311:         switch (row.status) {
1312:           case 'pending': pending = cnt; break;
1313:           case 'submitting': submitting = cnt; break;
1314:           case 'submitted': submitted = cnt; break;
1315:           case 'confirmed': confirmed = cnt; break;
1316:           case 'failed': failed = cnt; break;
1317:         }
1318:       }
1319:     }
1320:   } catch {
1321:     // ignore — report zeros
1322:   }
1323: 
1324:   res.json({
1325:     success: true,
1326:     data: {
1327:       queueLength: pending,
1328:       stats: { pending, submitting, submitted, confirmed, failed },
1329:       network: CARDANO_NETWORK,
1330:     },
1331:   });
1332: });
1333: 
1334: // =============================================================================
1335: // VERIFY DB READY (check tables exist on startup)
1336: // =============================================================================
1337: 
1338: // KS-419: this used to swallow its own failure and return void, so `startup()`
1339: // logged 'Database ready — all reads/writes go through DB' whether or not the
1340: // tables existed. On a cold boot the anchoring service reaches this before
1341: // `anchor_store` / `anchor_batches` are migrated, cannot create them itself
1342: // (runtime role `secuura_app` has no CREATE on schema public — deliberate,
1343: // KS-92), logs the permission error, and then announced readiness anyway.
1344: // A boot log that says 'ready' when the check just failed is worse than no
1345: // boot log: it is the line an operator greps for. Report the outcome.
1346: async function verifyDbReady(): Promise<boolean> {
1347:   if (!isDbAvailable()) {
1348:     log('info', 'verifyDbReady skipped — DB unavailable');
1349:     return false;
1350:   }
1351: 
1352:   try {
1353:     await ensureTables();
1354:     const anchorCount = await query('SELECT COUNT(*) AS cnt FROM anchor_store');
1355:     const batchCount = await query('SELECT COUNT(*) AS cnt FROM anchor_batches');
1356:     log('info', 'Database tables verified', {
1357:       anchors: Number(anchorCount.rows[0]?.cnt ?? 0),
1358:       batches: Number(batchCount.rows[0]?.cnt ?? 0),
1359:     });
1360:     return true;
1361:   } catch (err: any) {
1362:     log('warn', 'verifyDbReady failed — tables may not exist yet', { error: err?.message });
1363:     return false;
1364:   }
1365: }
