496:       //      against", so resolving "would attribute to ANY matching tenant
497:       //      user". (The separate phrase "not in a *different* org" belongs to
498:       //      `:136`, which is the org-less SUBJECT, not the org-less caller --
499:       //      an earlier revision of this comment borrowed that rationale for
500:       //      this branch and attributed it to `:109`, which does not say it.)
501:       //      Here the effect is: nothing can be bound, so the column folds to
502:       //      NULL and the caller's raw value survives in metadata.
503:       //      Reachable, not hypothetical -- migration 018 drops NOT NULL on
504:       //      `svc_api_keys.organization_id` outright (the originate admin
505:       //      endpoint issuing org-less keys is the migration's stated REASON,
506:       //      not a per-key condition -- a NOT NULL cannot be dropped
507:       //      selectively), and the gateway maps a missing connector org to ''
508:       //      (api-gateway `auth.ts:232`), which `normaliseOrgId` folds to null.
509:       //   3. no claim -> unchanged. This adds a refusal; it does not make the
510:       //      field required.
511:       //
512:       // Both sides go through the SHARED `normaliseOrgId`, never a private copy:
513:       // Peter Obeden's #795 review is quoted in `provenance.ts:126` observing
514:       // that two byte-identical private copies is the arrangement that produced
515:       // the last drift, and a third copy would re-open it. The normaliser also
516:       // does the work that makes case 2 correct -- it collapses '' to null.
517:       const callerOrgId = normaliseOrgId((req as any).user?.organizationId as string | undefined);
518:       const claimedOrgId = normaliseOrgId(sOrganizationUuid);
519:       // KS-597 option B (Kam's ruling, 2026-09-11 -- "K resolves S's externalRef,
520:       // then compares"): the claim may name the acting Organisation by EITHER of
521:       // its identifiers -- K's `organizations.id`, or the `externalRef` that
522:       // Organisation was registered with. register-connector stores Platform S's
523:       // Organisation GUID there (api-gateway `routes/platform.ts`), and S sends
524:       // that GUID here, so comparing the raw claim to K's id refused every S
525:       // originate (Stuart on KS-597, 2026-09-10: 144 refusals in 45 minutes).
526:       //
527:       // The read is CALLER-SCOPED -- the caller's own row, by id and request
528:       // tenant -- and never a lookup of the claim. `externalRef` has no unique
529:       // index and register-connector's idempotency is a lockless
530:       // check-then-insert, so resolving the CLAIM could pick one of two
531:       // Organisations sharing a ref by chance; resolving the CALLER cannot, and
532:       // it is the only resolution that could ever pass this comparison.
533:       //
534:       // It goes through `req.db`, the tenant-GUC client: `organizations` is under
535:       // fail-closed RLS (migration 039), and the same read with no tenant scope
536:       // returns nothing (`ks597-b-caller-org-externalref.integration.test.ts`). A
537:       // failed read throws to the handler's 500 -- never folded into an accept,
538:       // and never disguised as this refusal.
539:       if (claimedOrgId && callerOrgId && claimedOrgId !== callerOrgId) {
540:         const callerExternalRef = await getOrganizationExternalRef(callerOrgId, getReqTenantId(req), (req as any).db);
541:         if (normaliseOrgId(callerExternalRef) !== claimedOrgId) {
542:           return res.status(403).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'FORBIDDEN', message: 'organizationUuid differs from the acting Organisation' } });
543:         }
544:       }
545:       // Only a claim bound to the acting Organisation reaches the column. The
546:       // repository still resolves it through a tenant-scoped SELECT (defence in
547:       // depth): there is no database backstop to fall back on, because the two
548:       // provisioning paths disagree -- `docker/init/01-schema.sql:125` declares
549:       // `issuer_organization_id UUID` with NO foreign key while
550:       // `migrations/001_initial-schema.sql:105` declares one, and no migration
551:       // ever adds it to an already-created table. MEASURED, not inferred: on a
552:       // database built from `docker/init` + `run-migrations.sh` (applied=48
553:       // failed=0) the column carries ZERO foreign-key constraints, against a
554:       // control showing `documents` does carry one elsewhere -- so the query
555:       // discriminates and the zero is real.
556:       // Under option B both accepted identifiers bind to the acting Organisation's
557:       // K id; S's GUID is never the column value and stays in `metadata.sIdentity`
558:       // as what the caller sent.
559:       const boundIssuerOrgId: string | null = claimedOrgId && callerOrgId ? callerOrgId : null;
560: 
561:       // Malformed contentHash — accept a bare hex digest or an optional
562:       // `sha256:` prefix (mirrors the version-create + verification paths via
563:       // CONTENT_HASH_RE); reject anything else with 400 rather than storing it.
564:       if (clientHash !== undefined && clientHash !== null) {
565:         const normalisedClientHash = String(clientHash).replace(/^sha256:/i, '').toLowerCase();
566:         if (!CONTENT_HASH_RE.test(normalisedClientHash)) {
567:           return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'contentHash must be a sha256 hex digest (64 lowercase hex chars, optional sha256: prefix)' } });
568:         }
569:       }
570: 
571:       // F-10 (audit 2026-05-15): defence-in-depth XSS escape on free-text
572:       // fields. The gateway's `sanitizeInput` middleware skips body parsing
573:       // for /api/documents (it's in `proxyPaths`, since the gateway proxies
574:       // the raw stream to originate). Without sanitisation here, raw
575:       // `<script>` / `<img onerror=...>` payloads land in the DB. React
576:       // text-encodes on render so live portals are safe today; this protects
577:       // PDF / CSV / mobile-native render paths and any future
578:       // `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` consumer.
579:       const title = typeof rawTitle === 'string' ? sanitizeString(rawTitle) : rawTitle;
580:       const description = typeof rawDescription === 'string' ? sanitizeString(rawDescription) : rawDescription;
581: 
582:       // Validate required fields.
583:       // KS-444 (closes the KS-440 hole): the published DocumentCreateRequest
584:       // declares `title` REQUIRED, but the handler accepted any body that
585:       // carried a `data` object — a fuzzer body with no title anywhere was
586:       // stored as an untitled document (negative_data_rejection 201). Require
587:       // a title: top-level `title` per the spec, or `data.title` for the
588:       // legacy `{type, data}` shape this route still supports.
589:       const docTitle = title || (typeof rawData?.title === 'string' && rawData.title ? rawData.title : undefined);
590:       if (!docTitle) {
591:         return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'Document title is required (top-level `title`, or `data.title` in the legacy shape)' } });
592:       }
593: 
594:       const docType: string = documentType || rawType || DEFAULT_DOCUMENT_TYPE;
595: 
596:       // KS-1202: the type a document is SERVED as is `data.documentType || type`
597:       // (GET /:id and the list), but the type that was checked and stored is
598:       // docType. A caller could send an allowed type with a different
599:       // `data.documentType` and have the document served, and verified, as that
600:       // other type. A `data.documentType` that is present must equal docType.
601:       // Refused rather than overwritten: `data` is the caller's blob and the
602:       // content hash covers it.
603:       const dataDocumentType = rawData && typeof rawData === 'object' ? (rawData as Record<string, unknown>).documentType : undefined;
604:       if (dataDocumentType !== undefined && dataDocumentType !== docType) {
605:         return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'data.documentType must equal the document type (documentType, or type)' } });
606:       }
607: 
608:       const data: Record<string, unknown> =
609:         rawData && typeof rawData === 'object'
610:           ? { ...rawData, title: typeof rawData.title === 'string' ? sanitizeString(rawData.title) : rawData.title, description: typeof rawData.description === 'string' ? sanitizeString(rawData.description) : rawData.description }
611:           : { title, documentType: docType, contentHash: clientHash, description };
612: 
613:       // KS-1508: `versionedById` is the ACTOR of a version, set by the server (the three version routes below). A create has no
614:       // actor-of-a-version, and the erasure of an organisation (KS-695) selects documents by this key, so a supplied value is
615:       // DROPPED, silently (a 400 would break a caller that round-trips a K document's data into a create). Logged: tenant and
616:       // actor ids only, never the value. `data` is a copy of the body, so the caller's object is not mutated.
617:       if (Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(data, 'versionedById')) {
618:         delete data.versionedById;
619:         logger.info('KS-1508: a caller-supplied data.versionedById was dropped on create', { tenantId: getReqTenantId(req), userId: owner?.id });
620:       }
621: 
622:       if (title && !data.title) data.title = title;
623:       if (description && !data.description) data.description = description;
624:       if (documentType && !data.documentType) data.documentType = documentType;
625:       // H22: persist recipientEmail into the document's data blob if the
626:       // caller passed it at the top level. The list-side filter at line
627:       // ~111 reads d.data.recipientEmail to surface the doc on the named
628:       // holder's wallet view. Without this line, top-level recipientEmail
629:       // was being dropped before save, so holders never saw their own
630:       // credentials.
631:       if (recipientEmail && typeof recipientEmail === 'string' && !data.recipientEmail) {
632:         data.recipientEmail = recipientEmail;
633:       }
634: 
635:       // KS-549: persist a top-level issuerName into the data blob (same
636:       // top-level-field pattern as recipientEmail above). It was previously
637:       // only forwarded to the anchoring request body, so connector-originated
638:       // documents (S sends issuerName top-level per PS-496) resolved
639:       // `issuer: null` on every verify — all fallback sources were empty.
640:       // The E-01 no-emails refusal sits immediately below and 400s an "@"-carrying
641:       // value; the same condition here keeps an email out of the stored blob.
642:       const topLevelIssuerName =
643:         typeof req.body?.issuerName === 'string' ? req.body.issuerName.trim() : '';
644:       // KS-1265: refuse an "@"-carrying issuerName BEFORE the save below (it was refused at the anchoring
645:       // step, AFTER saveDocument and the provenance row) - a refused create must write nothing.
646:       if (topLevelIssuerName.includes('@')) {
647:         return res.status(400).json({
648:           success: false,
649:           error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'issuerName must not contain "@" - it should be the organisation name, not a user email (audit E-01)' },
650:         });
651:       }
652:       if (topLevelIssuerName && !topLevelIssuerName.includes('@') && !data.issuerName) {
653:         data.issuerName = topLevelIssuerName;
654:       }
655: 
656:       // KS-480 §6: `onBehalfOf` is a reserved provenance key — it must never
657:       // ride inside the content-addressed data blob (erasure would break the
658:       // content hash) and a caller must not be able to plant fake provenance.
659:       // It lives in action_provenance only.
660:       delete (data as Record<string, unknown>).onBehalfOf;
661: 
662:       // KS-596: an S-supplied documentUuid becomes the registration's
663:       // external_id (the column is UNIQUE); absent, K mints exactly as before.
664:       const id = documentUuid ?? `doc-${Date.now()}-${uuidv4().slice(0, 8)}`;
665:       const now = new Date().toISOString();
666:       const hash = clientHash || generateContentHash(data);
667: 
668:       // KS-4: tenantId is required for the tenant-scoped INSERT. Captured
669:       // here so the fire-and-forget closures below (anchor poll, thread
670:       // token, etc.) can reuse it from the request scope without the route
671:       // handler needing to stay alive.
672:       const tenantId = getReqTenantId(req);
673: 
674:       // KS-596 ruled idempotency (Kam 2026-08-10, ruling b): re-POST of a
675:       // seen documentUuid with the SAME content hash returns the existing
676:       // registration (a retry can never mint a phantom second document);
677:       // the same documentUuid with DIFFERENT bytes is 409 — surfacing an
678:       // S-side duplicate-UUID bug instead of masking it (S-pack §3.2).
679:       // Tenant-scoped by construction: external_id is unique per tenant DB.
680:       const respondForExisting = async (): Promise<boolean> => {
681:         if (!documentUuid) return false;
682:         const existing = await getDocument(documentUuid, tenantId, (req as any).db);
683:         if (!existing) return false;
684:         // getDocument also matches on the internal UUID pkey; a supplied
685:         // documentUuid that shadows ANOTHER document's pkey must refuse (it
686:         // is already a working lookup key for that document), same as a
687:         // byte mismatch. Only an external_id match can be idempotent.
688:         const matchedOnExternalId = existing.id.toLowerCase() === documentUuid;
689:         if (matchedOnExternalId && normaliseHash(existing.contentHash) === normaliseHash(hash)) {
690:           res.setHeader('Location', `/api/documents/${existing.id}`);
691:           res.status(200).json({
692:             id: existing.id,
693:             documentUuid: existing.documentUuid ?? documentUuid,
694:             type: existing.type,
695:             status: existing.status,
696:             contentHash: existing.contentHash,
697:             txHash: existing.blockchain?.txHash ?? null,
698:             createdAt: existing.createdAt,
699:             requiresWalletSignature: existing.status === 'pending_signature',
700:           });
701:           return true;
702:         }
703:         res.status(409).json(DOCUMENT_UUID_CONFLICT_BODY);
704:         return true;
705:       };
706:       if (await respondForExisting()) return;
707: 
708:       const document: DocumentRecord = {
709:         id,
710:         type: docType,
711:         status: requiresWalletSignature ? 'pending_signature' : 'draft',
712:         owner,
713:         data,
714:         contentHash: hash,
715:         signatures: [],
716:         createdAt: now,
717:         updatedAt: now,
718:       };
719: 
720:       // KS-480 §6: a same-org resolved user becomes the document's
721:       // owner_user_id (true per-user attribution); the document stays
722:       // org-owned either way.
723:       // KS-596: with an S-supplied documentUuid the insert must NOT upsert —
724:       // a concurrent duplicate has to surface (inserted:false), not merge.
725:       // KS-1612 (#1568 round 2): migration 050's trigger refuses a documentUuid that is ANOTHER document's
726:       // primary key in ANY tenant — the check above sees only this tenant. It answers with that check's 409,
727:       // byte for byte, so the reply does not say which tenant holds the id. ONLY that refusal is mapped;
728:       // every other error, including any other unique violation, keeps its path (the outer catch's 500).
729:       let saved: { dbId: string; inserted: boolean };
730:       try {
731:         saved = await saveDocument(document, tenantId, (req as any).db, {
732:           ...(oboResolvedUserId ? { ownerUserIdOverride: oboResolvedUserId } : {}),
733:           ...(documentUuid ? { conflictMode: 'skip' as const } : {}),
734:           ...(sUserUuid || sOrganizationUuid
735:             ? { sIdentity: { ...(sUserUuid ? { userUuid: sUserUuid } : {}), ...(sOrganizationUuid ? { organizationUuid: sOrganizationUuid } : {}) } }
736:             : {}),
737:           // KS-597: the column carries only what the route could BIND to the
738:           // acting Organisation; `sIdentity` above still carries the caller's raw
739:           // claim for the metadata. They differ exactly when the claim could not
740:           // be bound, which is the distinction the column is for.
741:           issuerOrganizationId: boundIssuerOrgId,
742:         });
743:       } catch (saveErr) {
744:         if (isIdNamespaceCollision(saveErr)) return res.status(409).json(DOCUMENT_UUID_CONFLICT_BODY);
745:         if (isLiveAnchorIdClaim(saveErr)) return res.status(409).json(LIVE_ANCHOR_ID_CONFLICT_BODY);
746:         throw saveErr;
747:       }
748:       if (documentUuid && !saved.inserted) {
749:         // A concurrent registration with the same documentUuid won the race
750:         // between the pre-check and the insert — apply the same rule to the
751:         // row that got there first.
752:         if (await respondForExisting()) return;
753:         // The conflicting row vanished between the two checks (deleted mid-
754:         // race); treat as a retryable conflict rather than inventing a state.
755:         return res.status(409).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'CONFLICT', message: 'documentUuid registration raced a concurrent request; retry' } });
756:       }
757:       const responseDocumentUuid = documentUuid ?? saved.dbId;
758: 
759:       if (onBehalfOf) {
760:         recordActionProvenance({
761:           documentId: id,
762:           action: 'create',
763:           tenantId,
764:           organizationId: (req as any).user?.organizationId as string | undefined,
765:           connectorId: owner.id,
766:           obo: onBehalfOf,
767:           resolvedUserId: oboResolvedUserId,
768:         }).catch((provErr: Error) => logger.warn('provenance record failed', { documentId: id, error: provErr.message }));
769:       }
770:       // KS-1557: the connector's OWN creation, recorded whether or not it supplied `onBehalfOf`.
771:       // The `create` row above carries the ATTRIBUTION and exists only when the caller named a
772:       // person; this row carries the FACT that this key made this document, which is what
773:       // `wasCreatedByConnector` is asked. Without it a connector that sends no `onBehalfOf` was
774:       // refused its own document on both certify paths. After the save, like the row above, so a
775:       // refused request still writes nothing (KS-1228).
776:       recordConnectorProvenance(req, id, CONNECTOR_CREATE_ACTION);
777: 
778:       // Register in platform cross-tenant registry (fire-and-forget)
779:       // Pen-test F-04: tenant slug from req.user, NOT from raw headers.
780:       const { registerInPlatformRegistry } = require('./verification');
781:       registerInPlatformRegistry({
782:         documentId: id, contentHash: hash, tenantId,
783:         tenantSlug: (req as any).user?.tenantSlug as string | undefined,
784:         documentType: docType, title: title, status: document.status,
785:       }).catch(() => {});
786: 
787:       // Publish document.created event (fire-and-forget)
788:       publishEvent(EventTypes.DOCUMENT_CREATED, {
789:         documentId: id,
790:         documentType: docType,
791:         contentHash: hash,
792:         ownerId: owner.id,
793:         status: document.status,
794:       }).catch(() => {});
795: 
796:       // F6 Sprint 2 Phase 2 part B — fire-and-forget thread-token mint+lock.
797:       // Gated on STATE_THREAD_NFT_ENABLED to keep tADA spend opt-in until
798:       // the burn-watcher (Phase 2 part C) lands. When enabled, anchoring
799:       // mints a per-document thread NFT, locks the initial Draft datum at
800:       // the parameterised document_contract address, and persists the
801:       // (policy_id, script_address, mint_tx_hash, seed) into
802:       // state_thread_registry. Failures are logged but do NOT block document
803:       // creation — the legacy metadata-label anchor still happens below.
804:       if (
805:         process.env.STATE_THREAD_NFT_ENABLED === 'true' &&
806:         process.env.SIMULATE_ANCHORING !== 'true'
807:       ) {
808:         const bearerToken = (req.headers.authorization as string) || '';
809:         // Originator PKH: prefer the explicit field on the user; fall back
810:         // to a deterministic blake2b-derived bytes from owner.id so the
811:         // PKH is always 28 bytes hex even if not yet wired through auth.
812:         const originatorPkhHex =
813:           ((req as any).user?.pkh as string) ||
814:           crypto.createHash('sha256').update(owner.id).digest().toString('hex').slice(0, 56);
815:         const metadataHashHex = crypto
816:           .createHash('sha256')
817:           .update(JSON.stringify(data))
818:           .digest()
819:           .toString('hex');
820: 
821:         mintAndRegisterThreadToken({
822:           documentId: id,
823:           documentHashHex: hash.replace(/^sha256:/i, '').toLowerCase(),
824:           originatorPkhHex,
825:           documentType: docType,
826:           metadataHashHex,
827:           deploymentIdHex: '',
828:           createdAtMs: Date.now(),
829:           bearerToken,
830:         })
831:           .then(async (entry) => {
832:             logger.info('thread-token minted at document create', {
833:               documentId: id,
834:               policyId: entry.policyId,
835:               mintTxHash: entry.mintTxHash,
836:             });
837:             // Surface the thread-token info on the document record so
838:             // dashboards can show it without a separate lookup. The
839:             // canonical truth is state_thread_registry; this is a cache.
840:             // KS-1124 O1: a PATCH of the one key this writer owns, merged into the stored blob INSIDE the UPDATE
841:             // (`mergeBlockchain`), where the row lock serialises it against the anchor writers. It used to read the
842:             // blob here and write the whole thing back, which narrowed the race but left a window: an anchor
843:             // write landing between the read and the write was erased, and one landing after replaced the token.
844:             updateDocument(id, tenantId, {
845:               blockchain: {
846:                 threadToken: {
847:                   policyId: entry.policyId,
848:                   scriptAddress: entry.scriptAddress,
849:                   mintTxHash: entry.mintTxHash,
850:                   network: entry.network,
851:                 },
852:               },
853:             }, undefined, { mergeBlockchain: true }).catch(() => {});
854:           })
855:           .catch((err: any) => {
856:             logger.warn('thread-token mint failed (document still created via legacy path)', {
857:               documentId: id,
858:               error: err?.message,
859:             });
860:           });
861:       }
862: 
863:       // Auto-anchor on blockchain (fire-and-forget — does not block the response)
864:       const anchoringBaseUrl = process.env.ANCHORING_SERVICE_URL || 'http://anchoring:4005';
865:       const anchorUrl = `${anchoringBaseUrl}/api/anchors`;
866:       const simulateAnchoring = process.env.SIMULATE_ANCHORING === 'true';
867:       // F-CHAIN-VERIFY-AUTH-01 / -02 (2026-05-02): the anchoring service
868:       // mounts authenticate() on /api/* — every call from originate must
869:       // forward the caller's bearer or anchoring returns 500 "No token
870:       // provided". Capture once at the top of the auto-anchor scope so
871:       // every fetch below uses the same value (poll loop, anchor POST,
872:       // status GET).
873:       const anchoringAuth = (req.headers.authorization as string) || '';
874:       // The `x-emitter-internal` marker is added, SIGNED, at the anchor POST below
875:       // (KS-1717): the signature covers the exact body bytes, so it is minted there.
876:       const anchoringHeaders: Record<string, string> = {
877:         ...(anchoringAuth ? { Authorization: anchoringAuth } : {}),
878:       };
879:       // KS-199: issuerName (the organisation name) is OPTIONAL — a user without
880:       // an organisation (public tenant) may upload without it, and we anchor
881:       // under the neutral DEFAULT_ISSUER_NAME rather than forcing a dummy org.
882:       // Audit E-01 still holds: we never fall back to the user's email/PII —
883:       // previously a missing org meant x-user-email could land verbatim on the
884:       // Cardano chain (immutable, public, forever). The gateway also strips x-*
885:       // trust headers (commit c796138f0). The default is a fixed non-PII label.
886:       const issuerOrgId = (req.body && (req.body.issuerOrgId || req.body.organizationId)) || owner.id;
887:       const suppliedIssuerName = req.body && typeof req.body.issuerName === 'string'
888:         ? req.body.issuerName.trim()
889:         : '';
890:       // KS-1291: the E-01 "@" refusal is NOT repeated here. KS-1265 (#1174) moved it above
891:       // `saveDocument` so a refused create writes nothing, and this copy became unreachable:
892:       // both read the same `req.body.issuerName`, nothing reassigns that field, and the early
893:       // guard is in this same block, so every path reaching this line has already passed it.
894:       // Do not re-add a check here — a refusal at this point would answer 400 for a document
895:       // that has already been saved, which is the defect KS-1265 fixed.
896:       const issuerName = suppliedIssuerName.length > 0 ? suppliedIssuerName : DEFAULT_ISSUER_NAME;
897: 
898:       // KS-1385: carry Platform-S's `identity` block through to the anchor. Kam's ruling on
899:       // card `secuura-ks1385-anchor-identity-needs-ps895-ruling-1013`, verbatim: "Latest
900:       // event's identity wins". All SIX sites that carry S's block forward it, and anchoring's
901:       // `anchor_store` upsert now updates `metadata_payload`, so the stored identity is the one
902:       // belonging to the event that produced the stored transaction hash.
903:       //
904:       // CONSEQUENCE, stated because it is a deliberate choice and not an oversight: an earlier
905:       // verb's identity is OVERWRITTEN by a later one on the same (document_id, network) row.
906:       // What travels with it is the WHOLE `secuura` object -- `hash`, `certId`, `issuerOrgId`,
907:       // `timestamp`, `environment`, `occurredAt`, `identityCommitment` (KS-721; S compares it
908:       // for equality) and the seven identity fields -- plus `metadata.documentType`.
909:       //
910:       // ⚠ CORRECTED on the pull request 1549 gate (INF-1): this comment, its sibling in
911:       // anchoring and the pull request body all said "documentType and signatureHash". There is
912:       // NO `signatureHash` in the anchored payload; it had 0 hits in anchoring and originate
913:       // non-test source apart from those new comments. It was written without being checked and
914:       // is named here so the correction outlives the round.
915:       //
916:       // The alternative — keeping the first write — presents the UPLOADER's identity beside a
917:       // later event's transaction, which is the false attribution of the KS-1119 / KS-1561
918:       // family. PS-895 / KS-1384 (per-document vs per-event anchoring) remain open and are the
919:       // structural answer; this is the ruled behaviour until one lands.
920:       //
921:       // WHO may overwrite is a separate question and is answered in
922:       // anchoring's `anchorOverwriteGuard.ts`, not here: the issuer's organisation, or anyone in the
923:       // custodian's organisation (Kam's (c)), including on a document with no issuer (Kam's (b), which is
924:       // every certification), always in the caller's own tenant. That tenant bound holds across the two
925:       // id namespaces only because migration 050 keeps them disjoint (KS-1612).
926:       //
927:       // The shape is anchoring's to judge: see utils/anchorIdentity.ts for why the block is
928:       // forwarded verbatim rather than re-validated here.
929:       const buildAnchorBody = () => JSON.stringify({
930:         documentId: id,
931:         contentHash: hash,
932:         network: process.env.CARDANO_NETWORK || 'preprod',
933:         environment: process.env.SECUURA_ENVIRONMENT || process.env.NODE_ENV || 'unknown',
934:         issuerOrgId,
935:         issuerName,
936:         certId: id,
937:         ...anchorIdentityFields(req.body),
938:       });
939: 
940:       // After anchoring accepts the request, poll the anchor resource until it's
941:       // confirmed on-chain (real txHash + blockHeight) or terminally failed
942:       // (KS-535: the fail-closed `anchor_failed` shape — previously a failed
943:       // anchor produced no write and the document claimed `anchored` forever).
944:       // Extracted to services/anchorStateSync.ts so the manual retry route
945:       // (POST /:id/anchor) shares it.
946: 
947:       if (simulateAnchoring) {
948:         const simData = {
949:           txHash: `tx_sim_${crypto.randomBytes(32).toString('hex')}`,
950:           blockHeight: 0,
951:           anchoredAt: new Date().toISOString(),
952:           simulated: true,
953:         };
954:         updateDocument(id, tenantId, { blockchain: simData, status: 'anchored' }).catch(() => {});
955:         logger.warn('SIMULATE_ANCHORING=true — simulated anchor applied immediately', { documentId: id });
956:       } else {
957:         // Cardano anchor — fire-and-forget relative to the HTTP response.
958:         // KS-566 / KS-1717: `originate-create` marks K's own hop so anchoring does
959:         // not write a second provenance row — POST /api/documents already wrote
960:         // the `create` row for this same user action. anchoring honours it only
961:         // when it verifies the signature, which covers these exact body bytes
962:         // (unsigned when EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET is unset: anchoring then records
963:         // the row). It also binds the caller's bearer forwarded below, so the hop
964:         // cannot be replayed under another connector's token. See emitLifecycleAnchor.
965:         const anchorBody = buildAnchorBody();
966:         fetch(anchorUrl, {
967:           method: 'POST',
968:           headers: {
969:             'Content-Type': 'application/json',
970:             ...anchoringHeaders,
971:             ...signEmitterHop({
972:               secret: process.env.EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET,
973:               marker: 'originate-create',
974:               method: 'POST',
975:               url: anchorUrl,
976:               authorization: anchoringAuth || undefined, // exactly what `anchoringHeaders` sends
977:               body: anchorBody,
978:             }),
979:           },
980:           body: anchorBody,
981:         })
982:           .then(async (anchorResp) => {
983:             if (!anchorResp.ok) {
984:               // KS-1385: carry the upstream REASON, not only its status. This path now
985:               // forwards a caller-supplied `identity` block that anchoring validates, so
986:               // "Anchoring service returned 400" would record a failure nobody can act on:
987:               // which field was malformed is in the body, and the body is what gets stored
988:               // in `blockchain.error` by the `.catch` below. Bounded and best-effort — a
989:               // body that cannot be read must not replace the status with nothing.
990:               let detail: string;
991:               try { detail = (await anchorResp.text()).slice(0, 500); } catch { detail = ''; }
992:               throw new Error(
993:                 `Anchoring service returned ${anchorResp.status}` + (detail ? `: ${detail}` : ''),
994:               );
995:             }
996:             const anchorResult = (await anchorResp.json()) as any;
997:             const anchorData = anchorResult.data || anchorResult;
998:             const anchorId = anchorData.id;
999:             const initial = {
1000:               txHash: anchorData.txHash || null,
1001:               blockHeight: anchorData.blockHeight || 0,
1002:               anchoredAt: anchorData.anchoredAt || anchorData.createdAt || new Date().toISOString(),
1003:               status: anchorData.status || 'pending',
1004:               anchorId,
1005:               // KS-587 (document-blob leg): carry the anchor's simulation
1006:               // declaration onto the blob from the first write, so a caller
1007:               // reading only the document can tell a mock anchor is not on
1008:               // chain (KS-589 D1) and presentBlockchainHonestly has its input
1009:               // (KS-589 D4). Empty for a real anchor.
1010:               ...simulatedFieldsFromAnchor(anchorData),
1011:             };
1012:             // KS-521: same terminal-status guard as the poll below — this
1013:             // write is fire-and-forget relative to the 201, so an immediate
1014:             // revoke can race it.
1015:             await updateDocument(id, tenantId, { blockchain: initial, status: 'anchored' }, undefined, { preserveTerminalStatuses: true });
1016:             logger.info('Document anchor request accepted — awaiting on-chain confirmation', { documentId: id, anchorId });
1017:             if (anchorId) pollAnchorUntilConfirmed({ anchorId, documentId: id, tenantId, authHeader: anchoringAuth });
1018:           })
1019:           .catch((err: any) => {
1020:             // KS-520: fail CLOSED. Simulation is an explicit opt-in — the
1021:             // SIMULATE_ANCHORING === 'true' branch above never reaches this
1022:             // fetch — so an anchoring failure here must never fabricate a
1023:             // tx_sim_ hash and mark the document anchored: the fabricated
1024:             // hash corresponds to no Cardano transaction and a caller cannot
1025:             // distinguish it from a genuine anchor. Record a retryable
1026:             // failure state instead; the document stays un-anchored (and the
1027:             // public verifier reports it unverified) until a real anchor
1028:             // lands via POST /api/documents/:id/anchor or a recreate.
1029:             logger.error('Auto-anchor failed — document saved without anchor (fail closed; retry via POST /api/documents/:id/anchor)', { documentId: id, error: err?.message });
1030:             updateDocument(id, tenantId, {
1031:               blockchain: {
1032:                 status: 'anchor_failed',
1033:                 txHash: null,
1034:                 blockHeight: 0,
1035:                 error: err?.message || 'anchoring unavailable',
1036:                 failedAt: new Date().toISOString(),
1037:               },
1038:             }, undefined, { preserveTerminalStatuses: true }).catch(() => {});
1039:           });
1040:       }
1041: 
1042:       // KS-154: emit RFC 7231 Location header on 201 — points at the
1043:       // canonical resource URL so REST clients can follow without parsing
1044:       // the body. The body still carries `id` for backward compatibility.
1045:       res.setHeader('Location', `/api/documents/${document.id}`);
1046:       res.status(201).json({
1047:         id: document.id,
1048:         // KS-596 Phase B: the canonical UUID rides on every registration
1049:         // response next to the legacy id — the S-supplied documentUuid, or
1050:         // the row's own UUID key (both already resolve via GET /:id).
1051:         documentUuid: responseDocumentUuid,
1052:         type: document.type,
1053:         status: document.status,
1054:         contentHash: document.contentHash,
1055:         // F-DOC-RESP-SHAPE-01 (2026-05-02): always include txHash so the
1056:         // client can distinguish "field absent / unsupported" from "anchor
1057:         // queued, not yet on chain". Auto-anchor is fire-and-forget; the
1058:         // tx hash arrives later (poll the doc by id, or subscribe to
1059:         // events). For SIMULATE_ANCHORING the tx is set synchronously
1060:         // below in updateDocument(); the client will see it on the next
1061:         // GET. For real anchoring on demo, expect the field to remain
1062:         // null until Cardano confirms (~30-60s typical on Preview).
1063:         txHash: null,
1064:         createdAt: document.createdAt,
1065:         requiresWalletSignature: !!requiresWalletSignature,
1066:       });
1067:     } catch (error) {
1068:       logger.error('Failed to create document', { error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error) });
1069:       res.status(500).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'Failed to create document' } });
1070:     }
1071:   },
1072: );
1073: 
1074: /**
1075:  * GET /api/documents/:id
1076:  * Get document details
1077:  */
1078: documentsRouter.get(
1079:   '/:id',
1080:   [param('id').notEmpty().withMessage('Document ID is required')],
1081:   async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
1082:     try {
1083:       const { id } = req.params;
1084:       let document: any = null;
1085: 
1086:       // KS-4: tenant-scoped lookup via req.tenantId (the effective tenant
1087:       // after extractTenantContext resolves any X-Tenant-Override). A
1088:       // super_admin asserting BUPA context will find 0 rows for an
1089:       // Oxford-owned doc — the SELECT now has AND tenant_id = $BUPA.
1090:       const tenantId = getReqTenantId(req);
1091:       try {
1092:         document = await getDocument(id, tenantId, (req as any).db);
1093:       } catch (dbErr: any) {
1094:         // If the table doesn't exist in this DB context, continue to fallback
1095:         if (!dbErr?.message?.includes('does not exist')) throw dbErr;
1096:       }
1097: 
1098:       // Cross-tenant fallback: only for admins — search the platform_document_registry
1099:       // Pen-test F-04: read role from authenticated req.user, NOT raw headers.
1100:       const userRole = (req as any).user?.role as string | undefined;
1101:       const isAdmin = userRole === 'system_admin' || userRole === 'SYSTEM_ADMIN' || userRole === 'super_admin';
1102:       // BUG-ISOLATION-001 fix (2026-05-02): if the admin explicitly set
1103:       // X-Tenant-Override (i.e., effective tenant ≠ JWT tenant), they
1104:       // are scoped to that override and the cross-tenant fallback MUST
1105:       // NOT leak documents from other tenants.
1106:       const userJwtTenant = (req as any).user?.tenantId as string | undefined;
1107:       const adminUsingOverride = userJwtTenant && userJwtTenant !== tenantId;
1108:       if (!document && isAdmin && !adminUsingOverride) {
1109:         try {
1110:           const { getTenantManager, createPoolProxy } = require('../db');
1111:           const mgr = getTenantManager?.();
1112:           if (mgr) {
1113:             const platformPool = mgr.getPlatformPool?.();
1114:             if (platformPool) {
1115:               const regResult = await platformPool.query(
1116:                 'SELECT tenant_id FROM platform_document_registry WHERE document_id = $1 LIMIT 1',
1117:                 [id]
1118:               ).catch(() => ({ rows: [] }));
1119:               if (regResult.rows.length > 0) {
1120:                 const registryTenant = regResult.rows[0].tenant_id as string;
1121:                 const tenantPool = mgr.getPool(registryTenant);
1122:                 const tenantDb = createPoolProxy ? createPoolProxy(tenantPool) : tenantPool;
1123:                 // Pass the registry tenant id so the SELECT scopes to the
1124:                 // doc's actual home tenant, not the requesting admin's.
1125:                 // KS-458: the registry row's tenant must WIN over the request
1126:                 // ALS (the admin's own tenant) — run the lookup under the
1127:                 // registry tenant so createPoolProxy bundles THAT tenant's
1128:                 // GUC into the query's transaction (fail-closed RLS).
1129:                 document = await runWithTenantId(registryTenant, () => getDocument(id, registryTenant, tenantDb));
1130:               }
1131:             }
1132:           }
1133:         } catch {
1134:           // Cross-tenant lookup not available — continue with 404
1135:         }
1136:       }
1137: 
1138:       if (!document) {
1139:         return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Document not found' } });
1140:       }
1141: 
1142:       // Ownership check: the owner, an admin, or the designated recipient can view
1143:       // Pen-test F-04: read from authenticated req.user, NOT raw headers.
1144:       const authUser = (req as any).user;
1145:       const userId = authUser?.userId as string | undefined;
1146:       const userEmail = authUser?.email as string | undefined;
1147:       const recipientEmail = (document.data as Record<string, unknown>)?.recipientEmail as string | undefined;
1148:       const isRecipient = userEmail && recipientEmail && userEmail.toLowerCase() === recipientEmail.toLowerCase();
1149:       if (userId && document.owner?.id && document.owner.id !== userId && !isAdmin && !isRecipient) {
1150:         // KS-480 §6: onBehalfOf attribution sets owner_user_id, which would
1151:         // 404-cloak the very connector key that created the document — and S
1152:         // drives the whole lifecycle through this id. The CREATING connector
1153:         // (matched via its creation provenance row) may read it back; any
1154:         // other caller stays 404-cloaked like any non-owner.
1155:         const isCreatingConnector = userRole === 'connector' && await wasCreatedByConnector(document.id, userId);
1156:         if (!isCreatingConnector) {
1157:           // Return 404 (not 403) to avoid leaking document existence to non-owners
1158:           return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Document not found' } });
1159:         }
1160:       }
1161: 
1162:       // KS-535: read-time self-healing for stale anchor claims. The in-process
1163:       // confirmation poller dies with the service (restart) and its window is
1164:       // shorter than anchoring's retry chain, so a document can sit claiming
1165:       // an in-flight (or failed) anchor state that anchor_store has long since
1166:       // resolved. Re-check stale rows against anchor_store and serve the truth.
1167:       document = await reconcileDocumentAnchorState(document, tenantId, (req.headers.authorization as string) || '');
1168: 
1169:       const walletSignature = document.signatures.length > 0
1170:         ? {
1171:             address: document.signatures[0].walletAddress,
1172:             signature: document.signatures[0].signature,
1173:             signedAt: document.signatures[0].signedAt,
1174:           }
1175:         : undefined;
1176: 
1177:       // KS-480 §6: provenance is first-class — echo the creation attribution
1178:       // (decrypted; pseudonymised rows echo externalRef only) so S and
1179:       // auditors can see who acted. Null for non-connector documents.
1180:       const createdOnBehalfOf = await getCreationProvenance(document.id);
1181: 
1182:       res.json({
1183:         id: document.id,
1184:         // KS-596 Phase B: canonical UUID alongside the legacy id on reads.
1185:         documentUuid: document.documentUuid ?? null,
1186:         type: document.type,
1187:         title: (document.data as Record<string, unknown>)?.title || document.type || 'Untitled',
1188:         documentType: (document.data as Record<string, unknown>)?.documentType || document.type,
1189:         description: (document.data as Record<string, unknown>)?.description,
1190:         status: document.status,
1191:         owner: document.owner,
1192:         data: document.data,
1193:         contentHash: document.contentHash,
1194:         signatures: document.signatures,
1195:         walletSignature,
1196:         blockchain: {
1197:           // KS-520: `anchored` is status-derived, not presence-derived — the
1198:           // fail-closed state records a blockchain object whose status is
1199:           // 'anchor_failed', and that must not read as anchored.
1200:           anchored: !!document.blockchain && document.blockchain.status !== 'anchor_failed',
1201:           // KS-587 (document-blob leg): the KS-522 rules applied at the serve
1202:           // boundary too, so blobs written BEFORE the writer fixes (e.g. the
1203:           // SIMULATE_ANCHORING branch's stored tx_sim_ hash) still read
1204:           // honestly — placeholder never presented as txHash, `simulated`
1205:           // declared. No-op for real anchors and fail-closed states.
1206:           ...(composeHonestBlockchainBlob(document.blockchain as Record<string, unknown> | null | undefined) || {}),
1207:         },
1208:         createdAt: document.createdAt,
1209:         updatedAt: document.updatedAt,
1210:         ...(createdOnBehalfOf ? { createdOnBehalfOf } : {}),
1211:       });
1212:     } catch (error) {
1213:       logger.error('Failed to get document', { error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error) });
1214:       res.status(500).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'Failed to get document' } });
1215:     }
1216:   },
1217: );
1218: 
1219: /**
1220:  * POST /api/documents/:id/sign/request
1221:  * Request document signing — returns hash and nonce for wallet signing
1222:  */
1223: documentsRouter.post(
1224:   '/:id/sign/request',
1225:   [param('id').notEmpty().withMessage('Document ID is required')],
1226:   async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
1227:     try {
1228:       const user = getUserFromRequest(req);
1229:       if (!user) {
1230:         return res.status(401).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'UNAUTHORIZED', message: 'Authentication required' } });
1231:       }
1232: 
1233:       const { id } = req.params;
1234:       const { walletAddress } = req.body;
1235: 
1236:       const document = await getDocument(id, getReqTenantId(req), (req as any).db);
1237:       if (!document) {
1238:         return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Document not found' } });
1239:       }
1240: 
1241:       const nonce = crypto.randomBytes(16).toString('hex');
1242:       const hash = document.contentHash;
1243:       const expiresAt = new Date(Date.now() + 5 * 60 * 1000).toISOString();
1244: 
1245:       saveSigningRequest(nonce, {
1246:         documentId: id,
1247:         walletAddress: walletAddress || user.walletAddress || '',
1248:         hash,
1249:         nonce,
1250:         expiresAt,
1251:       });
1252: 
1253:       res.json({
1254:         hash,
1255:         nonce,
1256:         message: `Sign this document hash: ${hash}`,
1257:         expiresAt,
1258:         documentId: id,
1259:       });
1260:     } catch (error) {
1261:       logger.error('Failed to create signing request', { error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error) });
1262:       res.status(500).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'Failed to create signing request' } });
1263:     }
1264:   },
1265: );
1266: 
1267: /**
1268:  * POST /api/documents/:id/sign
1269:  * Submit wallet signature for document
1270:  */
1271: documentsRouter.post(
1272:   '/:id/sign',
1273:   [
1274:     param('id').notEmpty().withMessage('Document ID is required'),
1275:     body('signature').notEmpty().withMessage('Signature is required'),
1276:     body('nonce').notEmpty().withMessage('Nonce is required'),
1277:   ],
1278:   async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
1279:     try {
1280:       const user = getUserFromRequest(req);
1281:       if (!user) {
1282:         return res.status(401).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'UNAUTHORIZED', message: 'Authentication required' } });
1283:       }
1284: 
1285:       const errors = validationResult(req);
1286:       if (!errors.isEmpty()) {
1287:         return res.status(400).json({ errors: errors.array() });
1288:       }
1289: 
1290:       const { id } = req.params;
1291:       const { signature, nonce, walletAddress } = req.body;
1292:       const tenantId = getReqTenantId(req);
1293: 
1294:       const document = await getDocument(id, tenantId, (req as any).db);
1295:       if (!document) {
1296:         return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Document not found' } });
1297:       }
1298: 
1299:       const signingRequest = getSigningRequest(nonce);
1300:       if (!signingRequest) {
1301:         return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'Invalid or expired signing request' } });
1302:       }
1303: 
1304:       if (signingRequest.documentId !== id) {
1305:         return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'Signing request does not match document' } });
1306:       }
1307: 
1308:       if (new Date(signingRequest.expiresAt) < new Date()) {
1309:         deleteSigningRequest(nonce);
1310:         return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'Signing request has expired' } });
1311:       }
1312: 
1313:       const isValidSignature = signature.startsWith('mock_signature_') || signature.length > 50;
1314:       if (!isValidSignature) {
1315:         return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'Invalid signature' } });
1316:       }
1317: 
1318:       const now = new Date().toISOString();
1319:       document.signatures.push({
1320:         signerId: user.id,
1321:         walletAddress: walletAddress || signingRequest.walletAddress,
1322:         signature,
1323:         signedAt: now,
1324:       });
1325: 
1326:       await updateDocument(id, tenantId, {
1327:         signatures: document.signatures,
1328:         status: 'signed',
1329:       });
1330: 
1331:       deleteSigningRequest(nonce);
1332: 
1333:       res.json({
1334:         id: document.id,
1335:         status: 'signed',
1336:         signed: true,
1337:         signature,
1338:         signatureCount: document.signatures.length,
1339:         signedAt: now,
1340:       });
1341:     } catch (error) {
1342:       logger.error('Failed to sign document', { error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error) });
1343:       res.status(500).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'Failed to sign document' } });
1344:     }
1345:   },
1346: );
1347: 
1348: /**
1349:  * Emit (or resolve) the on-chain anchor for a non-mutating lifecycle event
1350:  * (share / transfer-custody / revoke) — KS-319. Anchoring is idempotent per
1351:  * (document_id, network): re-anchoring an already-anchored document returns its
1352:  * EXISTING anchor id, so the event is recorded against the existing document
1353:  * (no new version / parent_document_id fork). Returns a resolvable anchor id
1354:  * (GET /api/anchors/{id}) for the caller to surface, so Platform-S can persist
1355:  * it as ExternalRef and its CardanoAnchorRefresher can upgrade it to a tx hash.
1356:  * Shared so PS-123's generic lifecycle endpoint can reuse it (KS-274).
1357:  *
1358:  * Best-effort: the domain action (share/custody/revoke) has already committed by
1359:  * the time this runs, so an anchoring outage degrades to {anchorId:null} + a
1360:  * warning rather than failing the verb — S then falls back to POST /api/anchors
1361:  * (its existing HTTP-failure path).
1362:  */
1363: async function emitLifecycleAnchor(params: {
1364:   documentId: string;
1365:   contentHash: string;
1366:   authHeader?: string;
1367:   // KS-1385 site 3: the caller's `identity` block, forwarded verbatim. Raw on purpose —
1368:   // utils/anchorIdentity.ts is the one place that decides whether the key is sent.
1369:   identity?: unknown;
1370:   // KS-387: widened beyond the three per-verb endpoints — the generic
1371:   // /lifecycle-events route reuses this one shared emitter (the KS-319 note)
1372:   // for its whole action vocabulary rather than growing a second implementation.
1373:   eventType: 'share' | 'transfer-custody' | 'revoke' | LifecycleEventAction;
1374: }): Promise<{ anchorId: string | null; txHash: string | null; status: string }> {
1375:   const { documentId, contentHash, authHeader, eventType, identity } = params;
1376:   try {
1377:     // Mirror POST /:id/anchor: forward the caller's bearer to anchoring's
1378:     // authenticate() middleware; ANCHORING_SERVICE_URL is injected on Azure,
1379:     // the service-name URL is the Docker Compose fallback.
1380:     const anchoringBase = process.env.ANCHORING_SERVICE_URL || 'http://anchoring:4005';
1381:     const anchorUrl = `${anchoringBase}/api/anchors`;
1382:     // The signature below covers these exact bytes, so the body is built once.
1383:     const anchorBody = JSON.stringify({
1384:       documentId,
1385:       // KS-282: anchoring requires a bare 64-hex digest; strip any sha256: prefix.
1386:       contentHash: String(contentHash || '').replace(/^sha256:/i, '').toLowerCase(),
1387:       network: process.env.CARDANO_NETWORK || 'preprod',
1388:       ...anchorIdentityFields({ identity }),
1389:     });
1390:     const resp = await fetch(anchorUrl, {
1391:       method: 'POST',
1392:       headers: {
1393:         'Content-Type': 'application/json',
1394:         ...(authHeader ? { Authorization: authHeader } : {}),
1395:         // KS-566: this is K's OWN hop, not Platform-S's flat-anchor fallback.
1396:         // The calling verb (share / transfer-custody / revoke / lifecycle) has
1397:         // already written its own action_provenance row, so anchoring must not
1398:         // write a second one for the same user action.
1399:         // KS-1717: the marker is not trusted by presence. The gateway's
1400:         // `x-emitter-*` strip covers only requests that pass through it; a
1401:         // caller reaching anchoring directly could set the header itself. So
1402:         // anchoring honours it only when this HMAC verifies — over the body and
1403:         // the bearer below as sent (unsigned when EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET is
1404:         // unset: anchoring then records the row).
1405:         ...signEmitterHop({
1406:           secret: process.env.EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET,
1407:           marker: 'originate-lifecycle',
1408:           method: 'POST',
1409:           url: anchorUrl,
1410:           authorization: authHeader || undefined, // exactly what the header above sends
1411:           body: anchorBody,
1412:         }),
1413:       },
1414:       body: anchorBody,
1415:     });
1416:     if (!resp.ok) {
1417:       throw new Error(`Anchoring service returned ${resp.status}`);
1418:     }
1419:     const result = (await resp.json()) as any;
1420:     const ad = result.data || result;
1421:     logger.info('Lifecycle event anchored', { documentId, eventType, anchorId: ad.id, txHash: ad.txHash || null });
1422:     return { anchorId: ad.id ?? null, txHash: ad.txHash ?? null, status: ad.status || 'submitted' };
1423:   } catch (err: any) {
1424:     logger.warn('Lifecycle anchor emission failed — event recorded without a resolvable anchor id (S falls back to /api/anchors)', {
1425:       documentId,
1426:       eventType,
1427:       error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err),
1428:     });
1429:     return { anchorId: null, txHash: null, status: 'pending' };
1430:   }
1431: }
1432: 
1433: /**
1434:  * POST /api/documents/:id/anchor
1435:  * Anchor document on blockchain via the anchoring service
1436:  */
1437: documentsRouter.post(
1438:   '/:id/anchor',
1439:   [param('id').notEmpty().withMessage('Document ID is required')],
1440:   async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
1441:     try {
1442:       const user = getUserFromRequest(req);
1443:       if (!user) {
1444:         return res.status(401).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'UNAUTHORIZED', message: 'Authentication required' } });
1445:       }
1446: 
1447:       const { id } = req.params;
1448:       const tenantId = getReqTenantId(req);
1449:       const document = await getDocument(id, tenantId, (req as any).db);
1450: 
1451:       if (!document) {
1452:         return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Document not found' } });
1453:       }
1454: 
1455:       // KS-520: a document whose auto-anchor FAILED (fail-closed state,
1456:       // blockchain.status === 'anchor_failed') is not anchored — it must be
1457:       // retryable through this route rather than 400ing as "already anchored".
1458:       if (document.blockchain && document.blockchain.status !== 'anchor_failed') {
1459:         return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'Document is already anchored' } });
1460:       }
1461: 
1462:       const now = new Date().toISOString();
1463:       let blockchainData: { txHash: string | null; blockHeight: number | null; anchoredAt: string | null; anchorId?: string; simulated?: boolean; simulatedTxRef?: string | null; confidence?: string; status?: string };
1464: 
1465:       // Attempt to call the real anchoring service.
1466:       // F-CHAIN-VERIFY-AUTH-01 / -02: forward bearer to anchoring's
1467:       // authenticate() middleware (else 500 "No token provided"). Use
1468:       // ANCHORING_SERVICE_URL when set (services.bicep injects the
1469:       // internal Container Apps URL); the hard-coded service-name URL is
1470:       // a Docker Compose fallback for local dev.
1471:       try {
1472:         const anchoringBase = process.env.ANCHORING_SERVICE_URL || 'http://anchoring:4005';
1473:         const anchoringAuth = (req.headers.authorization as string) || '';
1474:         const anchorResp = await fetch(`${anchoringBase}/api/anchors`, {
1475:           method: 'POST',
1476:           headers: {
1477:             'Content-Type': 'application/json',
1478:             ...(anchoringAuth ? { Authorization: anchoringAuth } : {}),
1479:           },
1480:           body: JSON.stringify({
1481:             documentId: id,
1482:             // KS-282: the anchoring service requires a bare 64-hex digest, but a
1483:             // document's stored contentHash may carry a `sha256:` prefix (every
1484:             // version does — see the /version handler). Strip it so the version's
1485:             // new hash actually anchors instead of 400'ing into the dev fallback.
1486:             contentHash: String(document.contentHash || '').replace(/^sha256:/i, '').toLowerCase(),
1487:             network: process.env.CARDANO_NETWORK || 'preprod',
1488:             // KS-1385 site 2: the re-anchor path carries S's identity too.
1489:             ...anchorIdentityFields(req.body),
1490:           }),
1491:         });
1492: 
1493:         if (anchorResp.ok) {
1494:           const anchorResult = await anchorResp.json() as any;
1495:           const ad = anchorResult.data || anchorResult;
1496:           blockchainData = {
1497:             // KS-281: capture the anchor id so the document's blockchain block
1498:             // carries a resolvable anchorId (GET /api/anchors/{id}). The 202 body
1499:             // has the id but not yet a txHash (the tx confirms asynchronously).
1500:             anchorId: ad.id,
1501:             txHash: ad.txHash || null,
1502:             blockHeight: ad.blockHeight || 0,
1503:             anchoredAt: ad.anchoredAt || ad.createdAt || now,
1504:             // KS-587 (document-blob leg): same declaration as the create path.
1505:             ...simulatedFieldsFromAnchor(ad),
1506:           };
1507:           logger.info('Document anchored via anchoring service', { documentId: id, anchorId: ad.id, txHash: blockchainData.txHash });
1508:         } else {
1509:           throw new Error(`Anchoring service returned ${anchorResp.status}`);
1510:         }
1511:       } catch (anchorErr: any) {
1512:         if (process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production') {
1513:           logger.error('Anchoring service unavailable in production', { documentId: id, error: anchorErr instanceof Error ? anchorErr.message : String(anchorErr) });
1514:           return res.status(503).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE', message: 'Blockchain anchoring service is temporarily unavailable. Please try again later.' } });
1515:         }
1516:         logger.warn('Anchoring service unavailable — recording pending-onchain state (dev mode)', { documentId: id, error: anchorErr instanceof Error ? anchorErr.message : String(anchorErr) });
1517:         // Pen-test F-03: was generating a fake blockHeight via Math.random()
1518:         // and persisting `simulated: true`. The simulated flag was easy to
1519:         // miss downstream and the verifier UI didn't surface it. New shape
1520:         // sets the chain-only fields to null and adds an explicit
1521:         // `confidence: 'pending-onchain'` that the verifier UI must honour.
1522:         blockchainData = {
1523:           txHash: null,
1524:           blockHeight: null,
1525:           anchoredAt: null,
1526:           confidence: 'pending-onchain',
1527:           // KS-1124 F4 (the retry route's copy): a statusless `pending-onchain` blob shows as pending forever, because
1528:           // the gateway reads `status`, not `confidence`. `failed` is the value its closed mapping treats as
1529:           // off-chain-only, the same one the two certification sites save (#1333). Non-production only: production 503s above.
1530:           status: 'failed',
1531:         };
1532:       }
1533: 
1534:       await updateDocument(id, tenantId, {
1535:         blockchain: blockchainData,
1536:         status: 'anchored',
1537:       });
1538: 
1539:       // KS-535: this retry path previously never polled — a retried anchor
1540:       // could neither gain its real txHash nor report a terminal on-chain
1541:       // failure (the same permanent-'anchored' mislabel as the create path).
1542:       // Same poller as create: confirms → txHash/blockHeight; terminal
1543:       // failure → the KS-520 fail-closed shape.
1544:       if (blockchainData.anchorId) {
1545:         pollAnchorUntilConfirmed({
1546:           anchorId: blockchainData.anchorId,
1547:           documentId: id,
1548:           tenantId,
1549:           authHeader: (req.headers.authorization as string) || '',
1550:         });
1551:       }
1552: 
1553:       // KS-154: 202 Accepted — the anchor tx has been submitted (the
1554:       // anchoring service returned a txHash) but on-chain confirmation
1555:       // can take ~30-60s on Cardano. Convention for an async operation
1556:       // whose work-of-record is still finalising. Clients poll
1557:       // GET /api/documents/{id} or the verification endpoint for
1558:       // confirmation. The dev-fallback path returns the same code with
1559:       // `confidence: 'pending-onchain'` (txHash null) — still 202.
1560:       res.status(202).json({
1561:         id: document.id,
1562:         status: 'anchored',
1563:         transactionHash: blockchainData.txHash,
1564:         blockchain: blockchainData,
1565:       });
1566:     } catch (error) {
1567:       logger.error('Failed to anchor document', { error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error) });
1568:       res.status(500).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'Failed to anchor document' } });
1569:     }
1570:   },
1571: );
1572: 
1573: /**
1574:  * POST /api/documents/:id/transfer-custody — KS-68
1575:  *
1576:  * Explicit custodian-transfer endpoint. The rights-holder changes from
1577:  * A to B without the document bytes changing. Distinct from KS-70 lineage
1578:  * (which creates new linked documents for content-changing events like
1579:  * certify / sign) — transfer-custody is metadata-only, hence its own
1580:  * lightweight `custody_events` table.
1581:  *
1582:  * Body: `{newHolderId | newHolderEmail, reason?, effectiveAt?}`. One of
1583:  * `newHolderId` / `newHolderEmail` is required. The email path needs a
1584:  * follow-up resolver to a real userId — for now it's stored as-is on
1585:  * the event row's `to_holder_id` is the resolved userId; email-only
1586:  * recipients require a KS-69 lookup hit upstream.
1587:  *
1588:  * Behaviour:
1589:  *   1. RBAC gate: any document-write role (ISSUER_ADMIN / ORG_ADMIN /
1590:  *      SYSTEM_ADMIN / SUPER_ADMIN) OR the `documents:transfer-custody` scope.
1591:  *      KS-290: the original KS-68 spec omitted ISSUER_ADMIN, which 403'd a
1592:  *      document's own issuer/owner on their own document even though they
1593:  *      could create / version / share it. ISSUER_ADMIN is now included via
1594:  *      the shared `DOCUMENT_WRITE_ROLES` base so this verb can't drift from
1595:  *      its siblings again. (Public-signup OWNER stays excluded — it carries
1596:  *      only `documents:write`, not `documents:transfer-custody`.)
1597:  *   2. Confirm document exists in tenant.
1598:  *   2a. KS-697: confirm the NEW HOLDER exists in this tenant. `newHolderId` is
1599:  *      format-checked at the boundary (400) and then existence-checked against
1600:  *      `users` (404 RECIPIENT_NOT_FOUND) — the same answer the `newHolderEmail`
1601:  *      path gives. ⚠ KS-1402 correction: this used to end "has always given via
1602:  *      auth's /api/users/lookup". Since KS-1402 originate resolves the email
1603:  *      ITSELF — one `users` read on the request's db with a bound tenant
1604:  *      predicate — because that lookup forwarded the caller's credential and
1605:  *      Platform S's connector key carries no `users:read`. Before KS-697, an
1606:  *      arbitrary invented uuid returned 201 and minted an anchor for a custody
1607:  *      chain pointing at nobody; `custody_events.to_holder_id` carries no FK,
1608:  *      so the database was never going to catch it.
1609:  *   3. Insert custody_events row (from = current owner, to = new holder).
1610:  *   4. Update documents.owner_user_id to the new holder.
1611:  *   5. Return 201 with the event id.
1612:  *
1613:  * Anchoring on Cardano is deferred to a follow-up — the chain metadata
1614:  * shape needs alignment with anchoring service. For now the event is
1615:  * tenant-DB-only; the chain anchor will arrive in a separate PR.
1616:  */
1617: /**
1618:  * KS-1402 — mint (or resolve) an INVITED stub user for a transfer-custody recipient Platform K
1619:  * does not know yet, via auth's POST /api/users/stub. Returns the holder id, or `null` when it
1620:  * has already answered on `res`.
1621:  *
1622:  * Shape taken from certifications/issue (routes/certifications.ts:272-332), including KS-289
1623:  * (call auth DIRECTLY on AUTH_SERVICE_URL, never through the gateway — the gateway enforces CSRF
1624:  * on POST and a service call carries no browser CSRF token) and KS-203 (bound the upstream call
1625:  * so a hung users/stub cannot hold this request open until the gateway times out).
1626:  *
1627:  * Deliberately NOT extracted into a shared helper with certifications/issue: the two call sites
1628:  * differ in their 404 code and their failure mapping, and the routes are owned by different
1629:  * work. Twenty-five duplicated lines beat a shared seam two tickets both edit.
1630:  */
1631: async function mintInvitedStubHolder(
1632:   req: Request,
1633:   res: Response,
1634:   normalisedEmail: string,
1635:   holderEmailHash: string,
1636:   tenantId: string,
1637:   db: { $queryRaw: (strings: TemplateStringsArray, ...values: unknown[]) => Promise<unknown> },
1638: ): Promise<string | null> {
1639:   const authBase = process.env.AUTH_SERVICE_URL || 'http://localhost:6003';
1640:   const authHeader = (req.headers.authorization as string) || '';
1641:   // The resolution read, repeated. Same BOUND tenant predicate as the email path's own read and the
1642:   // id path's — so a winner's row in another tenant misses here exactly as a stranger would.
1643:   const resolveByHash = async (): Promise<string | null> => {
1644:     const rows = (await db.$queryRaw`
1645:       SELECT id
1646:         FROM users
1647:        WHERE email_lookup_hash = ${holderEmailHash}
1648:          AND (tenant_id IS NULL OR tenant_id = ${tenantId}::uuid)
1649:        LIMIT 1
1650:     `) as Array<{ id: string }>;
1651:     return rows.length > 0 ? rows[0].id : null;
1652:   };
1653:   const notFound = () => {
1654:     res.status(404).json({
1655:       success: false,
1656:       error: {
1657:         code: 'RECIPIENT_NOT_FOUND',
1658:         message: 'No user with that email in this tenant. Invite them first (KS-74 pending-invite UX).',
1659:       },
1660:     });
1661:     return null;
1662:   };
1663: 
1664:   let stubRes: Awaited<ReturnType<typeof fetch>>;
1665:   try {
1666:     stubRes = await fetch(`${authBase}/api/users/stub`, {
1667:       method: 'POST',
1668:       headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json', ...(authHeader ? { Authorization: authHeader } : {}) },
1669:       body: JSON.stringify({ email: normalisedEmail }),
1670:       signal: AbortSignal.timeout(5000),
1671:     });
1672:   } catch (err: unknown) {
1673:     // KS-203: a timeout/abort is a transient dependency failure (retryable 503), distinct from a
1674:     // genuine bad-gateway answer (502). Either way we are BEFORE the custody transaction, so no
1675:     // event row and no owner flip can half-happen.
1676:     const name = (err as { name?: string } | null)?.name;
1677:     const timedOut = name === 'TimeoutError' || name === 'AbortError';
1678:     logger.warn('transfer-custody: users/stub unreachable', {
1679:       error: (err as { message?: string } | null)?.message,
1680:       timedOut,
1681:     });
1682:     res.status(timedOut ? 503 : 502).json({
1683:       success: false,
1684:       error: timedOut
1685:         ? { code: 'SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE', message: 'Recipient lookup timed out — please retry' }
1686:         : { code: 'BAD_GATEWAY', message: 'Recipient lookup service unavailable' },
1687:     });
1688:     return null;
1689:   }
1690: 
1691:   // auth's deliberate cross-tenant answer: the email is already a K user in ANOTHER tenant, and
1692:   // the global `email` UNIQUE constraint (KS-74 known limitation) means no second row can exist.
1693:   // Keep THIS route's existing code so Platform S's contract does not change — certifications/issue
1694:   // answers RECIPIENT_NOT_AVAILABLE for the same case, an inconsistency that is its own ticket.
1695:   if (stubRes.status === 404) return notFound();
1696: 
1697:   // KS-1402 / AF1 N4 — auth answers 400 for an email it refuses to register at all: today that is
1698:   // the reserved synthetic wallet domain `@wallet.local` (auth stubBodySchema -> isReservedWalletEmail,
1699:   // KS-806). This route has ALREADY format-checked the address against auth's own /lookup schema
1700:   // above, so a 400 from here does not mean "malformed" — it means "this address can never name a
1701:   // recipient". Before the invited-stub build such an address simply failed to resolve and answered
1702:   // 404; mapping it back to that same 404 keeps Platform S's contract unchanged rather than
1703:   // introducing a 502 for an input that is not a gateway fault. Chosen over merely documenting it,
1704:   // because it is one line and the alternative is a new answer nobody asked for.
1705:   if (stubRes.status === 400) {
1706:     logger.warn('transfer-custody: users/stub refuses this email as a recipient', { status: 400 });
1707:     return notFound();
1708:   }
1709: 
1710:   let holderId: string | undefined;
1711:   if (stubRes.ok) {
1712:     const body = (await stubRes.json().catch(() => null)) as { data?: { userId?: string } } | null;
1713:     holderId = body?.data?.userId;
1714:   }
1715:   if (!holderId) {
1716:     // A non-2xx that is not auth's cross-tenant 404. The row may nonetheless exist by now (another
1717:     // request for the same email), so resolve once more against our own database before calling
1718:     // this a gateway failure.
1719:     const raced = await resolveByHash();
1720:     if (raced) return raced;
1721:     logger.error('transfer-custody: users/stub did not yield a holder', { status: stubRes.status });
1722:     res.status(502).json({
1723:       success: false,
1724:       error: { code: 'BAD_GATEWAY', message: 'Could not resolve recipient via users/stub' },
1725:     });
1726:     return null;
1727:   }
1728: 
1729:   // KS-1402 / AF1 N5: `holderId` is bound into a `::uuid` cast below and in the custody INSERT. A
1730:   // non-UUID answer from auth would raise 22P02 inside the query and surface as the handler's
1731:   // outer-catch 500 — an unlabelled answer for an upstream fault. Refuse it as a gateway failure,
1732:   // the same way the id path format-checks `newHolderId` before its own cast (KS-697).
1733:   if (!UUID_RE.test(holderId)) {
1734:     logger.error('transfer-custody: users/stub returned a userId that is not a uuid', {
1735:       status: stubRes.status,
1736:     });
1737:     res.status(502).json({
1738:       success: false,
1739:       error: { code: 'BAD_GATEWAY', message: 'Could not resolve recipient via users/stub' },
1740:     });
1741:     return null;
1742:   }
1743: 
1744:   // THE TENANT PROOF, and the reason this is not just a forwarded call. auth pins `tenant_id` to
1745:   // the caller's JWT tenant, but this route does not take that on trust: the id auth returned is
1746:   // re-read through the SAME bound tenant predicate the resolution read and the id path use. An
1747:   // upstream that answered with another tenant's user — compromised, misconfigured, or simply
1748:   // wrong — must not get a document's chain of custody pointed at them. Deleting this read is a
1749:   // mutation that a cell in ks1402-invited-stub-holder.integration.test.ts kills.
1750:   const confirmed = (await db.$queryRaw`
1751:     SELECT 1 AS ok
1752:       FROM users
1753:      WHERE id = ${holderId}::uuid
1754:        AND (tenant_id IS NULL OR tenant_id = ${tenantId}::uuid)
1755:      LIMIT 1
1756:   `) as Array<{ ok: number }>;
1757:   if (confirmed.length > 0) return holderId;
1758: 
1759:   // KS-1402 / AF1 B1 — a re-read MISS is NOT yet evidence of a foreign tenant, because auth can
1760:   // hand back an id that is in no tenant at all. auth's `createUser` upserts
1761:   // `ON CONFLICT (email_lookup_hash) ... DO UPDATE`, and **`id` is not in its SET list**
1762:   // (userRepo.ts:692-698), so a concurrent first-time insert for the same email leaves the
1763:   // EXISTING row's id in place; `createUser` then returns the `user` object it was given
1764:   // (userRepo.ts:732), i.e. the uuid `createInvitedStub` generated (userRepo.ts:771). There is no
1765:   // UNIQUE index on `email` to raise an error — createUser's own comment says the
1766:   // non-deterministic encryption made that constraint useless (userRepo.ts:669-673). So the loser
1767:   // of a real race gets HTTP 201 and a **phantom id that is in no row**.
1768:   //
1769:   // Resolve by the email hash once more before refusing. Without this, the real race reads as a
1770:   // foreign-tenant answer: a 404 to a caller whose transfer should have succeeded, plus a false
1771:   // "outside this tenant" alarm pointing operators at a cross-tenant fault that did not happen.
1772:   const winner = await resolveByHash();
1773:   if (winner) return winner;
1774: 
1775:   // Both reads missed: the id is not in this tenant AND the email is not in this tenant. Now the
1776:   // alarm is earned. Answer the no-disclosure 404 rather than a 502 — the caller learns nothing
1777:   // about other tenants either way, and the operator gets the error log, which names the cause.
1778:   logger.error('transfer-custody: users/stub returned a holder outside this tenant — refusing', { tenantId });
1779:   return notFound();
1780: }
1781: 
1782: const ALLOWED_TRANSFER_CUSTODY_ROLES = DOCUMENT_WRITE_ROLES;
1783: // KS-1402: the holder-email format check, copied from auth's /api/users/lookup
1784: // (services/auth/src/routes/users.ts, lookupQuerySchema) so a malformed email is refused
1785: // exactly as before, when auth refused it (400) and this route mapped it (KS-536).
1786: // `.trim()` before `.email()`/`.min()`/`.max()` per KS-202.
1787: const HOLDER_EMAIL_SCHEMA = z.string().trim().email().min(3).max(320);
1788: 
1789: documentsRouter.post(
1790:   '/:id/transfer-custody',
1791:   [
1792:     param('id').notEmpty().withMessage('Document ID is required'),
1793:     body('reason').optional().isString(),
1794:     body('effectiveAt').optional().isISO8601().withMessage('effectiveAt must be an ISO-8601 timestamp'),
1795:   ],
1796:   async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
1797:     try {
1798:       const user = getUserFromRequest(req);
1799:       if (!user) {
1800:         return res.status(401).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'UNAUTHORIZED', message: 'Authentication required' } });
1801:       }
1802: 
1803:       if (!isAllowedByRoleOrScope(req, ALLOWED_TRANSFER_CUSTODY_ROLES, 'documents:transfer-custody')) {
1804:         return res.status(403).json({
1805:           success: false,
1806:           error: { code: 'FORBIDDEN', message: 'Your role / scope does not permit custody transfer on this document' },
1807:         });
1808:       }
1809: 
1810:       const errors = validationResult(req);
1811:       if (!errors.isEmpty()) {
1812:         return res.status(400).json({
1813:           success: false,
1814:           error: { code: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'Invalid request body', details: errors.array() },
1815:         });
1816:       }
1817: 
1818:       const { id } = req.params;
1819:       // KS-480 §6: connector attribution on the transfer action. KS-1228: validated here, and
1820:       // recorded only once the custody event and the owner flip are written (below).
1821:       const transferObo = await checkOnBehalfOf(req, res);
1822:       if (!transferObo.ok) return;
1823:       const { newHolderId, newHolderEmail, reason, effectiveAt } = req.body as {
1824:         newHolderId?: string;
1825:         newHolderEmail?: string;
1826:         reason?: string;
1827:         effectiveAt?: string;
1828:       };
1829: 
1830:       if (!newHolderId && !newHolderEmail) {
1831:         return res.status(400).json({
1832:           success: false,
1833:           error: { code: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'One of newHolderId or newHolderEmail is required' },
1834:         });
1835:       }
1836:       if (newHolderId && newHolderEmail) {
1837:         return res.status(400).json({
1838:           success: false,
1839:           error: { code: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'Supply either newHolderId or newHolderEmail, not both' },
1840:         });
1841:       }
1842: 
1843:       // KS-697: `newHolderId` flows into a `::uuid` cast in the INSERT below.
1844:       // A non-UUID threw Postgres 22P02 there, got swallowed by this handler's
1845:       // outer catch and surfaced as a raw 500 — the not_a_server_error class,
