1846:       // and the same boundary gap KS-497/KS-536 closed for tenant ids and
1847:       // `recipients[].userId`. Reject before the cast.
1848:       //
1849:       // ⚠ The KS-536 comment on /share asserted "/transfer-custody already
1850:       // format-checks its holder id before touching the DB". It did not — no
1851:       // such guard existed on this route until this line. The claim is
1852:       // corrected there too; a comment that says a guard exists is worse than
1853:       // no comment, because it stops the next reader looking.
1854:       if (newHolderId !== undefined && !UUID_RE.test(String(newHolderId))) {
1855:         return res.status(400).json({
1856:           success: false,
1857:           error: { code: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'newHolderId must be a valid UUID' },
1858:         });
1859:       }
1860: 
1861:       // KS-1402 (Q-HOIST): the tenant and the request's db are read HERE, above the holder
1862:       // resolution, because resolution now reads `users` itself and needs both. Before KS-1402
1863:       // they were read further down, after resolution. The move is pure — the same expressions,
1864:       // every later use unchanged. Moving resolution DOWN instead would have changed the order
1865:       // of answers (a missing document would 404 NOT_FOUND before an unknown email could 404
1866:       // RECIPIENT_NOT_FOUND), a contract change nobody asked for.
1867:       const tenantId = getReqTenantId(req);
1868:       // The request's db reference, for the holder READS below
1869:       // (per-tenant if multi-tenancy is on, default prisma otherwise).
1870:       // ⚠ KS-1312 correction: this comment used to end "same pattern as every
1871:       // other write route in this file". That described the pre-KS-1263 shape.
1872:       // Since KS-1263 this handler's WRITES — the custody event and the owner
1873:       // flip — go through withTenant(), and this reference serves reads only:
1874:       // the single `users` existence $queryRaw below. Left as a correction
1875:       // rather than a silent deletion, for the same reason as the KS-697 note
1876:       // further down this handler — the wrong half is why nobody looked.
1877:       // ⚠ KS-1402: "the single `users` existence $queryRaw" is no longer single. Since
1878:       // KS-1402 this reference also serves the email path's resolution read, just below.
1879:       // Both reads carry the same bound tenant predicate.
1880:       const { prisma: defaultPrisma, withTenant } = await import('../db');
1881:       const db = (req as any).db || defaultPrisma;
1882: 
1883:       // KS-1402 — the email-only path resolves the holder INSIDE originate.
1884:       // https://linear.app/secuura/issue/KS-1402
1885:       //
1886:       // PRIOR BEHAVIOUR (removed): this block called auth's GET /api/users/lookup (KS-69)
1887:       // directly on AUTH_SERVICE_URL (KS-289), FORWARDING THE CALLER'S Authorization. auth's
1888:       // /lookup gates on an allowed role or the `users:read` scope, and Platform S's connector
1889:       // key is minted WITHOUT `users:read` on purpose (api-gateway routes/platform.ts,
1890:       // S_CONNECTOR_SCOPES: "on-behalf-of resolution is K-side"). So every S custody transfer
1891:       // by email answered 403 — Stuart measured 7 of 7 refused on 2026-10-05. The block also
1892:       // mapped the lookup's 400/422 to 400 (KS-536), passed its other 4xx through (KS-739) and
1893:       // turned its 5xx or an unreachable auth into 502. With the call gone none of those
1894:       // upstream answers can occur here, and the code that mapped them went with it.
1895:       //
1896:       // NOW: originate computes the email's lookup HMAC and reads `users` on the request's db,
1897:       // under its OWN database role — the shape of the id path's existence check below. No
1898:       // token is minted, stored or sent; auth is not changed; no scope moves. Kam's ruling on
1899:       // card secuura-ks1402-s-key-cannot-carry-users-read-1006 (a): "Originate resolves the
1900:       // holder with its own service credential (Stuart's option 2)." Reading "its own service
1901:       // credential" as originate's own database role, rather than a new service token, is a
1902:       // reading of that sentence, confirmed by Wednesday on 2026-10-09.
1903:       //
1904:       // The act is still authorised on the CALLER — isAllowedByRoleOrScope above runs first and
1905:       // is unchanged. Only RESOLUTION moved.
1906:       let resolvedHolderId = newHolderId;
1907:       if (!resolvedHolderId && newHolderEmail) {
1908:         // KS-1402: format-check here, with the schema auth's /lookup used, so a malformed email
1909:         // is still the 400 it was. Before KS-1402 that 400 came back from auth and was mapped
1910:         // here (KS-536); the body is byte-identical.
1911:         if (!HOLDER_EMAIL_SCHEMA.safeParse(newHolderEmail).success) {
1912:           return res.status(400).json({
1913:             success: false,
1914:             error: { code: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'newHolderEmail is not a valid email address' },
1915:           });
1916:         }
1917:         // KS-1402: auth's normalisation exactly (auth repositories/userRepo.ts getUserByEmail,
1918:         // `email.toLowerCase().trim()`), applied to the value as sent. NOT provenance.ts's
1919:         // lowercase-only form — a stray space would hash differently and read as unknown.
1920:         const normalisedHolderEmail = String(newHolderEmail).toLowerCase().trim();
1921:         let holderEmailHash: string;
1922:         try {
1923:           holderEmailHash = lookupHash(normalisedHolderEmail);
1924:         } catch (keyErr: any) {
1925:           // KS-1402: lookupHash THROWS when PII_LOOKUP_HMAC_KEY is not registered. Originate
1926:           // registers it only when BOTH PII_ENCRYPTION_KEY and PII_LOOKUP_HMAC_KEY are set
1927:           // (index.ts -> initFromEnv), and nothing refuses to start without the second. Before
1928:           // KS-1402 this path never needed the key: auth hashed. Answer a labelled 502, as the
1929:           // unreachable-auth arm did, never an unlabelled 500.
1930:           logger.error('transfer-custody: cannot resolve holder email, lookup HMAC key unavailable', { error: keyErr?.message });
1931:           return res.status(502).json({
1932:             success: false,
1933:             error: { code: 'BAD_GATEWAY', message: 'Could not resolve recipient email: the recipient lookup is not configured' },
1934:           });
1935:         }
1936:         // KS-1402: one `users` read, values BOUND in the tagged template (never
1937:         // $queryRawUnsafe, never concatenation). Two conjuncts, both load-bearing:
1938:         //   * email_lookup_hash — the HMAC sibling of the encrypted email (migration 030;
1939:         //     UNIQUE, so an email names at most one user across ALL tenants);
1940:         //   * the tenant — bound to THIS request's tenant. auth's /lookup compared tenants in
1941:         //     the app layer and SKIPPED the compare when the caller's credential carried no
1942:         //     tenant (the KS 1406 class); a bound predicate has no such hole.
1943:         // `tenant_id IS NULL` is tolerated for parity with auth and with the id path below. A
1944:         // user in another tenant and no user at all both return zero rows, so both answer the
1945:         // same 404 — no cross-tenant existence disclosure. The request db also carries the RLS
1946:         // tenant GUC (KS-458).
1947:         // ⚠ CORRECTED 2026-10-19 (KS-1662), because this said the opposite. Measured on the repo's
1948:         // migrated schema at develop a0da739b6954: `users` is relrowsecurity=t AND
1949:         // relforcerowsecurity=t; policy `tenant_isolation` is cmd=ALL, roles={public}, keyed on
1950:         // app.current_tenant_id; the second policy `users_auth_lookup` (qual `true`) is
1951:         // roles={secuura_auth_lookup} — a DEDICATED role, NOT public, so it never ORs into this
1952:         // service's result. As `secuura_app`: 0 rows with no GUC, and with the GUC set to tenant A a
1953:         // tenant-B row is invisible, against `documents` reading 0 as the app role and NON-ZERO as
1954:         // the owner as a control (the exact count is DB-state dependent).
1955:         // The policy's OTHER branch — current_setting('app.tenant_scope_bypass') = 'platform_admin' —
1956:         // is UNREACHABLE from this route, measured: platform scope is entered only by an explicit
1957:         // runWithPlatformScope() (packages/shared/src/db/tenant-guc.ts:102), tenantGucContext() only
1958:         // ever sets { tenantId } (:116-131), and originate's only call sites are in
1959:         // routes/adminConfig.ts — zero in this file.
1960:         // Migration 039_rls_fail_closed.sql:156-157 is what changed it.
1961:         //
1962:         // 🔴 WHICH ROLE, AND WHY THE PREDICATE IS NOT MERELY BELT-AND-BRACES (corrected, gate AJ1
1963:         // N2ii — an earlier revision of this comment said originate is `secuura_app` "in both
1964:         // deployed shapes" and cited services.bicep:51-55; that was wrong evidence and an
1965:         // over-claim). Compose IS `secuura_app` (docker-compose.yml:581). On Azure,
1966:         // deployment/azure/deploy.sh:679-702 reads the `secuura-app-db-password` key-vault secret and
1967:         // **falls back to the admin/owner URL when it is absent** ("stay on admin-DIRECT so a
1968:         // first-time deploy still works"); services.bicep's own comments call the Stage-1 plumbing
1969:         // inert with both values still resolving to the admin URL (:50-54, :265-268). See
1970:         // RLS_ROLE_CUTOVER.md.
1971:         // SO: where the app role is in use, RLS is the enforcement and this predicate is the
1972:         // belt-and-braces. Where that key-vault secret is missing, the service runs as the OWNER,
1973:         // RLS does not bind, and THIS PREDICATE IS THE ONLY ENFORCEMENT. Keep it.
1974:         // Coverage, stated exactly: the cells in ks1662-tenant-predicate-without-rls cover THIS
1975:         // read and the id-path read below, plus the two inside the mint helper. They connect as the
1976:         // owner, which is the shape above.
1977:         // A database failure here reaches the handler's outer catch (500 INTERNAL_ERROR), as the
1978:         // id path's own `users` read below already does.
1979:         const holderByEmail: Array<{ id: string }> = await db.$queryRaw`
1980:           SELECT id
1981:             FROM users
1982:            WHERE email_lookup_hash = ${holderEmailHash}
1983:              AND (tenant_id IS NULL OR tenant_id = ${tenantId}::uuid)
1984:            LIMIT 1
1985:         `;
1986:         if (holderByEmail.length === 0) {
1987:           // KS-1402 — Platform K MINTS an invited stub holder here rather than refusing.
1988:           // https://linear.app/secuura/issue/KS-1402
1989:           //
1990:           // AUTHORITY. Kam, card `secuura-ks1402-who-creates-the-holder-1019` = (a): "K creates an
1991:           // invited stub user on transfer. As certifications/issue already does for holderEmail."
1992:           // That precedent is the server-to-server call in mintInvitedStubHolder, NOT a local
1993:           // INSERT: auth is the only writer of `users` and owns the per-user DEK PII encryption,
1994:           // the `email_lookup_hash`, the global email-uniqueness rule with its cross-tenant branch,
1995:           // and the claim-on-/api/auth/register flow.
1996:           //
1997:           // THIS DOES NOT UNDO THE EARLIER RULING. Card
1998:           // `secuura-ks1402-s-key-cannot-carry-users-read-1006` = (a) removed this route's
1999:           // forwarded-token call to auth's GET /api/users/lookup — but it removed it BECAUSE it
2000:           // 403'd: /lookup gates on `users:read`, which S's key is minted WITHOUT on purpose
2001:           // (Stuart measured 7 of 7 refused, 2026-10-05). POST /api/users/stub has a DIFFERENT gate
2002:           // (auth routes/users.ts:247-260, hasUsersStubScope) that S's key passes. RESOLUTION — the
2003:           // subject of that ruling — stays local, above, and is untouched.
2004:           //
2005:           // THE ADMISSION WAS INCIDENTAL AND IS NOT ANY MORE (KS-1653, #1630, da9e96ffa099). S's minted
2006:           // set (api-gateway routes/platform.ts:592-596, S_CONNECTOR_SCOPES) used to pass
2007:           // hasUsersStubScope only on `certifications:write` (KS-564 Option A): a transfer-custody
2008:           // caller was admitted because it may ALSO write certifications. hasUsersStubScope now also
2009:           // admits `documents:transfer-custody` on its own, and S's key carries BOTH, so it is admitted
2010:           // on either. Widening a scope gate in auth is a security change, so it got its own ticket
2011:           // and was not done in KS-1402.
2012:           // Where that dependency is ACTUALLY guarded, since it is not guarded here — it has
2013:           // TWO halves, and both are covered since KS-1653 (gate AJ1 N3 found only the first):
2014:           //   * the MINT half: api-gateway `ks843-erasure-scope-gate.test.ts` F-2 regex-reads this
2015:           //     very array out of the source and asserts it equals the agreed contract set — gate
2016:           //     AH1 measured it reddening 1/9 when the scope leaves the mint set. COVERED.
2017:           //   * the ADMISSION half: auth `ks1653-stub-admits-transfer-custody-on-its-own-scope.test.ts`.
2018:           //     Planting each mutant on `hasUsersStubScope` reds C1 alone when `certifications:write`
2019:           //     leaves it (auth users.ts:259), and S1 and S2 when `documents:transfer-custody` leaves it
2020:           //     (users.ts:258). S's key carries both scopes, so it loses access only if both go. COVERED.
2021:           // ⚠ CORRECTED 2026-10-19 (KS-1662): this comment also named auth
2022:           // `ks564-connector-stub-auth.test.ts` as a guard. **It is not one.** Removing
2023:           // `certifications:write` from auth's `hasUsersStubScope` (then auth users.ts:257, now :259) left ks564 at
2024:           // 4/4 and the auth unit suite at 1076/1076. The claim came from grepping ks564 for the
2025:           // string `certifications:write` and finding it — the presence of a string in a test is not
2026:           // evidence that the test guards it; the measurement is to remove the thing and see what
2027:           // reds. The cells in ks1402-invited-stub-holder.integration.test.ts exercise a LITERAL
2028:           // COPY of the list, so they document the dependency and would catch an edit to that copy —
2029:           // they do not watch the real array.
2030:           const minted = await mintInvitedStubHolder(
2031:             req, res, normalisedHolderEmail, holderEmailHash, tenantId, db,
2032:           );
2033:           if (!minted) return; // mintInvitedStubHolder has already answered on `res`
2034:           resolvedHolderId = minted;
2035:         } else {
2036:           resolvedHolderId = holderByEmail[0].id;
2037:         }
2038:       }
2039: 
2040:       if (!resolvedHolderId) {
2041:         return res.status(500).json({
2042:           success: false,
2043:           error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'Resolved holder id missing after lookup' },
2044:         });
2045:       }
2046: 
2047:       // KS-1402 (Q-HOIST): `tenantId` and the request's `db` used to be read HERE, after the
2048:       // holder resolution. Resolution now reads `users` itself and needs both, so they are read
2049:       // ABOVE it (see there) — a pure move: the same expressions, every later use unchanged.
2050:       const doc = await getDocument(id, tenantId, (req as any).db);
2051:       if (!doc) {
2052:         return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Document not found in this tenant' } });
2053:       }
2054: 
2055:       const fromHolderId = doc.owner.id || null;
2056:       const eventEffectiveAt = effectiveAt ? new Date(effectiveAt) : new Date();
2057: 
2058:       // KS-697: the id path never checked that the holder EXISTS. `custody_events`
2059:       // has an FK on `document_id` but NOT on `to_holder_id`, so any well-formed
2060:       // uuid inserted happily: an arbitrary invented holder returned 201 AND minted
2061:       // an anchor, while `newHolderEmail` for the same operation correctly 404s.
2062:       // The document's chain of custody could be pointed at nobody, and anchored.
2063:       //
2064:       // Mirror the email path exactly: unknown holder → 404 RECIPIENT_NOT_FOUND.
2065:       // Only the id path needs this — the email path has already resolved the holder,
2066:       // and resolving IS its existence + tenant check.
2067:       // ⚠ KS-1402 correction: this used to say the email path resolved "through auth's
2068:       // /api/users/lookup, which does its own existence + tenant check". Since KS-1402 the
2069:       // email path reads `users` itself, above, with the same bound tenant predicate as the
2070:       // read below. Corrected rather than deleted, for the reason the KS-1312 note gives.
2071:       //
2072:       // Scoping: since KS-458 this `$queryRaw` carries the request's RLS tenant
2073:       // GUC, and `users` is FORCE RLS fail-closed — so a holder in another tenant
2074:       // is simply invisible here and falls out as the same 404. That matches auth's
2075:       // deliberate stance on /lookup, where "no such user" and "exists in another
2076:       // tenant" are made indistinguishable so the negative response cannot be read
2077:       // as cross-tenant existence disclosure.
2078:       // ⚠ CORRECTED 2026-10-19 (KS-1662): this said the explicit tenant_id predicate was
2079:       // "belt-and-braces for any deployment where the GUC is not carried (tenant RLS has been
2080:       // inert before now)". Measured at develop a0da739b6954, that is backwards — RLS is live and
2081:       // enforcing for the `secuura_app` role this service connects as, so RLS is the enforcement
2082:       // and the predicate is the belt-and-braces. The full measurement, with its controls, is on
2083:       // the email path above. The predicate stays, for the reasons given there.
2084:       // `tenant_id IS NULL` is tolerated because auth's own lookup only enforces tenancy when both
2085:       // sides are known.
2086:       if (newHolderId !== undefined) {
2087:         const holderRows: Array<{ ok: number }> = await db.$queryRaw`
2088:           SELECT 1 AS ok
2089:             FROM users
2090:            WHERE id = ${resolvedHolderId}::uuid
2091:              AND (tenant_id IS NULL OR tenant_id = ${tenantId}::uuid)
2092:            LIMIT 1
2093:         `;
2094:         if (holderRows.length === 0) {
2095:           return res.status(404).json({
2096:             success: false,
2097:             error: {
2098:               code: 'RECIPIENT_NOT_FOUND',
2099:               message: 'No user with that id in this tenant. Invite them first (KS-74 pending-invite UX).',
2100:             },
2101:           });
2102:         }
2103:       }
2104: 
2105:       // KS-1263: the custody event and the owner flip are ONE transaction. They used to be two
2106:       // statements, and the comment here accepted the window between them ("Prisma's $executeRaw
2107:       // doesn't compose nicely ... we accept a small window where the event row exists without the
2108:       // doc-owner update"). A throw in that window left a custody event for a transfer that did not
2109:       // happen, and since KS-1228 no action_provenance row either.
2110:       // KS-1263: withTenant() is used UNCONDITIONALLY here, never `(req as any).db`. On a
2111:       // multi-tenant deployment (index.ts:225, gated on MULTI_TENANCY_ENABLED, which
2112:       // deployment/azure/services.bicep:798 sets to 'true') that reference is
2113:       // createPoolProxy(pool), which wraps BEGIN + set_config + COMMIT around EACH statement
2114:       // (db.ts:24-26) — two transactions again, which is the defect this ticket removes.
2115:       // withTenant checks out ONE client, runs BEGIN + both set_config GUCs + the callback on
2116:       // that client, then COMMIT, or ROLLBACK on a throw (db.ts:302-336); handed that open
2117:       // client, createPoolProxy queries it directly rather than re-bundling (db.ts:24-26,
2118:       // pinned by ks458-db-tenant-guc.test.ts:111).
2119:       // KS-1402: `transferred_by_id` is a UUID column, but a connector principal is the literal
2120:       // string `connector:<id>` (auth generateConnectorToken). Bound straight into the ::uuid cast
2121:       // below, it raised 22P02 inside the transaction, and every Platform-S transfer whose holder
2122:       // RESOLVED answered the outer catch's raw 500 — the KS-564 class /share was fixed for (043).
2123:       // Same treatment as /share: prefer the onBehalfOf-resolved same-org user; otherwise NULL
2124:       // (migration 052), with the connector recorded on action_provenance by recordOnBehalfOf below.
2125:       const transferredByUuid = toActorUuid(user.id, transferObo.resolvedUserId);
2126:       let eventId: string;
2127:       try {
2128:         eventId = await withTenant(tenantId, async (tx) => {
2129:           const eventRows: Array<{ id: string }> = await tx.$queryRaw`
2130:         INSERT INTO custody_events (
2131:           tenant_id, document_id, from_holder_id, to_holder_id,
2132:           transferred_by_id, reason, effective_at
2133:         ) VALUES (
2134:           ${tenantId}::uuid,
2135:           (SELECT id FROM documents WHERE external_id = ${id} AND tenant_id = ${tenantId}::uuid LIMIT 1),
2136:           ${fromHolderId}::uuid,
2137:           ${resolvedHolderId}::uuid,
2138:           ${transferredByUuid}::uuid,
2139:           ${reason ?? null},
2140:           ${eventEffectiveAt}
2141:         )
2142:             RETURNING id::text AS id
2143:           `;
2144:           const written = eventRows[0]?.id;
2145:           if (!written) {
2146:             // Throw, never return: a `return res.json(...)` inside the callback resolves it
2147:             // normally and COMMITS. Throwing rolls the INSERT back; the 500 is mapped below.
2148:             throw Object.assign(new Error('custody event not written'), { code: 'CUSTODY_EVENT_NOT_WRITTEN' });
2149:           }
2150:           // Flip the owner. Use external_id as the lookup key to match
2151:           // the rest of the originate-route convention.
2152:           await tx.$executeRaw`
2153:             UPDATE documents
2154:                SET owner_user_id = ${resolvedHolderId}::uuid,
2155:                    updated_at = NOW()
2156:              WHERE external_id = ${id}
2157:                AND tenant_id = ${tenantId}::uuid
2158:           `;
2159:           return written;
2160:         });
2161:       } catch (err: any) {
2162:         if (err?.code === 'CUSTODY_EVENT_NOT_WRITTEN') {
2163:           return res.status(500).json({
2164:             success: false,
2165:             error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'Failed to write custody event' },
2166:           });
2167:         }
2168:         throw err;
2169:       }
2170:       recordOnBehalfOf(req, id, 'transfer-custody', transferObo);
2171: 
2172:       logger.info('Custody transferred', {
2173:         documentId: id,
2174:         fromHolderId,
2175:         toHolderId: resolvedHolderId,
2176:         eventId,
2177:       });
2178: 
2179:       // EventTypes doesn't yet have an OWNERSHIP_TRANSFERRED const —
2180:       // pass the literal string. When events.ts grows the const, swap
2181:       // to EventTypes.DOCUMENT_OWNERSHIP_TRANSFERRED.
2182:       publishEvent('document.ownership_transferred' as never, {
2183:         documentId: id,
2184:         fromHolderId,
2185:         toHolderId: resolvedHolderId,
2186:         transferredById: user.id,
2187:         eventId,
2188:       }).catch(() => {});
2189: 
2190:       // KS-319: emit the on-chain anchor for the custody-transfer event and
2191:       // return a resolvable anchor id (idempotent — reuses the document's
2192:       // existing anchor). Replaces the previously-deferred anchoredTxHash:null.
2193:       const custodyAnchor = await emitLifecycleAnchor({
2194:         documentId: id,
2195:         contentHash: doc.contentHash,
2196:         authHeader: req.headers.authorization as string,
2197:         eventType: 'transfer-custody',
2198:         identity: (req.body as any)?.identity,
2199:       });
2200: 
2201:       return res.status(201).json({
2202:         transfer: {
2203:           id: eventId,
2204:           documentId: id,
2205:           fromHolderId,
2206:           toHolderId: resolvedHolderId,
2207:           effectiveAt: eventEffectiveAt.toISOString(),
2208:           anchoredTxHash: custodyAnchor.txHash,
2209:           anchorId: custodyAnchor.anchorId,
2210:           anchor: { id: custodyAnchor.anchorId, txHash: custodyAnchor.txHash, status: custodyAnchor.status },
2211:         },
2212:       });
2213:     } catch (error) {
2214:       logger.error('Failed to transfer custody', { error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error) });
2215:       return res.status(500).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'Failed to transfer custody' } });
2216:     }
2217:   },
2218: );
2219: 
2220: /**
2221:  * POST /api/documents/:id/version — KS-86 (Option B sub-task of KS-84)
2222:  *
2223:  * Issuer-side lifecycle action endpoint. Records a new versioned document
2224:  * (with `parent_document_id` pointing at the source) for any byte-changing
2225:  * action the caller has already performed externally — typically a
2226:  * watermark or an X.509 sign-and-sidecar. Platform K is a hash-only
2227:  * registry; the bytes themselves live with the issuer (e.g. SSD).
2228:  *
2229:  * Body: `{action, newContentHash, metadata?, title?}`
2230:  *   - `action`: 'watermark' | 'sign-cert'  (enum locked here; future
2231:  *     verbs land via a deliberate ticket so we don't grow drift).
2232:  *   - `newContentHash`: sha256 hex digest of the new bytes. Optional
2233:  *     `sha256:` prefix accepted (and stripped); 64 lowercase hex chars
2234:  *     required after the prefix.
2235:  *   - `metadata`: free-form issuer metadata folded into the new doc's
2236:  *     `data` blob (e.g. `{watermarkText, watermarkPosition}` for
2237:  *     watermark, `{signedBy, certThumbprint, sidecarUrl}` for sign-cert).
2238:  *   - `title`: override the new version's title; defaults to
2239:  *     "<source title> (watermarked)" / "<source title> (signed)".
2240:  *
2241:  * Behaviour:
2242:  *   1. RBAC gate — same role allow-list as transfer-custody, plus
2243:  *      `documents:write` scope (versioning is a write op; we don't
2244:  *      carve a `documents:version` scope until a connector needs it).
2245:  *   2. Resolve source doc in tenant. 404 if missing.
2246:  *   3. Mint new external_id `doc-<ts>-<rand>`.
2247:  *   4. KS-70 cycle check on the new id against the source. Should always
2248:  *      pass for fresh ids but defence-in-depth keeps the invariant solid.
2249:  *   5. Save derived doc with `parent_document_id` = source.
2250:  *   6. Audit log + DOCUMENT_VERSIONED event.
2251:  *   7. Return 201 with the new doc shape.
2252:  *
2253:  * Anchoring is intentionally NOT triggered here — the existing
2254:  * `POST /api/documents/{id}/anchor` endpoint can be called against the
2255:  * new id when the issuer is ready. Keeps Cardano cost decisions in the
2256:  * issuer's hands per the KS-70 model.
2257:  */
2258: const ALLOWED_VERSION_ROLES = DOCUMENT_WRITE_ROLES;
2259: const ALLOWED_VERSION_ACTIONS = ['watermark', 'sign-cert'] as const;
2260: type VersionAction = (typeof ALLOWED_VERSION_ACTIONS)[number];
2261: const CONTENT_HASH_RE = /^([a-f0-9]{64})$/;
2262: 
2263: documentsRouter.post(
2264:   '/:id/version',
2265:   [
2266:     param('id').notEmpty().withMessage('Document ID is required'),
2267:     body('action').isString().isIn(ALLOWED_VERSION_ACTIONS as unknown as string[]).withMessage(`action must be one of: ${ALLOWED_VERSION_ACTIONS.join(', ')}`),
2268:     body('newContentHash').isString().notEmpty().withMessage('newContentHash is required'),
2269:     body('metadata').optional().isObject().withMessage('metadata must be an object'),
2270:     body('title').optional().isString(),
2271:   ],
2272:   async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
2273:     try {
2274:       const user = getUserFromRequest(req);
2275:       if (!user) {
2276:         return res.status(401).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'UNAUTHORIZED', message: 'Authentication required' } });
2277:       }
2278: 
2279:       if (!isAllowedByRoleOrScope(req, ALLOWED_VERSION_ROLES, 'documents:write')) {
2280:         return res.status(403).json({
2281:           success: false,
2282:           error: { code: 'FORBIDDEN', message: 'Your role / scope does not permit creating a new version of this document' },
2283:         });
2284:       }
2285: 
2286:       const errors = validationResult(req);
2287:       if (!errors.isEmpty()) {
2288:         return res.status(400).json({
2289:           success: false,
2290:           error: { code: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'Invalid request body', details: errors.array() },
2291:         });
2292:       }
2293: 
2294:       const { id } = req.params;
2295:       const action = req.body.action as VersionAction;
2296:       // KS-480 §6: connector attribution on the version action (watermark/sign). KS-1228: validated
2297:       // here, and recorded only once the new version is saved (below), after every refusal.
2298:       const versionObo = await checkOnBehalfOf(req, res);
2299:       if (!versionObo.ok) return;
2300:       const rawHash = String(req.body.newContentHash || '');
2301:       const metadata = (req.body.metadata && typeof req.body.metadata === 'object') ? req.body.metadata as Record<string, unknown> : {};
2302:       // KS-480 §6: reserved provenance key — lives in action_provenance only,
2303:       // never in a row's plain metadata blob (it would be unencrypted PII).
2304:       delete metadata.onBehalfOf;
2305:       const overrideTitle = typeof req.body.title === 'string' ? req.body.title.trim() : '';
2306: 
2307:       // Normalise hash: accept both `sha256:hex` and `hex`. Reject anything
2308:       // that isn't 64 lowercase hex chars after the optional prefix — every
2309:       // store path downstream expects the bare digest.
2310:       const normalisedHash = rawHash.replace(/^sha256:/i, '').toLowerCase();
2311:       if (!CONTENT_HASH_RE.test(normalisedHash)) {
2312:         return res.status(400).json({
2313:           success: false,
2314:           error: { code: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'newContentHash must be a sha256 hex digest (64 lowercase hex chars, optional sha256: prefix)' },
2315:         });
2316:       }
2317:       const newContentHash = `sha256:${normalisedHash}`;
2318: 
2319:       const tenantId = getReqTenantId(req);
2320:       const source = await getDocument(id, tenantId, (req as any).db);
2321:       if (!source) {
2322:         return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Document not found in this tenant' } });
2323:       }
2324:       // KS-1213: this row is stored as `source.type` but served as `data.documentType || type`, and
2325:       // the caller's `metadata` is spread into `data`. A `metadata.documentType` that differs from the
2326:       // stored type would serve, and verify, the new row as another type. Same rule as the KS-1202
2327:       // create guard: refused, never overwritten; exact comparison, and a non-string never matches.
2328:       if (metadata.documentType !== undefined && metadata.documentType !== source.type) {
2329:         return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'metadata.documentType must equal the source document type' } });
2330:       }
2331: 
2332:       const newExternalId = `doc-${Date.now()}-${uuidv4().slice(0, 8)}`;
2333: 
2334:       try {
2335:         await assertNoCycle(newExternalId, id, tenantId, { db: (req as any).db });
2336:       } catch (cycleErr: any) {
2337:         if (cycleErr?.code === 'LINEAGE_CYCLE') {
2338:           return res.status(422).json({
2339:             success: false,
2340:             error: {
2341:               code: 'LINEAGE_CYCLE',
2342:               message: `Refusing to version: source ${id} would form a cycle in the lineage chain (corrupted parent chain?)`,
2343:             },
2344:           });
2345:         }
2346:         throw cycleErr;
2347:       }
2348: 
2349:       const now = new Date().toISOString();
2350:       const sourceTitle = (source.data?.title as string) || source.type || 'Untitled';
2351:       const titleSuffix = action === 'watermark' ? ' (watermarked)' : ' (signed)';
2352:       const derivedTitle = overrideTitle || `${sourceTitle}${titleSuffix}`;
2353: 
2354:       // KS-282: a version is a fresh document with its OWN content hash; it must
2355:       // NOT inherit the parent's anchor. The parent's `blockchain` block rides in
2356:       // via `...source.data` (mapRow spreads metadata into `data`, and blockchain
2357:       // is read back from metadata) — strip it so the version reads as
2358:       // un-anchored and POST /{versionId}/anchor anchors the version's new hash
2359:       // (its document_id is new, so it gets its own anchor row).
2360:       const { blockchain: _parentBlockchain, ...sourceDataSansChain } =
2361:         (source.data || {}) as Record<string, unknown>;
2362: 
2363:       const derivedDoc: DocumentRecord = {
2364:         id: newExternalId,
2365:         type: source.type,
2366:         status: 'draft',
2367:         owner: source.owner,
2368:         rightsHolderId: source.rightsHolderId,
2369:         parentDocumentId: id,
2370:         data: {
2371:           ...sourceDataSansChain,
2372:           title: derivedTitle,
2373:           ...metadata,
2374:           // KS-1508: the server-owned keys come AFTER the caller's metadata, so a caller cannot choose them.
2375:           // `versionedById` is the actor (user.id); the erasure of an organisation (KS-695) selects documents by it.
2376:           versionAction: action,
2377:           versionedAt: now,
2378:           versionedById: user.id,
2379:           parentDocumentId: id,
2380:         },
2381:         contentHash: newContentHash,
2382:         signatures: [],
2383:         blockchain: undefined,
2384:         createdAt: now,
2385:         updatedAt: now,
2386:       };
2387: 
2388:       await saveDocument(derivedDoc, tenantId, (req as any).db);
2389:       recordOnBehalfOf(req, id, `version:${action}`, versionObo);
2390:       // KS-1557: the row above records the version ACTION on the PARENT (`id`); this one records
2391:       // that this connector CREATED the document just minted (`newExternalId`). A version-minted
2392:       // document had no creation row of its own, so `wasCreatedByConnector` was false for the very
2393:       // key that made it, and a Platform S certify attached to the current version answered 403
2394:       // (Stuart, KS-1557 comment `16e8498d`). Attribution stays with the minter: this cannot admit
2395:       // a caller who did not create the document.
2396:       recordConnectorProvenance(req, newExternalId, CONNECTOR_CREATE_ACTION);
2397: 
2398:       logger.info('Document versioned', {
2399:         sourceId: id,
2400:         newId: newExternalId,
2401:         action,
2402:         tenantId,
2403:         userId: user.id,
2404:       });
2405: 
2406:       // EventTypes doesn't yet have a DOCUMENT_VERSIONED const — pass
2407:       // the literal string. When events.ts grows the const, swap to
2408:       // EventTypes.DOCUMENT_VERSIONED.
2409:       publishEvent('document.versioned' as never, {
2410:         sourceId: id,
2411:         newId: newExternalId,
2412:         action,
2413:         contentHash: newContentHash,
2414:         userId: user.id,
2415:       }).catch(() => {});
2416: 
2417:       return res.status(201).json({
2418:         document: {
2419:           id: newExternalId,
2420:           parentDocumentId: id,
2421:           type: source.type,
2422:           status: 'draft',
2423:           contentHash: newContentHash,
2424:           action,
2425:           title: derivedTitle,
2426:           versionedAt: now,
2427:           // Anchoring intentionally not auto-triggered — caller hits
2428:           // POST /api/documents/{newId}/anchor when ready.
2429:           anchored: false,
2430:         },
2431:       });
2432:     } catch (error) {
2433:       logger.error('Failed to version document', { error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error) });
2434:       return res.status(500).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'Failed to create new document version' } });
2435:     }
2436:   },
2437: );
2438: 
2439: /**
2440:  * POST /api/documents/:id/share — KS-67
2441:  *
2442:  * Share an uploaded document (with or without a prior certification) to
2443:  * one or more recipients. Writes to the polymorphic `shares` table from
2444:  * migration 022; the existing `/api/certifications/:id/share` route is
2445:  * unchanged and still writes to the legacy `share_records` table (a
2446:  * follow-up ticket consolidates the two).
2447:  *
2448:  * Body: `{recipients: [{email | userId, name?, shareType: 'view'|'verify'|'reshare'}], message?, expiresAt?}`
2449:  *
2450:  * Per recipient an `email` OR a `userId` is required. The `userId` form
2451:  * is used by SSD after a `/api/users/lookup` hit (KS-69); the `email`
2452:  * form covers the unregistered-recipient case (the share row carries
2453:  * the email and is claimed if the user signs up later, per KS-74).
2454:  *
2455:  * RBAC: ISSUER_ADMIN / ORG_ADMIN / SYSTEM_ADMIN / SUPER_ADMIN role OR
2456:  * `documents:share` scope (KS-71 helper).
2457:  */
2458: const ALLOWED_DOCUMENT_SHARE_ROLES = DOCUMENT_WRITE_ROLES;
2459: const ALLOWED_SHARE_TYPES: ShareType[] = ['view', 'verify', 'reshare'];
2460: // KS-536: recipients[].userId is used in a `::uuid` cast downstream, so it must
2461: // be format-checked at the boundary (mirrors the transfer-custody guard).
2462: const SHARE_RECIPIENT_UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i;
2463: 
2464: documentsRouter.post(
2465:   '/:id/share',
2466:   [
2467:     param('id').notEmpty().withMessage('Document ID is required'),
2468:     body('recipients').isArray({ min: 1 }).withMessage('At least one recipient is required'),
2469:   ],
2470:   async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
2471:     try {
2472:       const user = getUserFromRequest(req);
2473:       if (!user) {
2474:         return res.status(401).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'UNAUTHORIZED', message: 'Authentication required' } });
2475:       }
2476: 
2477:       if (!isAllowedByRoleOrScope(req, ALLOWED_DOCUMENT_SHARE_ROLES, 'documents:share')) {
2478:         return res.status(403).json({
2479:           success: false,
2480:           error: { code: 'FORBIDDEN', message: 'Your role / scope does not permit sharing this document' },
2481:         });
2482:       }
2483: 
2484:       const errors = validationResult(req);
2485:       if (!errors.isEmpty()) {
2486:         return res.status(400).json({
2487:           success: false,
2488:           error: { code: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'Invalid request body', details: errors.array() },
2489:         });
2490:       }
2491: 
2492:       const { id } = req.params;
2493:       // KS-480 §6: connector attribution on the share action. KS-1228: validated and resolved here
2494:       // (the resolved person is the sharer below), and recorded only once every share row is
2495:       // written, because createShare itself can still refuse (404, and the SQLSTATE 400s).
2496:       const shareObo = await checkOnBehalfOf(req, res);
2497:       if (!shareObo.ok) return;
2498:       const { recipients, message, expiresAt } = req.body as {
2499:         recipients: Array<{ email?: string; userId?: string; shareType?: ShareType; name?: string }>;
2500:         message?: string;
2501:         expiresAt?: string;
2502:       };
2503: 
2504:       const tenantId = getReqTenantId(req);
2505: 
2506:       // Confirm the document exists in this tenant before we write any
2507:       // share rows. Otherwise the shareRepo.createShare subquery would
2508:       // silently fail with SHARE_TARGET_NOT_FOUND on the FIRST recipient
2509:       // and we'd respond mid-flight.
2510:       const doc = await getDocument(id, tenantId, (req as any).db);
2511:       if (!doc) {
2512:         return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Document not found in this tenant' } });
2513:       }
2514: 
2515:       // Validate recipient shape up-front so we don't half-write.
2516:       for (const r of recipients) {
2517:         // KS-593: a null (or any non-object) recipient threw a TypeError on `r.email` here - a raw 500.
2518:         if (!r || typeof r !== 'object' || (!r.email && !r.userId)) {
2519:           return res.status(400).json({
2520:             success: false,
2521:             error: { code: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'Each recipient must have either an email or a userId' },
2522:           });
2523:         }
2524:         // KS-536 sweep: a non-UUID `userId` reached a `::uuid` cast in the share
2525:         // repo and threw Postgres 22P02 ("invalid input syntax for type uuid"),
2526:         // surfacing as a raw 500 — the not_a_server_error class, and the same
2527:         // boundary gap KS-497 closed for tenant ids.
2528:         // ⚠ KS-697 correction: this comment used to end "/transfer-custody
2529:         // already format-checks its holder id before touching the DB; /share
2530:         // did not." That was false — /transfer-custody had NO such guard, and
2531:         // 500'd on a non-uuid holder for as long as this comment claimed
2532:         // otherwise. It does now. Left as a correction rather than a silent
2533:         // deletion, because the wrong half is why nobody went and looked.
2534:         if (r.userId !== undefined && !SHARE_RECIPIENT_UUID_RE.test(String(r.userId))) {
2535:           return res.status(400).json({
2536:             success: false,
2537:             error: { code: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'recipients[].userId must be a valid UUID' },
2538:           });
2539:         }
2540:         const st = (r.shareType ?? 'view') as ShareType;
2541:         if (!ALLOWED_SHARE_TYPES.includes(st)) {
2542:           return res.status(400).json({
2543:             success: false,
2544:             error: { code: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: `Invalid shareType '${st}'. Allowed: ${ALLOWED_SHARE_TYPES.join(', ')}` },
2545:           });
2546:         }
2547:       }
2548: 
2549:       // KS-564: `shares.shared_by_id` is a UUID column, but a connector
2550:       // principal is `connector:<platform>:<id>` — writing it hit the ::uuid
2551:       // cast and 22P02'd into a raw 500 on every Platform S share. Prefer the
2552:       // onBehalfOf-resolved human; otherwise store NULL (same treatment
2553:       // POST /api/documents has always given owner_user_id). A provenance row
2554:       // carries the connector only when onBehalfOf was supplied — see
2555:       // utils/principalId and BACKLOG.md for the bare-connector gap.
2556:       const sharedByUuid = toActorUuid(user.id, shareObo.resolvedUserId);
2557: 
2558:       const created: Array<{ id: string; recipientEmail?: string | null; recipientUserId?: string | null; shareType: ShareType }> = [];
2559:       // KS-1263: every recipient's row is written in ONE transaction, so a refusal at recipient
2560:       // k > 1 rolls the earlier rows back rather than leaving them behind with no
2561:       // action_provenance row (which is what develop did after KS-1228).
2562:       // KS-1263: withTenant() is used UNCONDITIONALLY here, never `(req as any).db`. On a
2563:       // multi-tenant deployment (index.ts:225, gated on MULTI_TENANCY_ENABLED, which
2564:       // deployment/azure/services.bicep:798 sets to 'true') that reference is
2565:       // createPoolProxy(pool), which wraps BEGIN + set_config + COMMIT around EACH statement
2566:       // (db.ts:24-26) — two transactions again, which is the defect this ticket removes.
2567:       // withTenant checks out ONE client, runs BEGIN + both set_config GUCs + the callback on
2568:       // that client, then COMMIT, or ROLLBACK on a throw (db.ts:302-336); handed that open
2569:       // client, createPoolProxy queries it directly rather than re-bundling (db.ts:24-26,
2570:       // pinned by ks458-db-tenant-guc.test.ts:111).
2571:       const { withTenant } = await import('../db');
2572:       try {
2573:         await withTenant(tenantId, async (tx) => {
2574:           for (const r of recipients) {
2575:             const share = await createShare(
2576:               {
2577:                 tenantId,
2578:                 targetType: 'document',
2579:                 targetId: id,
2580:                 recipientUserId: r.userId ?? null,
2581:                 recipientEmail: r.email ?? null,
2582:                 shareType: (r.shareType ?? 'view') as ShareType,
2583:                 sharedById: sharedByUuid,
2584:                 expiresAt: expiresAt ?? null,
2585:                 message: message ?? null,
2586:               },
2587:               tx,
2588:             );
2589:             created.push({
2590:               id: share.id,
2591:               recipientEmail: share.recipientEmail,
2592:               recipientUserId: share.recipientUserId,
2593:               shareType: share.shareType,
2594:             });
2595:           }
2596:         });
2597:       } catch (err: any) {
2598:         // The rollback has already happened; `created` describes rows that no longer exist, and
2599:         // nothing below this point reads it on an error path.
2600:         if (err?.code === 'SHARE_TARGET_NOT_FOUND') {
2601:           return res.status(404).json({
2602:             success: false,
2603:             error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Document not found in this tenant (resolution failed at share time)' },
2604:           });
2605:         }
2606:         throw err;
2607:       }
2608:       recordOnBehalfOf(req, id, 'share', shareObo);
2609: 
2610:       // KS-319: emit the on-chain anchor for the share event + return a
2611:       // resolvable anchor id (idempotent — reuses the document's existing anchor).
2612:       const shareAnchor = await emitLifecycleAnchor({
2613:         documentId: id,
2614:         contentHash: doc.contentHash,
2615:         authHeader: req.headers.authorization as string,
2616:         eventType: 'share',
2617:         identity: (req.body as any)?.identity,
2618:       });
2619: 
2620:       return res.status(201).json({
2621:         documentId: id,
2622:         sharesCreated: created.length,
2623:         shares: created,
2624:         anchorId: shareAnchor.anchorId,
2625:         anchor: { id: shareAnchor.anchorId, txHash: shareAnchor.txHash, status: shareAnchor.status },
2626:       });
2627:     } catch (error) {
2628:       // KS-564 (Stuart's ask 2): a bad-input constraint failure is the
2629:       // caller's to fix — answering INTERNAL_ERROR made S retry it forever on
2630:       // every refresher tick. Map the storable-input SQLSTATEs to a 400 so S
2631:       // can tell "will never work" from "retry later".
2632:       const pgCode = extractPgCode(error);
2633:       if (pgCode && ['22P02', '22001', '22007', '22008', '23502', '23503', '23514', '42804'].includes(pgCode)) {
2634:         logger.warn('Share rejected on unstorable input', { pgCode });
2635:         return res.status(400).json({
2636:           success: false,
2637:           error: { code: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'Share could not be stored: one or more fields are not storable (check recipients[].userId and expiresAt)' },
2638:         });
2639:       }
2640:       logger.error('Failed to share document', { error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error) });
2641:       return res.status(500).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'Failed to share document' } });
2642:     }
2643:   },
2644: );
2645: 
2646: /**
2647:  * POST /api/documents/:id/revoke
2648:  * Revoke a document
2649:  */
2650: documentsRouter.post(
2651:   '/:id/revoke',
2652:   [param('id').notEmpty().withMessage('Document ID is required')],
2653:   async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
2654:     try {
2655:       const user = getUserFromRequest(req);
2656:       if (!user) {
2657:         return res.status(401).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'UNAUTHORIZED', message: 'Authentication required' } });
2658:       }
2659: 
2660:       const { id } = req.params;
2661:       const { reason } = req.body;
2662:       const tenantId = getReqTenantId(req);
2663: 
2664:       let document: any;
2665:       try {
2666:         document = await getDocument(id, tenantId, (req as any).db);
2667:       } catch (dbErr: any) {
2668:         if (dbErr?.message?.includes('does not exist')) {
2669:           return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Document not found' } });
2670:         }
2671:         throw dbErr;
2672:       }
2673:       if (!document) {
2674:         return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Document not found' } });
2675:       }
2676: 
2677:       if (document.owner?.id !== user.id) {
2678:         return res.status(403).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'FORBIDDEN', message: 'Only the owner can revoke this document' } });
2679:       }
2680: 
2681:       if (document.status === 'revoked') {
2682:         return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'Document is already revoked' } });
2683:       }
2684: 
2685:       // KS-566 (G-1 ruling, KS-539): revoke is on the onBehalfOf pattern — share /
2686:       // revoke / transfer-custody attribute to the real person behind a connector call.
2687:       //
2688:       // PLACEMENT (#742 review): the CHECK is deliberately AFTER the 404, the ownership
2689:       // 403 and the already-revoked 400, and NOT at the top of the handler where /share
2690:       // and /transfer-custody put it. `recordOnBehalfOf` writes an action_provenance row
2691:       // carrying email_enc, display_name_enc and email_hash. At the top of the handler a
2692:       // connector could POST /api/documents/<any-string>/revoke and persist that PII
2693:       // against a document id that need not exist or belong to its tenant —
2694:       // migrations/041_action_provenance.sql:33-36 makes document_id deliberately NOT a
2695:       // foreign key and no later migration adds one, so nothing downstream rejects it.
2696:       // KS-1264 then moved the WRITE below updateDocument, so a row exists only for a
2697:       // revoke that actually happened — not merely for one that was going to succeed.
2698:       // /share and /transfer-custody kept the top-of-handler shape until KS-1228, which
2699:       // moved their row (and /version's) after the action; see checkOnBehalfOf.
2700:       const revokeObo = await checkOnBehalfOf(req, res);
2701:       if (!revokeObo.ok) return;
2702:       const now = new Date().toISOString();
2703:       // KS-1278: the already-revoked decision is made IN the UPDATE, not only on the read above, so of two
2704:       // overlapping revokes exactly one changes the row; the other answers the same 400 and records nothing.
2705:       const revoked = await updateDocument(id, tenantId, { status: 'revoked' }, undefined, { ifStatusIsNot: 'revoked' });
2706:       if (revoked === null) {
2707:         return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'Document is already revoked' } });
2708:       }
2709: 
2710:       // KS-1264: the row is recorded only now, after updateDocument, so a revoke that throws writes none.
2711:       recordOnBehalfOf(req, id, 'revoke', revokeObo);
2712:       // KS-319: emit the on-chain anchor for the revoke event + return a
2713:       // resolvable anchor id (idempotent — reuses the document's existing anchor).
2714:       // K `revoke` = global invalidation of the document (status:'revoked'); S's
2715:       // owner-local soft-delete routes here per PS-124 (semantics noted) — the
2716:       // anchor records the revoke against the existing document, not a derived one.
2717:       const revokeAnchor = await emitLifecycleAnchor({
2718:         documentId: id,
2719:         contentHash: document.contentHash,
2720:         authHeader: req.headers.authorization as string,
2721:         eventType: 'revoke',
2722:         identity: (req.body as any)?.identity,
2723:       });
2724: 
2725:       res.json({
2726:         id: document.id,
2727:         status: 'revoked',
2728:         revokedAt: now,
2729:         reason,
2730:         anchorId: revokeAnchor.anchorId,
2731:         anchor: { id: revokeAnchor.anchorId, txHash: revokeAnchor.txHash, status: revokeAnchor.status },
2732:       });
2733:     } catch (error) {
2734:       logger.error('Failed to revoke document', { error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error) });
2735:       res.status(500).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'Failed to revoke document' } });
2736:     }
2737:   },
2738: );
2739: 
2740: // =============================================================================
2741: // POST /api/documents/:id/lifecycle-events — KS-387 generic non-mutating verbs
2742: // =============================================================================
2743: //
2744: // The KS-134 verbs with NO dedicated endpoint — KS-1275: that set is
2745: // LIFECYCLE_EVENT_ACTIONS in services/originate/src/lifecycleActions.ts and the
2746: // vocabulary contract is docs/VOCABULARY.md. An inline list stood here and had
2747: // drifted from both; it is replaced by these pointers rather than
2748: // re-synchronised, because a hand-maintained copy of an enum is what drifted.
2749: // These verbs
2750: // previously anchored via the flat POST /api/anchors fallback and carried no
2751: // lineage. This one extensible route (generic over per-verb — KS-274) records
2752: // each as a NON-MUTATING lifecycle row (bytes unchanged → attaches to the
2753: // CURRENT document via parent_document_id, no version fork) and emits a
2754: // resolvable anchor via KS-319's shared emitLifecycleAnchor, exactly like
2755: // /share, /transfer-custody and /revoke do — so Platform-S gets a top-level
2756: // anchorId its CardanoAnchorRefresher can upgrade to a Cardano tx.
2757: documentsRouter.post(
2758:   '/:id/lifecycle-events',
2759:   [param('id').notEmpty().withMessage('Document ID is required')],
2760:   async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
2761:     try {
2762:       const user = getUserFromRequest(req);
2763:       if (!user) {
2764:         return res.status(401).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'UNAUTHORIZED', message: 'Authentication required' } });
2765:       }
2766: 
2767:       const { id } = req.params;
2768:       const { action, payload } = (req.body ?? {}) as { action?: unknown; payload?: unknown };
2769:       // KS-566 (G-1 ruling, KS-539): lifecycle is on the CONNECTOR pattern, so
2770:       // `onBehalfOf` no longer applies here. A sent field is IGNORED, not 400'd
2771:       // — Platform S is live sending it (PS-616, UAT since 2026-08-21T10:07Z)
2772:       // and a rejection would turn this deploy into an S outage. Tightening to
2773:       // a 400 stays available under its own ticket once S stops sending it.
2774:       // The reserved key still never rides in payload.
2775:       if (payload && typeof payload === 'object') {
2776:         delete (payload as Record<string, unknown>).onBehalfOf;
2777:       }
2778: 
2779:       // Strict vocabulary enforcement (KS-387 acceptance): unknown verbs 400 with
2780:       // the accepted list, mirroring the flat-anchor enum (KS-388). Verbs with a
2781:       // dedicated endpoint are rejected here too — S routes those to their own
2782:       // endpoints; accepting them twice would double-record lifecycle rows.
2783:       if (typeof action !== 'string' || !LIFECYCLE_EVENT_ACTIONS.includes(action as LifecycleEventAction)) {
2784:         return res.status(400).json({
2785:           success: false,
2786:           error: {
2787:             code: 'VALIDATION_ERROR',
2788:             message: `Invalid lifecycle action '${String(action ?? '')}'. Accepted: ${LIFECYCLE_EVENT_ACTIONS.join(', ')}`,
2789:           },
2790:         });
2791:       }
2792:       if (payload !== undefined && (payload === null || typeof payload !== 'object' || Array.isArray(payload))) {
2793:         return res.status(400).json({
2794:           success: false,
2795:           error: { code: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'payload, when supplied, must be a JSON object' },
2796:         });
2797:       }
2798: 
2799:       const tenantId = getReqTenantId(req);
2800:       const doc = await getDocument(id, tenantId, (req as any).db);
2801:       if (!doc) {
2802:         return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Document not found in this tenant' } });
2803:       }
2804: 
2805:       // Record the event FIRST — the domain fact is the row; anchoring is
2806:       // best-effort enrichment (an anchoring outage must not lose the event).
2807:       let event;
2808:       try {
2809:         event = await createLifecycleEvent(
2810:           {
2811:             tenantId,
2812:             documentId: id,
2813:             action,
2814:             payload: (payload ?? {}) as Record<string, unknown>,
2815:             // KS-564: same ::uuid trap as /share — `actor_user_id` is a UUID
2816:             // column and a connector principal is not a UUID. KS-566: there is
2817:             // no onBehalfOf-resolved user on this route any more, so a
2818:             // connector write stores NULL here and its attribution lives on the
2819:             // connector-only action_provenance row recorded below.
2820:             actorUserId: toActorUuid(user.id, null),
2821:           },
2822:           (req as any).db,
2823:         );
2824:       } catch (err: any) {
2825:         if (err?.code === 'LIFECYCLE_TARGET_NOT_FOUND') {
2826:           return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Document not found in this tenant (resolution failed at event time)' } });
2827:         }
2828:         throw err;
2829:       }
2830: 
2831:       // KS-566: one connector-only provenance row per RECORDED event. Placed
2832:       // after the vocabulary gate and after the event row exists, so a 400'd
2833:       // verb leaves no provenance behind and the count stays 1:1 with the
2834:       // events it describes — which is what the pre-KS-566 comment here always
2835:       // claimed ("recorded with the verb once it validates below") but the code
2836:       // did not do: it recorded before validation, with an `unknown` fallback.
2837:       recordConnectorProvenance(req, id, `lifecycle:${action}`);
2838: 
2839:       // KS-319 shared emitter: idempotent per (document, network) — the event
2840:       // anchors against the document's existing anchor, returning a resolvable
2841:       // anchor id (GET /api/anchors/{id}); null when anchoring is down (S falls
2842:       // back to POST /api/anchors, its existing HTTP-failure path).
2843:       const eventAnchor = await emitLifecycleAnchor({
2844:         documentId: id,
2845:         contentHash: doc.contentHash,
2846:         authHeader: req.headers.authorization as string,
2847:         eventType: action as LifecycleEventAction,
2848:         identity: (req.body as any)?.identity,
2849:       });
2850:       if (eventAnchor.anchorId) {
2851:         await setLifecycleEventAnchor(event.id, eventAnchor.anchorId, (req as any).db);
2852:       }
2853: 
2854:       return res.status(201).json({
2855:         documentId: id,
2856:         eventId: event.id,
2857:         action,
2858:         recordedAt: event.createdAt,
2859:         anchorId: eventAnchor.anchorId,
2860:         anchor: { id: eventAnchor.anchorId, txHash: eventAnchor.txHash, status: eventAnchor.status },
2861:       });
2862:     } catch (error) {
2863:       // KS-564 (Stuart's ask 2): same mapping as /share — an unstorable field
2864:       // is a 400 the caller can act on, not an opaque retryable 500.
2865:       const pgCode = extractPgCode(error);
2866:       if (pgCode && ['22P02', '22001', '22007', '22008', '23502', '23503', '23514', '42804'].includes(pgCode)) {
2867:         logger.warn('Lifecycle event rejected on unstorable input', { pgCode });
2868:         return res.status(400).json({
2869:           success: false,
2870:           error: { code: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'Lifecycle event could not be stored: one or more payload fields are not storable' },
2871:         });
2872:       }
2873:       logger.error('Failed to record lifecycle event', { error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error) });
2874:       return res.status(500).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'Failed to record lifecycle event' } });
2875:     }
2876:   },
2877: );
2878: 
2879: /**
2880:  * GET /api/documents/:id/lifecycle-events
2881:  * List a document's recorded lifecycle events (tenant-scoped, newest first) —
2882:  * the read side of KS-387, so S (and the portals) can confirm lineage without
2883:  * walking anchors.
2884:  */
2885: documentsRouter.get(
2886:   '/:id/lifecycle-events',
2887:   [param('id').notEmpty().withMessage('Document ID is required')],
2888:   async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
2889:     try {
2890:       const user = getUserFromRequest(req);
2891:       if (!user) {
2892:         return res.status(401).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'UNAUTHORIZED', message: 'Authentication required' } });
2893:       }
2894:       const { id } = req.params;
2895:       const tenantId = getReqTenantId(req);
2896:       const doc = await getDocument(id, tenantId, (req as any).db);
2897:       if (!doc) {
2898:         return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Document not found in this tenant' } });
2899:       }
2900:       const events = await listLifecycleEvents(id, tenantId, (req as any).db);
2901:       return res.json({ documentId: id, count: events.length, events });
2902:     } catch (error) {
2903:       logger.error('Failed to list lifecycle events', { error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error) });
2904:       return res.status(500).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'Failed to list lifecycle events' } });
2905:     }
2906:   },
2907: );

2909: // =============================================================================
2910: // POST /api/documents/:id/sign-cert — KS-83 PR 3
2911: // =============================================================================
2912: //
2913: // Active X.509 signing path. The source document's bytes stay untouched
2914: // (KS-21 Q3 v1: out-of-band JSON envelope, not embedded). The endpoint:
2915: //
2916: //   1. Looks up the source doc in the caller's tenant.
2917: //   2. Forwards a sign request to auth-service's POST /api/issuer-certs/sign
2918: //      with the source's contentHash. Caller's Bearer token rides along so
2919: //      auth applies the same RBAC; auth picks the tenant's active cert.
2920: //   3. Mints a new versioned document row (parent_document_id = source)
2921: //      carrying the signature envelope in its `data` blob. The new row's
2922: //      contentHash equals the source's — the bytes didn't change, the
2923: //      signature lives out-of-band in the data blob (and is served via
2924: //      GET /api/documents/{id}/sig-json).
2925: //   4. Returns the new doc shape + the envelope inline (so callers don't
2926: //      need a second round-trip for the sidecar bytes).
2927: //
2928: // Anchoring is intentionally NOT auto-triggered (consistent with KS-86's
2929: // /version path — caller hits POST /api/documents/{newId}/anchor when ready).
2930: //
2931: // Why a dedicated endpoint and not just /version action='sign-cert' with
2932: // the caller supplying a hash they already signed somewhere else: this
2933: // path is the one where Platform K actively uses the stored cert to sign.
2934: // The /version endpoint is for "the issuer did something externally, please
2935: // register it" (watermark, future PAdES). Different concern; cleaner API.
2936: documentsRouter.post(
2937:   '/:id/sign-cert',
2938:   [
2939:     param('id').notEmpty().withMessage('Document ID is required'),
2940:     body('metadata').optional().isObject().withMessage('metadata must be an object'),
2941:     body('title').optional().isString(),
2942:     // Optional cert id override — when omitted, auth picks the tenant's
2943:     // most-recently-uploaded active cert (KS-83 PR 3 v1 selection rule).
2944:     body('certId').optional().isString().withMessage('certId must be a string'),
2945:   ],
2946:   async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
2947:     try {
2948:       const user = getUserFromRequest(req);
2949:       if (!user) {
2950:         return res.status(401).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'UNAUTHORIZED', message: 'Authentication required' } });
2951:       }
2952: 
2953:       if (!isAllowedByRoleOrScope(req, ALLOWED_VERSION_ROLES, 'documents:write')) {
2954:         return res.status(403).json({
2955:           success: false,
2956:           error: { code: 'FORBIDDEN', message: 'Your role / scope does not permit signing this document' },
2957:         });
2958:       }
2959: 
2960:       const errors = validationResult(req);
2961:       if (!errors.isEmpty()) {
2962:         return res.status(400).json({
2963:           success: false,
2964:           error: { code: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'Invalid request body', details: errors.array() },
2965:         });
2966:       }
2967: 
2968:       const { id } = req.params;
2969:       const metadata = (req.body?.metadata && typeof req.body.metadata === 'object')
2970:         ? (req.body.metadata as Record<string, unknown>)
2971:         : {};
2972:       const overrideTitle = typeof req.body?.title === 'string' ? req.body.title.trim() : '';
2973:       const certIdOverride = typeof req.body?.certId === 'string' ? req.body.certId : undefined;
2974: 
2975:       // 1. Resolve source doc + tenant scope.
2976:       const tenantId = getReqTenantId(req);
2977:       const source = await getDocument(id, tenantId, (req as any).db);
2978:       if (!source) {
2979:         return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Document not found in this tenant' } });
2980:       }
2981:       // KS-1213: stored as `source.type`, served as `data.documentType || type`; same refusal as /version.
2982:       if (metadata.documentType !== undefined && metadata.documentType !== source.type) {
2983:         return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'metadata.documentType must equal the source document type' } });
2984:       }
2985: 
2986:       // 2. Call auth-service DIRECTLY (AUTH_SERVICE_URL) to sign
2987:       // source.contentHash. KS-289: same fix as the holder-lookup paths —
2988:       // routing this POST through the gateway both hit the gateway's CSRF
2989:       // gate (no browser token on a service call) and fell back to an
2990:       // unreachable http://localhost:6882 inside the container. auth's
2991:       // /issuer-certs/sign self-authenticates from the forwarded JWT.
2992:       const authHeader = (req.headers.authorization as string) || '';
2993:       const authBase = process.env.AUTH_SERVICE_URL || 'http://localhost:6003';
2994: 
2995:       let envelope: {
2996:         signature: string;
2997:         signatureAlgorithm: string;
2998:         signedContentHash: string;
2999:         signedAt: string;
3000:         cert: {
3001:           id: string;
3002:           fingerprintSha256: string;
3003:           subject: string;
3004:           issuer: string;
3005:           certPem: string;
3006:           validFrom: string;
3007:           validUntil: string;
3008:           trustLevel: string;
3009:         };
3010:       };
3011:       try {
3012:         const signRes = await fetch(`${authBase}/api/issuer-certs/sign`, {
3013:           method: 'POST',
3014:           headers: {
3015:             'Content-Type': 'application/json',
3016:             ...(authHeader ? { Authorization: authHeader } : {}),
3017:           },
3018:           body: JSON.stringify({
3019:             contentHash: source.contentHash,
3020:             ...(certIdOverride ? { certId: certIdOverride } : {}),
3021:           }),
3022:         });
3023:         if (signRes.status === 404) {
3024:           // NO_ACTIVE_CERT — no cert uploaded for this tenant.
3025:           const body = await signRes.json().catch(() => ({})) as { error?: { code?: string; message?: string } };
3026:           return res.status(409).json({
3027:             success: false,
3028:             error: {
3029:               code: body?.error?.code || 'NO_ACTIVE_CERT',
3030:               message: body?.error?.message || 'No active issuer cert for this tenant. Upload one via POST /api/issuer-certs first.',
3031:             },
3032:           });
3033:         }
3034:         if (signRes.status === 403) {
3035:           // Caller has documents:write but not issuer-certs:sign. Surface clearly.
3036:           return res.status(403).json({
3037:             success: false,
3038:             error: {
3039:               code: 'CERT_SIGN_FORBIDDEN',
3040:               message: 'Caller has documents:write but is not authorised to use issuer-certs:sign. Request the scope from your admin.',
3041:             },
3042:           });
3043:         }
3044:         if (!signRes.ok) {
3045:           const body = await signRes.json().catch(() => ({})) as { error?: { code?: string; message?: string } };
3046:           // Propagate the upstream error code/status so e.g. 422 KEY_MISMATCH
3047:           // surfaces accurately to the caller instead of being flattened to 502.
3048:           if (signRes.status >= 400 && signRes.status < 500) {
3049:             return res.status(signRes.status).json({
3050:               success: false,
3051:               error: {
3052:                 code: body?.error?.code || 'CERT_SIGN_ERROR',
3053:                 message: body?.error?.message || `Cert sign failed with status ${signRes.status}`,
3054:               },
3055:             });
3056:           }
3057:           return res.status(502).json({
3058:             success: false,
3059:             error: { code: 'BAD_GATEWAY', message: `Cert sign upstream returned ${signRes.status}` },
3060:           });
3061:         }
3062:         envelope = await signRes.json() as typeof envelope & { success: true };
3063:       } catch (err: any) {
3064:         logger.warn('issuer-certs/sign unreachable during /sign-cert', { error: err?.message });
3065:         return res.status(502).json({
3066:           success: false,
3067:           error: { code: 'BAD_GATEWAY', message: 'Cert sign service unavailable' },
3068:         });
3069:       }
3070: 
3071:       // 3. Mint the new versioned document row. contentHash matches the
3072:       //    source (bytes unchanged); the signature lives in data blob.
3073:       const newExternalId = `doc-${Date.now()}-${uuidv4().slice(0, 8)}`;
3074:       try {
3075:         await assertNoCycle(newExternalId, id, tenantId, { db: (req as any).db });
3076:       } catch (cycleErr: any) {
3077:         if (cycleErr?.code === 'LINEAGE_CYCLE') {
3078:           return res.status(422).json({
3079:             success: false,
3080:             error: {
3081:               code: 'LINEAGE_CYCLE',
3082:               message: `Refusing to sign: source ${id} would form a cycle in the lineage chain (corrupted parent chain?)`,
3083:             },
3084:           });
3085:         }
3086:         throw cycleErr;
3087:       }
3088: 
3089:       const now = new Date().toISOString();
3090:       const sourceTitle = (source.data?.title as string) || source.type || 'Untitled';
3091:       const derivedTitle = overrideTitle || `${sourceTitle} (signed)`;
3092: 
3093:       const sigJsonEnvelope = {
3094:         version: 1,
3095:         documentId: newExternalId,
3096:         parentDocumentId: id,
3097:         signedContentHash: envelope.signedContentHash,
3098:         signature: envelope.signature,
3099:         signatureAlgorithm: envelope.signatureAlgorithm,
3100:         certFingerprintSha256: envelope.cert.fingerprintSha256,
3101:         certPem: envelope.cert.certPem,
3102:         subject: envelope.cert.subject,
3103:         issuer: envelope.cert.issuer,
3104:         trustLevel: envelope.cert.trustLevel,
3105:         signedAt: envelope.signedAt,
3106:         signedById: user.id,
3107:       };
3108: 
3109:       const derivedDoc: DocumentRecord = {
3110:         id: newExternalId,
3111:         type: source.type,
3112:         status: 'signed',
3113:         owner: source.owner,
3114:         rightsHolderId: source.rightsHolderId,
3115:         parentDocumentId: id,
3116:         data: {
3117:           ...source.data,
3118:           title: derivedTitle,
3119:           ...metadata,
3120:           // KS-1508: the server-owned keys come AFTER the caller's metadata (see /version).
3121:           versionAction: 'sign-cert',
3122:           versionedAt: now,
3123:           versionedById: user.id,
3124:           parentDocumentId: id,
3125:           // Signature envelope — stored alongside the versioned doc so
3126:           // GET /sig-json can serve it without a second auth round trip.
3127:           sigJson: sigJsonEnvelope,
3128:         },
3129:         contentHash: source.contentHash, // unchanged — OOB envelope per Q3
3130:         signatures: [],
3131:         blockchain: undefined,
3132:         createdAt: now,
3133:         updatedAt: now,
3134:       };
3135: 
3136:       await saveDocument(derivedDoc, tenantId, (req as any).db);
3137: 
3138:       logger.info('Document signed via issuer cert', {
3139:         sourceId: id,
3140:         newId: newExternalId,
3141:         certId: envelope.cert.id,
3142:         certFingerprint: envelope.cert.fingerprintSha256.slice(0, 16),
3143:         algorithm: envelope.signatureAlgorithm,
3144:         tenantId,
3145:         userId: user.id,
3146:       });
3147: 
3148:       publishEvent('document.versioned' as never, {
3149:         sourceId: id,
3150:         newId: newExternalId,
3151:         action: 'sign-cert',
3152:         contentHash: source.contentHash,
3153:         certId: envelope.cert.id,
3154:         userId: user.id,
3155:       }).catch(() => {});
3156: 
3157:       return res.status(201).json({
3158:         document: {
3159:           id: newExternalId,
3160:           parentDocumentId: id,
3161:           type: source.type,
3162:           status: 'signed',
3163:           contentHash: source.contentHash,
3164:           action: 'sign-cert',
3165:           title: derivedTitle,
3166:           versionedAt: now,
3167:           anchored: false,
3168:         },
3169:         sigJson: sigJsonEnvelope,
3170:         // Convenience URL — callers can also fetch via GET /sig-json later.
3171:         sigJsonUrl: `/api/documents/${newExternalId}/sig-json`,
3172:       });
3173:     } catch (error) {
3174:       logger.error('Failed to sign document via issuer cert', {
3175:         error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error),
3176:       });
3177:       return res.status(500).json({
3178:         success: false,
3179:         error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'Failed to sign document' },
3180:       });
3181:     }
3182:   },
3183: );
